# Uncertain rendering decisions: vi

Generated by `python3 scripts/review_checklist.py vi --write`; do not edit by hand. 964 decisions in 60 books, every vi rendering decision the drafters marked uncertain. Tick an item once a reviewer has settled it, and regenerate the list after the content changes.

## Sáng Thế Ký (52)

- [ ] [GEN 1:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/1/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · God says "chúng ta" (spanned mid-sentence; "Chúng ta" at the start) — God speaks in the plural (m1). Vietnamese "chúng ta" (we, including the hearer) is the form a speaker uses calling others to act with him, as God does here; "chúng tôi" would shut the hearers out. It takes the deity_pronoun setting like God's "Ta".
- [ ] [GEN 2:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/2/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "con người" (L, B), "người đàn ông" (R) for ha-adam in chapters 2–3 — The man is one particular person but is not yet given the name Adam; "người nam" is kept for ish in 2:23–24. Literal and Balanced say "con người" (the human), which keeps the link with all humankind; Readable says "người đàn ông", as the English Readable says "a man".
- [ ] [GEN 2:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/2/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "người giúp đỡ tương xứng" (L), "người giúp đỡ xứng hợp" (B), "người bạn đời thật hợp" (R) — The helper gives the help the other needs and is not a lesser assistant (m2), so "phụ" (assistant) is avoided. Readable says "bạn đời" (life partner), as the English Readable says "partner"; like the English choice it says more than the word, so it is marked uncertain.
- [ ] [GEN 2:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/2/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "Người Nữ" ... "Người Nam" (capitals at L, B) — The Hebrew ishah (woman) sounds like ish (man) (m2). Vietnamese "người nữ" and "người nam" share the word "người" and differ only in the last word, which keeps the likeness as far as Vietnamese can; "nàng" is used for the woman in the narrative, the word for a young wife.
- [ ] [GEN 3:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/3/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "chê-ru-bim" — The Hebrew word is kept as a name, written in the Vietnamese way; Readable adds that they are mighty angels, as the English Readable does, with the same uncertainty.
- [ ] [GEN 4:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/4/#v22) L/B/R · supplied_words · "em gái" for "sister" — Vietnamese must say whether a sister is older or younger, which the Hebrew does not. She is named after her brother, so "em gái" (younger sister) is the likelier reading; a reviewer may weigh it.
- [ ] [GEN 5:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/5/#v29) R · supplied_words · "Tên này nghe giống như chữ "nghỉ ngơi" trong tiếng Hê-bơ-rơ" (R) — The Genesis terms note asks Readable to say what an explained name sounds like. The English Readable leaves it to the meaning decision (m1) here; Vietnamese Readable says it in a short sentence so that "nghỉ ngơi" in Lamech's words can be heard against the name.
- [ ] [GEN 6:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/6/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Nê-phi-lim", with "người khổng lồ" added at R — The Hebrew name is kept, as in the English; Readable says they were giants, with the uncertainty of the English choice.
- [ ] [GEN 8:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/8/#v1) L/B · word_choice · "nhớ đến" (L, B), "không quên" (R) — To remember here is to turn and act for someone (m1); "nhớ đến" in Vietnamese also means to care for. Readable says "did not forget", as the English Readable does, with its uncertainty.
- [ ] [GEN 9:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/9/#v22) L/B/R · supplied_words · "hai anh mình" (his two older brothers) — Vietnamese must say whether a brother is older or younger. Verse 24 calls Ham the youngest son (m1 there), so Shem and Japheth are his older brothers; that reading is uncertain, and so is this choice.
- [ ] [GEN 10:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/10/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "anh của Gia-phết" — The Hebrew can be read either way, and the English reads Shem as the elder (m1); Vietnamese must choose "anh" (older brother) or "em" (younger), and follows that reading.
- [ ] [GEN 10:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/10/#v25) L/B/R · supplied_words · "em ông" for "his brother" — Vietnamese must say older or younger; Peleg is named first, so Joktan is taken as the younger. A reviewer may weigh it.
- [ ] [GEN 12:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/12/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · the wording of Acts 7:3 — Stephen quotes this command in Acts 7:3, and the Vietnamese of this project already says it there: "ra khỏi xứ sở", "rời bỏ quê hương và họ hàng", "xứ Ta sẽ chỉ cho con". Genesis keeps those words, adding "nhà cha" (your father's house), which Acts leaves out. Literal says "ngươi" where Acts …
- [ ] [GEN 13:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/13/#v8) L/B/R · register · Abram speaks to Lot as "bác" (himself) and "cháu" (Lot) — Lot was the son of Abram's brother Haran (11:27). Vietnamese must say how the two are related: "bác" is a father's older brother, and Abram is named first among Terah's sons. If Haran was the older, "chú" would be right.
- [ ] [GEN 14:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/14/#v12) L · word_choice · "con của anh Áp-ram" at Literal; "cháu của Áp-ram" at Balanced and Readable — The Hebrew says "the son of Abram's brother". Vietnamese must say older or younger brother; "anh" follows the order of Terah's sons in 11:26, where Abram comes first, so this may need "em": see 13:8.
- [ ] [GEN 16:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/16/#v10) L/B/R · setting_alternative · the angel's "Ta" under the deity_pronoun setting — The angel makes a promise that only God can keep and speaks as God himself (meaning decision on 16:7), so his "I" is God's own.
- [ ] [GEN 18:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/18/#v10) L/B/R · register · the visitor says "Ta" under the deity_pronoun setting but keeps calling Abraham "ông" — The speaker is the LORD (18:13 names him), so his "I" is God's "Ta", as in the Vietnamese Romans 9:9, which quotes this promise. Abraham still treats him as a guest, and the visitor speaks to him as a guest would, so "ông" stays through the meal scene (18:10-15).
- [ ] [GEN 19:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/19/#v21) L/B/R · register · the angel says plain "ta" to Lot, with no setting — Here the angel speaks as the LORD's messenger who has been sent (19:13), not in God's own name, so his "ta" is the plain word of one with authority.
- [ ] [GEN 20:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/20/#v3) L/B/R · register · God speaks to Abimelech as "ngươi" at every level; Abimelech prays as "con" and calls God "Chúa" and "Ngài" — The Genesis terms note lets God say "ngươi" or "con" as the relationship allows. Abimelech is a foreign king under sentence (meaning decision), not one of God's own household, so "ngươi" fits; Abraham, God's friend, is "con".
- [ ] [GEN 20:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/20/#v9) L/B/R · register · Abimelech speaks to Abraham as a king, "ta" and "ông"; Abraham answers as "tôi" — The Genesis terms note sets "tôi" and "ông" between strangers; a king speaking to a foreigner in his land keeps the "ta" of his rank, as Pharaoh does to Abram in 12:18 and the angel to Lot in 19:21.
- [ ] [GEN 22:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/22/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "Na-cô, em ông" for "Nahor your brother" — Vietnamese must say older or younger brother; Nahor is named after Abram in 11:26 and 11:27, as in 13:8 and 14:12.
- [ ] [GEN 24:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/24/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "người anh" for Rebekah's brother Laban — Vietnamese must say older or younger; Laban acts as head of the family with his father in 24:50, so "anh" is the likely relation.
- [ ] [GEN 24:58](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/24/#v58) L/B/R · register · the family speaks to Rebekah as "con" and she answers as "con" — Her mother and brother ask together (24:55); Vietnamese takes the parent's word, and Rebekah answers as a daughter would.
- [ ] [GEN 25:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/25/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" (Rebekah as Laban's younger sister); "anh" (R) for Laban — Vietnamese has no word for "sister" that leaves age open. Laban acts for the family in chapter 24 while Rebekah is still unmarried, so the draft makes him the older; a reviewer may weigh this.
- [ ] [GEN 26:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/26/#v9) L/B/R · register · Abimelech and Isaac call each other "ông" and themselves "tôi" ("chúng tôi" for the king's people) — The terms note sets "ông" and "tôi" between a host and a stranger. Abimelech is a king, but he speaks to Isaac as to a man of standing whose God he fears (verse 28), not as to a subject.
- [ ] [GEN 27:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/27/#v27) L/B/R · register · in the blessing Isaac says "ta" and "con" ("con trai ta") — The blessing is a solemn saying, not talk; Vietnamese marks the change of register with "ta", as an elder pronouncing words over someone.
- [ ] [GEN 29:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/29/#v14) L/B/R · register · Laban and Jacob call each other "cậu" (mother's brother) and "cháu" (nephew), at every level, through chapters 29-31 — Laban is the brother of Jacob's mother, and Vietnamese has a pair of words for exactly that bond; the Hebrew stresses it (verse 10, "my bone and my flesh"). They stay in use after Jacob marries Laban's daughters, until the quarrel of 31:36.
- [ ] [GEN 30:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/30/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "trái phong già" for the mandrakes — The usual Vietnamese name of the plant (Mandragora); the meaning decision explains why the women wanted it.
- [ ] [GEN 31:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/31/#v36) L/B/R · register · in his anger Jacob drops "cậu" and "cháu" and says "ông" and "tôi" to Laban (verses 36-42) — Jacob brings a formal complaint (meaning decision); in Vietnamese a nephew who stops calling his uncle "cậu" shows that he is speaking as one party against another, which is what the Hebrew shows. Laban keeps "cậu" and "cháu" in reply (verse 43).
- [ ] [GEN 32:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/32/#v27) L/B/R · register · the man says "ta" and "ngươi" with no deity_pronoun setting; Jacob says "tôi" and "ông" — The man is not named, and the English gives his pronouns no setting here. Vietnamese has him speak as a superior ("ta", "ngươi"), while Jacob, who does not yet know who he is, speaks as to a stranger; after the blessing Jacob says he has seen God (verse 31).
- [ ] [GEN 35:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/35/#v2) B/R · register · Jacob calls his household "anh em" at B and R; "các ngươi" at L — His hearers are his wives, children and servants together; "anh em" is the plain word for a mixed group a leader addresses, and Literal keeps the bare second person plural.
- [ ] [GEN 35:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/35/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây vân hương" (L) for the terebinth; "cây đại thụ" (B), "cây sồi lớn" (R) — The tree is a terebinth, a great landmark tree (meaning decision). Literal names the kind; Balanced and Readable say what a reader needs to see, a great tree, as the English does.
- [ ] [GEN 36:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/36/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" for Lotan's sister — The Hebrew does not say whether she was older or younger; Vietnamese must choose, and "em gái" is the usual default where nothing is said.
- [ ] [GEN 38:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/38/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "người nữ được hiến dâng" (L) and "cô gái mãi dâm nơi miếu thờ" (B, R) for the qedeshah — Hirah uses a different word from the narrator's "prostitute", one tied to a shrine (meaning decision); Literal keeps its root, "consecrated", and Balanced and Readable say what it meant, as the English does.
- [ ] [GEN 41:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/41/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · "thần linh của Đức Chúa Trời", lowercase — Pharaoh speaks of God's power at work in a man (meaning decision), not of the Spirit of 1:2; the Vietnamese style guide writes lowercase "thần linh" where the Holy Spirit is not meant.
- [ ] [GEN 41:43](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/41/#v43) L · word_choice · "Áp-rếch!" (L), the cry transliterated; "Hãy quỳ xuống!" (B, R) — The Egyptian word is unclear, perhaps "Bow down!" (meaning decision); Literal keeps the sound, as the English does.
- [ ] [GEN 44:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/44/#v27) L · register · "vợ ta" (L); "vợ cha" (B, R) — Jacob speaking to his sons calls himself "cha" at Balanced and Readable; Literal keeps the bare "ta" of a man speaking of his own wife, close to the Hebrew "my wife".
- [ ] [GEN 45:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/45/#v17) L/B/R · register · Pharaoh to Joseph: "ngươi" and "ta" — Master and servant, as the terms note sets: the king speaks to his highest official with "ngươi"; the royal court words "khanh" and "trẫm" would make the story sound like a Vietnamese court chronicle.
- [ ] [GEN 46:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/46/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "I-óp" for Iob — "Gióp" is how Vietnamese writes Job; "I-óp" keeps this son of Issachar apart from him, following the Hebrew spelling (meaning decision).
- [ ] [GEN 46:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/46/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-ra" for Serah; "em gái họ" for "their sister" — "Sê-ra" keeps Asher's daughter apart from Judah's son Xê-rách. Vietnamese must say older or younger sister; the Hebrew does not say, and she is named last.
- [ ] [GEN 46:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/46/#v34) L/B · register · "các đầy tớ của bệ hạ" for "your servants" before Pharaoh — A king is addressed as "bệ hạ"; the brothers will use it when they stand before Pharaoh in 47:3–4. Readable says "chúng tôi" as the English Readable says "we".
- [ ] [GEN 47:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/47/#v1) L/B/R · register · Joseph to Pharaoh: "tôi" for himself — Joseph speaks to the king respectfully but plainly with "tôi", as Pharaoh's highest official; "tâu" marks the court.
- [ ] [GEN 47:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/47/#v3) L/B/R · register · to Pharaoh: "bệ hạ" and "các đầy tớ của bệ hạ" (L, B), "chúng tôi" (R); Pharaoh says "các ngươi" — A king is addressed as "bệ hạ" in Vietnamese; the brothers keep "ngài" for Joseph. Readable says "chúng tôi" as the English Readable says "we".
- [ ] [GEN 47:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/47/#v8) L/B/R · register · Pharaoh to Jacob: "ông"; Jacob to Pharaoh: "tôi" and "bệ hạ" — The king speaks to an old man, his chief minister's father, with "ông"; Jacob, who blesses him, answers with the plain "tôi".
- [ ] [GEN 48:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/48/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ra-chên ... nàng"; "Ê-phơ-rát", "Bết-lê-hem" — Jacob speaks tenderly of his beloved wife, so "nàng" rather than the formal "bà". The place names are the Protestant forms; "Bết-lê-hem" as in the Vietnamese New Testament.
- [ ] [GEN 48:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/48/#v20) L/B/R · register · "các con" for "you" (the boys); in the blessing formula "ngươi" (L), "anh" (B, R) — Jacob speaks to his grandsons as their father now (48:5). The formula is what any Israelite will say to another, so Balanced and Readable use the ordinary "anh".
- [ ] [GEN 49:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/49/#v3) L · word_choice · "trái đầu mùa của sức lực cha" (L) — The first child of his father's strength (meaning decision); "trái đầu mùa" is the Vietnamese picture for a first yield, close to the Hebrew "first".
- [ ] [GEN 49:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/49/#v4) L/B · word_choice · "nó đã lên giường ta" (L, B), Jacob turning from Reuben to speak of him — The Hebrew turns from "you" to "he" in mid-line (meaning decision); Vietnamese keeps the turn with "nó" and "ta", the words a father uses when he speaks of a son in anger. Readable keeps "con" for clarity.
- [ ] [GEN 49:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/49/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng mà quyền ấy thuộc về" and "Người" for the one who comes — The meaning decision reads shiloh as "he to whom it belongs"; the old reading as a name, Shiloh, is in its footnote. "Đấng" and the capital in "Người" mark a figure of honor without a deity_pronoun span, since the English uses none here.
- [ ] [GEN 50:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/50/#v4) L/B/R · register · Joseph to Pharaoh's court: "các ông" and "tôi" — Joseph asks a favor of the king's officials, so he speaks as an equal asking help, not as their master.
- [ ] [GEN 50:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/50/#v15) L/B/R · register · the brothers among themselves speak of Joseph as "nó" — Older brothers speaking of a younger one among themselves; "nó" is what they called him in 37:19–20, and their fear brings back the old way of speaking.
- [ ] [GEN 50:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gen/50/#v17) L/B · word_choice · "sự vi phạm" (L), "tội ác" (B) for transgression; "tội lỗi" for sin — Two words for wrongdoing (meaning decision): a breach of what is owed, and sin. Balanced names "the servants of the God of your father" as those who worship him, since "đầy tớ" alone could sound like household servants.

## Xuất Ai Cập Ký (48)

- [ ] [EXO 1:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/1/#v16) L/B · word_choice · "hai hòn đá" (L); "đá sinh" (B) — Probably the stones a woman knelt or sat on to give birth (meaning decision, footnoted); Literal keeps "the two stones".
- [ ] [EXO 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/2/#v3) L/B · word_choice · "hòm" (L, B); "giỏ" (R) — The Hebrew word is the one used of Noah's ark (meaning decision), but Vietnamese "con tàu" (Genesis) would picture a ship; "hòm" keeps a closed vessel, as the English keeps "ark", and Readable says basket as the English does.
- [ ] [EXO 3:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/3/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "TA LÀ ĐẤNG TA LÀ"; "TA LÀ" (L), "Đấng TA LÀ" (B) as a name — The terms note asks this verse to follow the English wording: the name is explained from the verb "to be" (meaning decision, footnoted), and "là" is that verb. "Đấng Hằng Hữu" (the Ever-Existing One), used for the echo of this verse in John 8, explains the name rather than giving it; a reviewer may …
- [ ] [EXO 4:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/4/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "bệnh ngoài da" for the scaly skin disease — The terms note's word for the disease (meaning decision, footnoted): it was a scaly skin disease, not necessarily what is now called leprosy, so "phong" is not used.
- [ ] [EXO 4:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/4/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "làm cho lòng vua cứng rắn" (L); "cứng cỏi" (B); "cứng lòng" (R) — God will make Pharaoh stubborn (meaning decision, footnoted). The Hebrew uses three verbs for this in the coming chapters, "strengthen", "make heavy" and "make hard"; Literal keeps them apart ("cứng rắn", "nặng nề", "cứng"), Balanced and Readable say stubborn with "cứng cỏi", "cứng lòng", as 1 …
- [ ] [EXO 4:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/4/#v25) L/B/R · register · Zipporah to Moses: "anh"; "tôi" (L, B), "em" (R) — The Genesis terms note's address of a wife to her husband; L and B keep "tôi" for the hard, formal tone of a phrase whose meaning is uncertain (meaning decision, footnoted).
- [ ] [EXO 5:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/5/#v2) L/B/R · register · Pharaoh speaks of God as "hắn" (L) or with no pronoun (B, R), and calls himself "ta"; no deity_pronoun span — Pharaoh does not recognise God (meaning decision), and Vietnamese pronouns show the speaker's attitude: "Ngài" would put reverence in his mouth. The English span on "his" has no Vietnamese counterpart here.
- [ ] [EXO 6:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/6/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-mi-na-đáp", "Na-ách-son" as in Matthew 1:4; "em gái" for sister — The Gospel forms of the names. Vietnamese must say older or younger sister; the text does not say, and "em gái" is the usual default.
- [ ] [EXO 7:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/7/#v3) L · word_choice · "làm cho lòng ... cứng" (L) for the verb "make hard" — The second of the three verbs for Pharaoh's heart (meaning decision, footnoted); Literal keeps "cứng" for this one, "cứng rắn" for "strong" (4:21; 7:13) and "nặng nề" for "heavy" (7:14).
- [ ] [EXO 7:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/7/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "con rắn lớn" for the serpent (tannin) — A different word from the "snake" of 4:3 (meaning decision, footnoted); "rắn lớn" keeps the two apart without naming a species.
- [ ] [EXO 8:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/8/#v4) L/B/R · register · Pharaoh names {{divine_name:CHÚA|Gia-vê}}; L keeps the narrator's span "{{deity_pronoun:ngài|Ngài}}" for God, B and R repeat the name — Pharaoh now names Yahweh (meaning decision) and asks a favour of him, so the contempt of "hắn" in 5:2 is gone; Balanced and Readable avoid a pronoun in his mouth.
- [ ] [EXO 8:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/8/#v5) L/B · word_choice · "Xin vua được vinh dự hơn tôi" (L); "Xin bệ hạ cứ định ngày giờ" (B) — You may have the honor of setting the time (meaning decision, footnoted): a courteous turn of phrase; Literal keeps the honor, Balanced says what it grants.
- [ ] [EXO 8:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/8/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "muỗi mắt" for gnats — Small biting insects (meaning decision, footnoted); "muỗi mắt" names tiny gnats, where "chấy rận" (lice) is a narrower reading.
- [ ] [EXO 9:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/9/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "lúa mì spelta" (L, B), "lúa spelta, một loại lúa mì khác" (R) for spelt — Vietnamese has no common word for this hardy wheat; the scientific name gives "spelta". "Lúa mì đen" is rye, a different grain. A reviewer may know a better farming word.
- [ ] [EXO 11:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/11/#v7) L · word_choice · "le lưỡi" (L) for "sharpen its tongue" — A literal "mài lưỡi" means nothing in Vietnamese; "le lưỡi" keeps the dog's tongue in the picture while the sense (meaning decision: not even a dog will growl) is given at Balanced and Readable.
- [ ] [EXO 15:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/15/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "chị của A-rôn"; "trống cơm" for the tambourine — Vietnamese must say whether a sister is older or younger. Miriam watched over the infant Moses (2:4) and Aaron was older than Moses (7:7), so she is taken as the eldest, "chị"; the Hebrew does not say. "Trống cơm" is a small hand drum; a tambourine with jingles may be meant.
- [ ] [EXO 17:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/17/#v9) L/B/R · register · Moses says "tôi" to Joshua — Joshua is Moses' younger assistant, but he is also the commander sent out to battle; "tôi" keeps Moses' words plain and respectful, without the master's "ta". A reviewer may prefer "ta" / "con".
- [ ] [EXO 19:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/19/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Si-na-i" — The form the project's New Testament uses (Acts 7; Galatians 4), kept through the Pentateuch so the two agree.
- [ ] [EXO 19:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/19/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "nổi giận phạt họ" (L), "nổi giận đánh phạt họ" (B), "đánh phạt họ" (R) for "break out against them" — The verb pictures a sudden breach, God striking with death (meaning decision); Vietnamese has no matching verb for a person breaking out, so each level says what happens.
- [ ] [EXO 20:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/20/#v5) L/B · word_choice · "Đức Chúa Trời kỵ tà" (L, B); "Ta đòi hỏi ngươi phải trung thành trọn vẹn" (R) — The Hebrew word is a passion that will not share Israel's loyalty (meaning decision). "kỵ tà", the older Protestant word, keeps God's jealousy apart from human "ghen tuông" and is the word used at 34:14 and in Deuteronomy; the footnote explains it. Readable says what the jealousy demands, as the …
- [ ] [EXO 20:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/20/#v6) L · supplied_words · "ngàn [đời]" (L); "hàng ngàn người" (B, R) — The Hebrew says only "thousands": thousands of people or the thousandth generation (meaning decision). Vietnamese needs a noun; Literal supplies "đời" in brackets to match the generations of verse 5, and Balanced and Readable follow the English reading.
- [ ] [EXO 20:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/20/#v25) L · word_choice · "làm ô uế" (L) — To profane is to make common, no longer fit for God (meaning decision); Literal uses "ô uế", the term sheet's opposite of clean, and Balanced and Readable say that the altar loses its holiness.
- [ ] [EXO 21:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/21/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "nguyền rủa" — To curse is to treat with contempt and call down harm (meaning decision); "nguyền rủa" is the Genesis terms note's word for cursing. The project's Matthew 15:4 and Mark 7:10, which quote this law, say "chửi rủa" at Balanced; a reviewer may want the two to agree.
- [ ] [EXO 22:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/22/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "tận diệt" (L, B); "tiêu diệt hoàn toàn" (R) for "devoted to destruction" — The Hebrew verb means given over wholly to God by being destroyed (meaning decision). Vietnamese has no one word for the dedication; "tận diệt" keeps the completeness, and the meaning decision explains the rest.
- [ ] [EXO 23:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/23/#v5) L · supplied_words · "bỏ mặc" ... "giúp ... [gỡ nó ra]" (L) — The Hebrew uses one verb twice, "leave" and "help" (meaning decision); Vietnamese cannot keep one verb for both, so Literal uses the two senses and brackets what the help is.
- [ ] [EXO 23:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/23/#v12) L · word_choice · "đầy tớ gái" at every level — A slave born in the household (meaning decision), not one bought or sold, so the term sheet's "đầy tớ" stands even at Literal, where the English Literal says "slave woman".
- [ ] [EXO 23:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/23/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "Lễ Mùa Gặt" for the Feast of Harvest; "Lễ Thu Hoạch" for the Feast of Ingathering — These are the feasts later called Weeks and Booths (meaning decisions), "Lễ Các Tuần" and "Lễ Lều Tạm". Here the Hebrew names them by the work of the season, so Vietnamese does too: "gặt" for the reaping of the grain, "thu hoạch" for gathering in the whole year's crops. A reviewer may prefer other …
- [ ] [EXO 25:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/25/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "gỗ keo" — "keo" is the Vietnamese name of the acacia tree (m3); the older transliteration "si-tim" names nothing a reader knows. Used for the whole tabernacle account.
- [ ] [EXO 25:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/25/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "đá mã não sọc" — The stone is not certainly identified (m1). "mã não sọc" (banded onyx, as sardonyx in Revelation 21:20) follows the English "onyx" and keeps "hồng mã não" free for the carnelian of 28:17, as Revelation 4:3 uses it.
- [ ] [EXO 26:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/26/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "khung" — The word may mean open frames or solid boards (m1); "khung" follows the English choice, and "tấm ván" is the familiar older reading.
- [ ] [EXO 26:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/26/#v24) L · word_choice · "đôi ... trọn vẹn" — The Hebrew "twins ... complete" describes doubled corner frames joined together (m1), whose exact build is not certain; Literal keeps the two words as plainly as Vietnamese allows.
- [ ] [EXO 27:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/27/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "tấm rá ... đan như lưới" — A lattice of bronze (m1), whose exact place on the altar is not certain; "rá" names a woven grid without saying what it held.
- [ ] [EXO 28:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/28/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "bảng đeo ngực", "áo ê-phót", "áo choàng", "áo lót dài", "khăn vấn", "đai" — The breastpiece and ephod as the Pentateuch terms note sets them; the other garments get plain modern names kept through the chapter: the robe worn under the ephod "áo choàng", the long shirt "áo lót dài" (as Leviticus 8; bare "áo lót" would read as underwear), the head wrapping "khăn vấn" and the …
- [ ] [EXO 28:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/28/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · the twelve stones named as Revelation 4:3 and 21:19-20 name them in this translation: "hồng mã não, hoàng ngọc, ngọc xanh biển; ngọc lam, lam ngọc, lục bảo … — Several of the stones cannot be identified with certainty (m1). The Vietnamese follows the English identifications, using the names already chosen for the same stones in Revelation, so the high priest's stones and the foundations of the new Jerusalem read alike.
- [ ] [EXO 30:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/30/#v4) L · word_choice · "hai cạnh" — The Hebrew "ribs" means its sides, perhaps its corners (m2); Literal says "cạnh", which can mean either.
- [ ] [EXO 30:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/30/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "thùng (rửa)" (L, B), "chậu lớn" (R); "chân" for its stand — A large bowl for water (m1), on a base; "thùng rửa" keeps it apart from the smaller "chậu" of the altar (27:3), and Readable says "large bowl", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [EXO 30:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/30/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "một dược", "quế", "xương bồ" — The usual Vietnamese names for myrrh and cinnamon; the fragrant cane is not certainly identified (m3), and "xương bồ" (sweet flag) is the common guess.
- [ ] [EXO 30:33](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/30/#v33) L/B/R · word_choice · "bị khai trừ khỏi dân mình" — "cut off from his people" means removed from the community (m2), which may include death; "khai trừ" keeps the Hebrew's openness. "mình" covers men and women, so no gender span is needed.
- [ ] [EXO 30:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/30/#v34) L/B/R · word_choice · "nhựa thơm", "hương ốc", "nhựa khổ thảo", "nhũ hương" — Stacte, onycha and galbanum are not certainly identified (m1-m3); the Vietnamese names describe what each probably was (a fragrant gum, a shell-based scent, a bitter resin), and "nhũ hương" is frankincense.
- [ ] [EXO 34:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/34/#v7) L · supplied_words · "đến hàng ngàn [đời]" (Literal) — Vietnamese needs a noun after "hàng ngàn"; "đời" (generations) follows the end of the verse, as the meaning decision explains. Balanced keeps it without brackets; Readable follows the English "thousands".
- [ ] [EXO 34:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/34/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "kỵ tà" for "jealous" — The word used for God's jealousy through the Pentateuch (20:5): it keeps the sense of the meaning decision, God's zeal for an undivided relationship, without the petty sense of "ghen tị" or "ghen tuông". A reviewer may prefer "ghen" as a name.
- [ ] [EXO 34:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/34/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "Lễ Các Tuần"; "Lễ Thu Hoạch" — "Lễ Các Tuần" as the Pentateuch terms note names the feast; the Feast of Ingathering is named by its sense, the autumn harvest (meaning decision), "Lễ Thu Hoạch" as in 23:16. The same feast is "Lễ Lều Tạm" in the later laws.
- [ ] [EXO 35:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/35/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "da thuộc loại tốt"; "gỗ keo" — The kind of skin is not certainly known (meaning decision); "da thuộc loại tốt" follows the English "fine leather", as the Vietnamese of Exodus 26:14 does. "gỗ keo" is the acacia, the tree Vietnamese calls "keo".
- [ ] [EXO 38:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/38/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "bồn" for the basin and "chân bồn" for its stand — The word the drafts of Exodus 31-37 use; it keeps the large washbasin apart from the altar's blood basins ("chậu", 38:3), though Kings uses "chậu" for the temple basins (1 Kings 7:38). "chân" keeps the stand apart from the "đế" (bases) of the pillars.
- [ ] [EXO 39:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/39/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "đá mã não sọc" for the onyx — Revelation 21:20's word for sardonyx, as the drafts of Exodus 25 and 28 use it; Genesis 2:12 gave the same Hebrew stone as "hồng mã não", which here names the carnelian of the first row.
- [ ] [EXO 39:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/39/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · the twelve stones follow the names Revelation 21:19-20 already uses where the English names agree (topaz "hoàng ngọc", beryl "ngọc xanh biển", sapphire "lam … — Revelation 21:19-20 gives the Vietnamese names; the same list in Exodus 28 is drafted the same way. Several of these stones cannot be identified with certainty, and Genesis 2:12 calls the onyx "hồng mã não".
- [ ] [EXO 39:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/39/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "ngọc lam" (turquoise) beside "lam ngọc" (sapphire) — Both are the usual Vietnamese names of these stones, and "lam ngọc" is Revelation 21:19's sapphire; the two names sound alike, so a reviewer may prefer to change one.
- [ ] [EXO 39:30](https://openwordbible.org/vi/exo/39/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "Thánh khiết cho CHÚA" (L), "Thánh, thuộc về CHÚA" (B, R) — Literal keeps the Hebrew noun "holiness"; Balanced and Readable say what the inscription means, that the one who wears it belongs to the LORD, rather than the bare "Thánh cho", which is not natural Vietnamese.

## Lê-vi Ký (28)

- [ ] [LEV 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/2/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "bông lúa" for the ears of grain; "giã nát" for the crushed kernels — Vietnamese "lúa" can mean grain in general, which keeps the Hebrew's open "fresh ears" without naming rice; "lúa mạch" (barley) would choose a crop the verse does not name.
- [ ] [LEV 3:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/3/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "phần phụ trên gan" (Literal); "thùy dài của gan" (Balanced); "phần nhô ra trên gan" (Readable) — Literal keeps the Hebrew's vague "appendage"; Balanced names the long lobe, as the English does. Readable describes the part as one that sticks out from the liver, which says the likely sense in plain words without adding a judgement of quality.
- [ ] [LEV 5:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/5/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "[lễ vật] đền lỗi" (Literal); "lễ vật đền lỗi" (Balanced); "để đền lỗi" (Readable) for "his guilt" here — Here the Hebrew word means what is owed for the wrong, not the named guilt offering, since the animal is a sin offering. "đền" (make amends) says that, while "tế lễ chuộc lỗi" is kept for the offering itself.
- [ ] [LEV 6:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/6/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "hành lang" (Literal, Balanced), "sân" (Readable) for the courtyard of the tent — "hành lang" is the word for the enclosed court of the sanctuary in Protestant Vietnamese; Readable says "sân", the everyday word for a courtyard.
- [ ] [LEV 7:37](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/7/#v37) L/B/R · word_choice · "[tế lễ] tấn phong" (Literal), "tế lễ tấn phong" (Balanced); "tế lễ lập thầy tế lễ" (Readable) for the ordination offering — "tấn phong" names the installing of the priests in office (chapter 8); Readable says what the offering is for, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [LEV 8:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/8/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "áo lót dài" (tunic), "đai lưng" (sash), "áo choàng" (robe), "áo ê-phót" (ephod), "dây thắt lưng" / "dây lưng dệt" (its band) — Plain names for the high priest's clothing; "áo ê-phót" is the Pentateuch sheet's form. Readable adds a short explanation of the ephod, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [LEV 8:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/8/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "khăn vấn" (turban); "tấm thẻ bằng vàng" (plate); "mão thánh" (crown of holiness) — "mão" is the word for a ceremonial headdress and avoids the royal picture of "vương miện"; "khăn vấn" describes a wrapped linen headdress.
- [ ] [LEV 8:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/8/#v31) L/B/R · register · Moses to Aaron and his sons: "ta" / "các ngươi" (Literal); "tôi" with no second-person pronoun or "anh em" (Balanced, Readable) — Moses gives the LORD's command to his older brother and his nephews. Literal keeps the bare authority of the command; Balanced and Readable use "tôi" for Moses and imperatives or "anh em" for the priests, the term sheet's form for Moses speaking, without "các ngươi", which would sound harsh from a …
- [ ] [LEV 9:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/9/#v2) L/B/R · register · Moses calls Aaron "anh" at every level — Aaron is Moses' older brother (Exodus 7:7); the Genesis sheet has a younger brother say "anh" to an older one. Moses speaks with authority through imperatives, so he needs no word for himself.
- [ ] [LEV 10:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/10/#v19) L/B/R · register · Aaron speaks to Moses with "tôi" and no word of address — Aaron is the older brother but speaks here to Moses as leader; Vietnamese can leave the address out, so no relation word is forced on the sentence.
- [ ] [LEV 11:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/11/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "con đa-man" (rock badger, the hyrax) — The rock hyrax has a Vietnamese zoological name, "đa-man"; a literal "lửng đá" would name a badger, which it is not. The English footnote explains the animal.
- [ ] [LEV 11:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/11/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "con heo" (pig) — "heo" is the word of the south and of most church use; "lợn" is the northern word. A reviewer may prefer the northern form.
- [ ] [LEV 11:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/11/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · Bird names (verses 13-19) are the nearest Vietnamese names for the English identifications — Many of these birds cannot be identified with certainty (see the English footnotes); the Vietnamese follows the English identification with the usual Vietnamese name for each bird, e.g. "kên kên râu" (bearded vulture), "cú lợn" (barn owl), "chim đầu rìu" (hoopoe).
- [ ] [LEV 13:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/13/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "bệnh ngoài da nghiêm trọng" (scale disease) — The term sheet's choice: the Hebrew word covers several skin conditions and is not modern leprosy (Hansen's disease), so the traditional "phong" / "phong hủi" is not used; readers who know it from older Bibles will find it named here. Used through chapters 13-14.
- [ ] [LEV 13:30](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/13/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "chốc lở" (scall); Readable "một mảng ngứa có vảy" — A scaly, itching patch on the scalp or chin; "chốc lở" is the closest common name, though the condition cannot be identified with certainty. Readable describes it instead.
- [ ] [LEV 13:47](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/13/#v47) L/B/R · word_choice · Cloth and leather: Literal "vết của bệnh nghiêm trọng", Balanced and Readable "mốc" (mold) — The Hebrew uses the same word as for the skin disease; Literal keeps a word for disease without "ngoài da", since this is cloth, and the Balanced and Readable say "mốc", as the English does.
- [ ] [LEV 14:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/14/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "gỗ bá hương" (cedar wood), "chỉ đỏ thẫm" (scarlet yarn; Readable "chỉ đỏ"), "cây kinh giới" (hyssop) — The usual Vietnamese names; "kinh giới" for hyssop is the traditional church word, though the plant is not the Vietnamese herb of that name.
- [ ] [LEV 14:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/14/#v10) B/R · setting_alternative · {{units:khoảng 6,6 lít|ba phần mười ê-pha}}; {{units:khoảng 0,3 lít|một lóc}} — Metric by default with the original unit as the alternative, as the Vietnamese measures elsewhere; an ephah is about 22 litres (a tenth about 2.2 litres) and a log about a third of a litre. Literal keeps "ba phần mười [ê-pha]" and "một lóc" as the English Literal does. "lóc" is the traditional …
- [ ] [LEV 15:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/15/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "bị chảy dịch" (has a discharge), "chất dịch" (the discharge) — A plain, modest description of an abnormal flow; "chảy mủ" or a disease name would claim more than the Hebrew says. "từ thân thể mình" keeps the Hebrew's discreet "from his flesh".
- [ ] [LEV 16:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/16/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "vải gai" for linen — The word for linen in the sanctuary and the priests' clothing that Chronicles already uses in Vietnamese ("vải gai mịn"); "vải lanh" is the more modern word and a reviewer may prefer it.
- [ ] [LEV 16:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/16/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-xa-sên" for Azazel, kept as a name — As the English, the name is kept and its unknown meaning is left to the footnote of the meaning decision. "A-xa-sên" is the form most Vietnamese Protestant readers know.
- [ ] [LEV 16:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/16/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "máu" for the blood of the offerings, at every level — "máu" is the everyday word and is used through Leviticus, also in 17:11 where the life is in the blood; "huyết" is the older word that the New Testament keeps for the blood of Christ. A reviewer may prefer "huyết" for the sacrificial blood.
- [ ] [LEV 16:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/16/#v31) L · word_choice · "hạ mình xuống" for "afflict yourselves" at Literal; "kiêng ăn để hạ mình xuống" (B) and "nhịn ăn và hạ mình" (R) — The Hebrew says "afflict your souls". Word for word, Vietnamese would sound like self-harm; "hạ mình xuống" keeps the sense of humbling oneself, and the other levels name the fasting as the English does (meaning decision).
- [ ] [LEV 17:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/17/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "sự sống" for the life in the blood and "mạng sống" for "your lives" — The Hebrew word is the same (nefesh), but Vietnamese needs "sự sống" for the life that is in a creature and "mạng sống" for the lives of people that are ransomed; "linh hồn" (soul) would bring in a sense the meaning decision does not give.
- [ ] [LEV 18:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/18/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "anh em cha ngươi" for the father's brother, "chú bác" for the uncle, and "thím" for his wife — Vietnamese names relatives by age and side of the family. The Hebrew does not say whether the uncle is older or younger than the father; "chú bác" takes in both, and "thím" (the wife of a father's younger brother) is the usual general word for an aunt by marriage on the father's side. A reviewer …
- [ ] [LEV 23:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/23/#v24) B/R · word_choice · "thổi kèn" at Balanced and Readable for the loud blast — The Hebrew names a loud blast without saying of what (meaning decision, footnoted); Vietnamese needs a verb, and "thổi kèn" gives the usual reading, as the English "loud blasts are to be sounded" implies. The Literal keeps "tiếng thổi vang".
- [ ] [LEV 25:39](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/25/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "nô lệ" for a slave bought and sold; "đầy tớ của Ta" where God calls Israel his own (25:42, 55) — The term sheet keeps "nô lệ" for slaves bought and sold and "đầy tớ" for a servant; the English uses "slaves" in both places to keep the Hebrew wordplay. Vietnamese "nô lệ của Ta" would sound harsh for God's people, so the contrast is carried by "đầy tớ của Ta ... không được bán như nô lệ". A …
- [ ] [LEV 27:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lev/27/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "vật hiến dâng trọn", "dâng trọn" (devoted thing, devote) — The word means something given over to God wholly and for good, beyond buying back; "dâng trọn" says this and stays distinct from "biệt riêng" (consecrate), which in this chapter can be redeemed. For a person under the ban (verse 29) Balanced says "bị đặt dưới án tận diệt".

## Dân Số Ký (53)

- [ ] [NUM 3:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/3/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "Dít-sê-ha" for Izhar — Both spellings occur in the Vietnamese of Exodus and Chronicles; "Dít-sê-ha" is the form the Protestant tradition uses in Numbers, and it is kept through Numbers. A reviewer may want one form for the whole Bible.
- [ ] [NUM 4:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/4/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "cái cáng" for the carrying frame — The object is a frame or pole on which a load is borne by men walking at both ends, which is what "cáng" names; "đòn" stays for the poles fixed to the ark, table and altars.
- [ ] [NUM 5:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/5/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "bệnh ngoài da nghiêm trọng" (scale disease) — The term sheet's choice, as Leviticus 13-14 uses it: the Hebrew word covers several skin conditions and is not modern leprosy, so the traditional "phong" is not used.
- [ ] [NUM 5:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/5/#v13) L/B/R · register · the wife is "nàng" (L, B) and "cô ấy" / "cô" (R); the priest speaking to her says "ngươi" (L) and "bà" (B, R) — Vietnamese must choose a pronoun for the woman. "nàng" is the usual third person for a woman in written narrative; Readable uses the plainer "cô ấy". The priest's oath is formal and solemn, so he addresses her with the respectful "bà" at Balanced and Readable, and with "ngươi" at Literal like the …
- [ ] [NUM 5:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/5/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "đùi ... teo đi" (L, B), "dạ con ... teo lại" (R); "bụng ... phình lên" — Literal and Balanced keep the Hebrew "thigh"; Readable says "dạ con" (womb), as the English Readable does, because the meaning decision says the harm ends her ability to bear children.
- [ ] [NUM 7:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/7/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "khánh thành bàn thờ" for the dedication of the altar — "khánh thành" is the ordinary word for opening something new for use, which is what the gifts marked; it also stays clear of "bánh cung hiến".
- [ ] [NUM 9:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/9/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "bị khai trừ khỏi dân mình" for "cut off from his people" — "khai trừ" says the person is removed from the community, without settling whether death is meant, which the Hebrew also leaves open.
- [ ] [NUM 10:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/10/#v29) L/B/R · register · Moses and Hobab speak as "tôi" / "chúng tôi" and "ông" — Two grown men of different peoples, related by marriage, speak politely and as equals; "cha" would settle that Hobab himself is the father-in-law, which the meaning decision leaves open.
- [ ] [NUM 11:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/11/#v29) L/B/R · register · Moses calls Joshua "con" and himself "ta" — Joshua had served Moses since his youth; Vietnamese speaks from the older master to his young attendant with "ta" and "con", which is warm rather than harsh.
- [ ] [NUM 13:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/13/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · names of the twelve men in their usual Protestant forms (Sam-mua, Xa-cu, Sa-phát, Hô-ri, Di-ganh, Hô-sê, Phan-ti, Ra-phu, Gát-đi-ên, Sô-đi, Ga-đi, Su-si, … — Names not in the term sheets follow the usual Protestant transliteration, as the sheets ask; "con trai" without "của" keeps a list of names short.
- [ ] [NUM 13:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/13/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "Hô-sê" and "Giô-suê" — "Giô-suê" is the term sheet's form; "Hô-sê" keeps the h of the Hebrew so that it is not confused with the prophet Hosea ("Ô-sê"). The meanings of the two names are left to the meaning decision, as the English leaves them.
- [ ] [NUM 13:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/13/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "Lê-bô Ha-mát" as a place name — The English reads a place name and footnotes "the entrance of Hamath" (meaning decision); Vietnamese follows, with "Xin" and "Rê-hốp" in their usual Protestant forms.
- [ ] [NUM 14:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/14/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · God addresses the people as "các ngươi" at every level in 14:25-35 — These are words of judgment on the whole rebellious generation; the distance of "các ngươi" fits them at every level, where "các con" would sound tender.
- [ ] [NUM 15:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/15/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "bột nhồi" for dough; "ổ bánh" for the loaf; "sân đạp lúa" for the threshing floor — The first of what is prepared from the new grain (meaning decision, footnoted); "bột nhồi" is the kneaded dough, "mẻ" the batch.
- [ ] [NUM 15:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/15/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · "tua" for tassels; "màu xanh dương" for blue; "góc áo" for the corners of the garment — Twisted threads hanging from the cloak (meaning decision); "xanh dương" is the colour word settled for the same dye in the Vietnamese Exodus, though the meaning decision allows blue or violet.
- [ ] [NUM 15:39](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/15/#v39) L/B · word_choice · "đi lang thang" for go about — The verb is the one used of the scouts (meaning decision); Vietnamese cannot keep the echo of "do thám" without losing the sense of roaming after one's desires.
- [ ] [NUM 16:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/16/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · Moses calls Korah's band "các ngươi" and Korah "ngươi" (L); "các ông" and "ông" (B, R) — These are Levites of standing in open revolt; "anh em", the term sheet's word for Moses speaking to the people, would sound too warm, and "các người" too contemptuous before the LORD has judged. Uncertain for a reviewer.
- [ ] [NUM 16:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/16/#v11) B/R · word_choice · Moses speaks of Aaron as "anh ấy" (B, R); "ông ấy" (L) — Aaron is Moses' older brother, as in the Vietnamese Exodus 32, where Moses calls him "anh"; Literal keeps the more formal "ông ấy".
- [ ] [NUM 16:35](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/16/#v35) L/B · word_choice · "thiêu nuốt" (L, B) for consumed — The words the Vietnamese Leviticus 10:2 uses for the fire that consumed Nadab and Abihu, whose fate this recalls (meaning decision).
- [ ] [NUM 17:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/17/#v17) L/B · word_choice · "nhà cha" (L); "gia tộc" (B); "chi tộc" (R) for the father's house — The term sheet gives "nhà cha" at Literal; here a father's house is a whole tribe (meaning decision), so Balanced uses "gia tộc" for an ancestral family and Readable names the tribe.
- [ ] [NUM 17:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/17/#v22) L/B · word_choice · "lều chứng ước" (L, B) for the tent of the testimony — The tent of meeting, named for the covenant tablets it held (meaning decision); lowercase "lều" keeps it from looking like a new proper name beside "Lều Hội Kiến", as the Vietnamese Exodus writes "Đền Tạm chứng ước".
- [ ] [NUM 18:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/18/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · God calls Aaron "ngươi" (L), "con" (B, R), and Aaron with his sons "các ngươi" (L), "các con" (B, R); "nơi thánh" (lowercase) for the sanctuary — As settled for the Pentateuch, God speaks to Moses, Aaron or one person with "ngươi" at Literal and "con" at Balanced and Readable; "nơi thánh" in lowercase is the sanctuary as a whole, as settled in the Vietnamese Exodus. Recorded once for this chapter.
- [ ] [NUM 18:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/18/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "vật đã được hiến dâng trọn" for the devoted thing (herem) — Something given over wholly to the LORD (meaning decision); "hiến dâng trọn" says it can never be taken back, without the older "phú dâng". A reviewer may want the word later used for things devoted to destruction.
- [ ] [NUM 18:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/18/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · God's charge to Moses has "ngươi" only at L ("Ngươi phải nói"); the Levites are "các ngươi" at every level — The speech is a law for the Levites, given through Moses; God's laws to the people keep "các ngươi" at every level. Balanced and Readable need no pronoun for Moses here ("Hãy nói", "Hãy bảo").
- [ ] [NUM 18:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/18/#v32) L/B · word_choice · "làm ô uế" (L); "xúc phạm" (B); "xem ... như thức ăn thường" (R) for profane — To treat holy gifts as common (meaning decision); Literal uses the usual Vietnamese verb for profaning, Balanced the plainer "xúc phạm", and Readable says what it means.
- [ ] [NUM 20:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/20/#v10) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to the people as "các ngươi" (Literal) and "anh em" (Balanced, Readable), and of himself and Aaron as "chúng ta" (Literal) / "chúng tôi" … — As the Pentateuch term sheet for Moses speaking to Israel. "chúng tôi" at Balanced and Readable leaves the people out of the "we", as Moses means himself and Aaron; Literal keeps the plainer "chúng ta". The question is left open, as the meaning decision's footnote asks.
- [ ] [NUM 20:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/20/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "Y-sơ-ra-ên, em của ông" (your brother Israel), with "ông" for the king — Vietnamese must say older or younger brother: Israel comes from Jacob, the younger twin of Esau, the father of Edom, so "em". The king is addressed as "ông", as the envoys address him in the Vietnamese Judges 11:17.
- [ ] [NUM 21:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/21/#v5) L/B/R · register · "các người" (you, plural) for God and Moses together — The people speak to God and Moses as one plural "you". Vietnamese has no neutral plural: "các người" is cold and disrespectful, which fits a complaint spoken against them, and, like the English, gives God no word of honour here.
- [ ] [NUM 22:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/22/#v9) L/B/R · register · God speaks to Balaam as "ngươi" at every level; Balaam answers God with "con" — God addresses Moses as "con" at Balanced and Readable because he is God's servant; Balaam is a foreign seer called to account, so God keeps the distant "ngươi". Balaam speaks to God as people do in prayer, "con".
- [ ] [NUM 22:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/22/#v26) B/R · register · The narrator calls the angel of the LORD "ngài" in lower case, with no deity_pronoun span; the angel says "ta" and calls Balaam "ngươi", and Balaam answers him … — The angel speaks with the LORD's authority, so Vietnamese gives him a word of respect; the English has no deity_pronoun span for him, so Vietnamese adds none.
- [ ] [NUM 22:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/22/#v28) L/B/R · register · The donkey calls Balaam "ông" and herself "tôi"; Balaam calls her "ngươi" and himself "ta" — The donkey speaks as a faithful servant to her master; Balaam speaks down to her in anger. "mày / tao" would be more lifelike but is coarse for public reading.
- [ ] [NUM 24:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/24/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "con cháu Sết" (the sons of Sheth), with no gender span — Vietnamese "con cháu" already includes all the people, so the English setting (children / sons, people / sons) has no Vietnamese counterpart. "Sết" is the same name as Seth in Genesis, as the Hebrew is; who the people are is left open, as the meaning decision's footnote says.
- [ ] [NUM 25:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/25/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "treo ... lên" (Literal), "xử tử ... treo ... lên" (Balanced), "xử tử ... treo xác ... lên cọc" (Readable) for "impale" — The meaning decision reads the verb as putting to death and displaying the bodies; Vietnamese says both parts plainly at Balanced and Readable, and the rarer "đóng cọc" would be unclear.
- [ ] [NUM 26:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/26/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · Names as the Vietnamese Genesis 46, Exodus 6, Joshua 17 and 1 Chronicles spell them (Hê-nóc, Pha-lu, Hết-rôn, Cạt-mi; Thô-la, Phu-va; Mách-la, Nô-a, Hốt-la, … — Kept the same through the Bible so the lists can be compared; names found in no earlier Vietnamese book (Xê-phôn, Óc-ni, A-rốt, I-ê-xe, Ê-ran, A-hi-ram, Sê-phu-pham, Hu-pham, Su-cham) follow the usual Protestant transliteration.
- [ ] [NUM 26:59](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/26/#v59) B/R · word_choice · "chị của họ" (their older sister) for Miriam at Balanced and Readable; Literal "chị em của họ" — Vietnamese must say older or younger: Miriam is usually taken to be the sister who watched over the baby Moses (Exodus 2:4), so older than Moses; Literal keeps the neutral "chị em".
- [ ] [NUM 27:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/27/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "thần linh" (lowercase) — The Hebrew says "spirit" with no article, and the English keeps "the spirit" in lowercase; Vietnamese keeps the lowercase word and lets the footnote on the meaning decision speak to it.
- [ ] [NUM 28:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/28/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "tháng giêng" for the first month — As the Vietnamese Leviticus 23:5; the first month of the year by the moon.
- [ ] [NUM 28:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/28/#v22) R · word_choice · "để tội lỗi các ngươi được tha" — The English Readable keeps "make atonement" here; plain Vietnamese "chuộc tội" sounds like paying for a crime, so Readable says what the offering does for the people.
- [ ] [NUM 29:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/29/#v1) B/R · supplied_words · "thổi kèn" (B, R) — The Hebrew names only a loud blast or shout; Vietnamese needs an instrument to blow, and the Vietnamese Leviticus 23:24 supplies "kèn" in the same way.
- [ ] [NUM 30:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/30/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "nàng" (L) / "cô" (B, R) for the young woman of verses 4-9 — A young unmarried woman in her father's house, and the same woman newly married in verses 7-9; the pronoun that fits her age. Literal keeps "nàng" through the chapter.
- [ ] [NUM 30:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/30/#v14) L/B/R · register · "bà" for the married woman in verses 11-16 — Verse 11 turns to a woman in her husband's household; Balanced and Readable use the pronoun for a married woman, Literal keeps "nàng" through the chapter.
- [ ] [NUM 31:50](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/31/#v50) B/R · word_choice · "dây chuyền" — The last word names a piece of gold jewelry, perhaps a necklace; Balanced and Readable follow the English "necklaces", Literal keeps the general word.
- [ ] [NUM 31:54](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/31/#v54) L/B · word_choice · "làm kỷ niệm" — A reminder kept before the LORD on Israel's behalf; "kỷ niệm" is the plain word for something kept so as to be remembered.
- [ ] [NUM 32:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/32/#v5) L · word_choice · "làm sản nghiệp" — The word here (ahuzzah) is the "possession" of 27:4; in a request for land of their own, "sản nghiệp" reads naturally at Literal, with "của riêng" and "đất riêng" at the other levels.
- [ ] [NUM 32:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/32/#v14) L · word_choice · "một dòng giống tội nhân" — The word means a new growth of the same kind, the next generation; "dòng giống" carries that sense in Vietnamese.
- [ ] [NUM 32:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/32/#v29) B/R · register · Moses to Eleazar, Joshua and the leaders: "các ông" (B, R) — Here Moses gives orders to the priest and the leaders, not to the people as a whole; "các ông" is respectful to them. Literal keeps "các ngươi".
- [ ] [NUM 33:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/33/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · The stations of verses 18-35 in the usual Protestant transliteration ("Rít-ma", "Ri-môn Phê-rết", "Kê-hê-la-tha", "Mác-hê-lốt", "Bê-nê Gia-a-can", "Hô … — Places named only in this list; no other Vietnamese book of the project has them yet.
- [ ] [NUM 33:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/33/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ta-rách" for the place Terah — The place is spelled like the name of Abraham's father, but the station is kept apart from the person, as the Protestant tradition does.
- [ ] [NUM 33:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/33/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · "tháng năm" for the fifth month — Months are named by number, as "tháng bảy" in 29:1; the reader should not take it for May.
- [ ] [NUM 33:41](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/33/#v41) L/B/R · word_choice · "Xanh-mô-na", "Phu-nôn" — Usual Protestant forms for these two stations.
- [ ] [NUM 33:45](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/33/#v45) L · word_choice · "Y-dim" — Literal keeps the short form of the name the Hebrew uses here, as the English Literal does.
- [ ] [NUM 34:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/34/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "dốc Ạc-ráp-bim" (L, B), "đèo Bọ Cạp" (R); "Ca-đe-Ba-nê-a" — The pass follows Joshua 15:3 in Vietnamese. The town is spelled with "Ca-đe", the form the term sheet and Numbers 13-27 use for Kadesh, so that the two names visibly belong together; Joshua spells it "Ca-đê-Ba-nê-a", and a reviewer should make the books agree.
- [ ] [NUM 35:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/num/35/#v19) L/B · word_choice · "người báo thù máu" — Blood is "máu" everywhere in the Pentateuch drafts, not the Sino-Vietnamese "huyết"; 2 Samuel 14:11 in Vietnamese already has "kẻ báo thù máu". Joshua 20 keeps "huyết", so a reviewer should make the books agree.

## Phục Truyền Luật Lệ Ký (34)

- [ ] [DEU 1:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/1/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ca-đe-Ba-nê-a" — Kadesh-barnea is written as one hyphenated name, as in Numbers 34:4, Joshua 15:3 and Deuteronomy 9:23; Kadesh alone is "Ca-đe" (as Numbers 20:1).
- [ ] [DEU 2:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/2/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "Gia-hát" — The form of Numbers 21:23, which tells the same battle; Joshua 13:18 and 21:36 have "Gia-hác", and a reviewer should make them agree.
- [ ] [DEU 3:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/3/#v21) L/B/R · register · Moses to Joshua: "ngươi" (L), "con" (B, R) — Joshua is Moses' younger assistant, who served him from his youth; Balanced and Readable have Moses call him "con", as an older leader speaks to the one he trains. The plural "your God" in the same sentence means the whole people, so it is "của các ngươi" (L) and "của anh em" (B, R).
- [ ] [DEU 4:48](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/4/#v48) L/B/R · word_choice · "Si-ôn" — Another name for Hermon (meaning decision), spelled with "S" as the Hebrew "Siyon"; it is not the Zion of Jerusalem, and the following "tức là núi Hẹt-môn" makes that plain.
- [ ] [DEU 6:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/6/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "[là] một" (L); "là duy nhất" (B, R) — "One" means one and unique (meaning decision): Literal keeps the bare "một", as Mark 12:29 does at Literal; Balanced and Readable say "duy nhất", as Mark 12:29 does there. The words are kept in the Hebrew order, so the other readings the footnote gives stay open.
- [ ] [DEU 10:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/10/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "Bê-ê-rốt Bê-nê Gia-a-can", "Mô-sê-ra" — Protestant-style transliterations, chosen here; Readable translates "Bê-ê-rốt" as "các giếng nước", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [DEU 10:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/10/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "Gút-gô-đa", "Giốt-ba-tha" — Transliterations chosen here, in the Protestant pattern.
- [ ] [DEU 14:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/14/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "hoẵng" (roe deer, as 1 Kings 5:3), "dê rừng" (wild goat), "sơn dương" (ibex), "linh dương sừng dài" (antelope), "cừu núi" (mountain sheep) — Probable identifications, as in the English; "linh dương sừng dài" keeps the antelope apart from the gazelle ("linh dương").
- [ ] [DEU 14:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/14/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "rượu mạnh" for strong drink — Fermented drink other than wine; Vietnamese has no older word for beer, and "bia" would sound modern, so "rượu mạnh" is kept at every level.
- [ ] [DEU 15:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/15/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "sự miễn trừ" (L), "tha nợ" (B), "xóa nợ" (R) for the release (shemittah); "năm miễn trừ" (L) / "năm tha nợ" (B, R) for the year of release — Literal keeps a general word for letting go, as the Hebrew does; Balanced and Readable say what is let go, as the English does. Kept through this chapter.
- [ ] [DEU 18:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/18/#v8) L · supplied_words · "ngoài [tiền] bán [tài sản] của người ấy theo nhà cha" (L) — The Hebrew phrase is terse and uncertain (meaning decision, footnoted); Literal brackets what Vietnamese needs to make it a phrase, and follows the meaning decision's reading.
- [ ] [DEU 19:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/19/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "người báo thù máu" (L, B); "người bà con báo thù cho người chết" (R) — The dead person's near relative, who was to take life for life (meaning decision). Blood is "máu" everywhere in these books as settled; Joshua 20 still has "huyết" and should follow when it is revised.
- [ ] [DEU 23:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/23/#v3) L · word_choice · "con ngoại hôn" (L) for mamzer — The word means a child of a union the law forbids (meaning decision, footnoted); "con ngoại hôn" is neutral, while "con hoang" is an insult and narrower in sense.
- [ ] [DEU 24:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/24/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "tờ ly dị" for the certificate of divorce — A written document of release given to the wife (meaning decision); "tờ ly dị" is plain modern Vietnamese, and Matthew 19:7 says "giấy ly dị" for the same document, which a reviewer may wish to align.
- [ ] [DEU 25:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/25/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "làm bổn phận của anh em chồng" — The duty of a husband's brother, the custom of levirate marriage (meaning decision); Genesis 38:8 names it "bổn phận của em chồng". "anh em chồng" is used here because the brother may be older or younger.
- [ ] [DEU 26:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/26/#v5) L/B · word_choice · "lang thang" (L adds "sắp chết") for the Aramean who was perishing — The Hebrew word means wandering and close to being lost (see the meaning note); "lang thang" carries the homeless wandering, and the Literal keeps the danger as well.
- [ ] [DEU 26:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/26/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · the worshiper calls himself "con" once the words turn to prayer ("Ngài đã ban cho con, lạy CHÚA"), after "tôi" and "chúng tôi" in the story of verses 5-9 — People speaking to God say "con" in this translation; the change follows the turn the meaning note describes, from telling the story before the LORD to speaking to him.
- [ ] [DEU 27:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/27/#v13) B · supplied_words · "để công bố sự nguyền rủa" for "for the curse" — The tribes do not curse the people (see the meaning note); they stand on the side where the curse is pronounced.
- [ ] [DEU 28:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/28/#v22) L · word_choice · "bệnh lao lực" for the wasting disease — A disease that wears the body away; "bệnh lao" would name tuberculosis too exactly.
- [ ] [DEU 28:63](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/28/#v63) L/B/R · word_choice · "vui thích" (L, B), "vui lòng" (R) for the LORD rejoicing to destroy — The Hebrew uses the same verb for both halves; Vietnamese keeps one verb so the reversal is heard, as the meaning note asks.
- [ ] [DEU 29:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/29/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ta" and "các ngươi" in verses 4-5, where Moses speaks the LORD's words in the first person — The meaning note says the "I" here is God (verse 5: "I am the LORD your God"); Vietnamese marks God speaking to Israel with "Ta" and "các ngươi", as the settled choice for God's address to Israel.
- [ ] [DEU 31:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/31/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · Moses calls Joshua "con", as in Numbers 11:29, where he is Moses' young aide — An older leader to his assistant; Exodus 17:9 has Moses speak to Joshua without a pronoun of address, so this is the first place it is fixed.
- [ ] [DEU 31:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/31/#v10) L/B · word_choice · "năm giải trừ" (L), "năm giải trừ nợ" (B), "năm xóa nợ" (R) for the year of release — The seventh year when debts were cancelled (15:1-2); R says it plainly.
- [ ] [DEU 31:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/31/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · God speaks of the unfaithful people as "chúng" — In God's words of judgment "chúng" fits the tone, as "họ" would be more neutral; the people as one body ("it") is not kept in Vietnamese.
- [ ] [DEU 32:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/32/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · the song keeps Moses' address forms: "ngươi" / "các ngươi" at L and "anh em" at B and R for Israel; "Ngài" for the LORD; "Ta" and "các ngươi" where God himself … — Follows the term sheet for Moses speaking to Israel and the settled form for God speaking to Israel; recorded once for the song.
- [ ] [DEU 32:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/32/#v10) L/B · word_choice · "nó" for Israel pictured as one person in verses 10-15 — The Hebrew speaks of Jacob as "him"; Vietnamese "nó" keeps the single figure. R says "họ" for the people, as the English R says "them".
- [ ] [DEU 32:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/32/#v15) L/B · word_choice · "Giê-su-run" for Jeshurun, as in Deuteronomy 33:5, 26 — The form used in the Vietnamese of chapter 33; R says "Y-sơ-ra-ên", as the English R says Israel.
- [ ] [DEU 32:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/32/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "nổi lòng kỵ tà" for God made jealous, "ghen tức" for Israel made jealous — "kỵ tà" is settled for God's jealousy (qanna); the same Hebrew verb for Israel's envy of another nation is plain "ghen tức".
- [ ] [DEU 33:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/33/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "luật pháp rực lửa" (a blazing law) — Follows the reading the scribes' margin note gives, "fire" and "law" as two words; Vietnamese puts "rực lửa" after the noun as a description, so the two words stay together as one picture.
- [ ] [DEU 33:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/33/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ngài" for God both spoken of and spoken to — In the poem Moses speaks of God and to God in turn; "Ngài" serves for both in Vietnamese prayer, so the switch between "his" and "your" is not visible. Keeping one word avoids a reader taking "Chúa" for a second person.
- [ ] [DEU 33:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/33/#v5) L/B · word_choice · "Giê-su-run" for Jeshurun — The usual Protestant transliteration of this honorary name; no earlier Vietnamese text in the project has it. 33:26 uses the same form, and Deuteronomy 32:15 should match it.
- [ ] [DEU 33:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/33/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · a tribe spoken of as one man is "nó" at Literal and Balanced; Readable says "họ" or names the tribe — Follows Jacob's blessing of the tribes in Genesis 49, where the Vietnamese already calls each son "nó"; Readable follows the English Readable, which speaks of the tribe's people. Used for every tribe in this chapter.
- [ ] [DEU 33:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/33/#v18) L/B/R · register · a tribe addressed is "ngươi" at Literal and "anh em" at Balanced and Readable — Moses speaking to Israel is "anh em" at Balanced and Readable throughout Deuteronomy, and a tribe addressed in his blessing is part of that people; Literal keeps the singular "you" as "ngươi". Used for Zebulun, Issachar, Naphtali, Asher and Israel in this chapter.
- [ ] [DEU 34:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/deu/34/#v6) L/B/R · word_order · passive "ông được chôn" (he was buried) — The Hebrew "he buried him" does not name its subject. Vietnamese must choose a pronoun that marks either God ("Ngài") or people ("người ta", which is also plural); the passive leaves the burier unstated, as the English "he" without deity marking does, and the footnotes give the readings.

## Giô-suê (1)

- [ ] [JOS 15:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jos/15/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "em của Ca-lép": the younger brother — Vietnamese must say whether a brother is older or younger. Othniel belongs to the next generation of leaders (Judges 3:9), so he is taken as the younger; whether "brother" here is Othniel or Kenaz is a question of meaning the English decisions discuss.

## Các Quan Xét (24)

- [ ] [JDG 1:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/1/#v7) L/B/R · register · the captive king says "ta" — A defeated king still speaks as a king about the kings he once ruled; "ta" keeps his pride in his own words.
- [ ] [JDG 2:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/2/#v1) L/B/R · register · the LORD calls Israel "các ngươi" — This is a rebuke to a people who have broken faith; "các ngươi" keeps the distance of a judge speaking, which "các con" would soften.
- [ ] [JDG 3:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/3/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "bị tật ở tay phải" (L); "thuận tay trái" (B, R) — The Hebrew says his right hand was restricted, which means he was left-handed (m1); Literal keeps the Hebrew way of saying it.
- [ ] [JDG 4:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/4/#v6) L/B/R · register · Deborah and Barak speak as "tôi" and "ông" / "bà" — Deborah speaks for God but to a leader of Israel as an equal in rank; "ông" and "bà" keep the respect each owes the other. The LORD's own words in her mouth keep his forms ("ngươi" at Literal, verse 7).
- [ ] [JDG 5:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/5/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "các việc công chính" (L, B); "những việc công chính và giải cứu" (R) — The LORD's righteous acts are his saving deeds for his people (m2); "công chính" is the word kept for righteousness, and Readable adds that these acts rescued Israel, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [JDG 5:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/5/#v16) L/B/R · register · the song addresses the tribes as "ngươi" — The rebuke to Reuben speaks to a tribe as one person (m3); "ngươi" is the poetic form of address of a singer rebuking, as in the Psalms, and also covers Benjamin in verse 14.
- [ ] [JDG 5:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/5/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "rủa sả" for the curse — "rủa sả" is the curse spoken with authority, which the angel of the LORD speaks here (m2, m3); the Genesis note's "nguyền rủa" stays for the general word.
- [ ] [JDG 6:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/6/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây sồi" (L, B); "một cây lớn" (R) — The Hebrew names a large, long-lived tree (m2), the terebinth, which has no common Vietnamese name; "cây sồi" is the familiar word for such a great tree, and Readable says only "a big tree".
- [ ] [JDG 6:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/6/#v15) L/B/R · register · "Lạy Chúa", and "con" for himself — The Hebrew vowels here mark the title used for God (m1), so Gideon now speaks as one praying: "Chúa" for the Lord and "con" for himself, as the Genesis note sets.
- [ ] [JDG 7:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/7/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "Gươm của CHÚA và của Ghê-đê-ôn!" — The sword fights on the LORD's behalf and on Gideon's (m1); "của" (of) keeps the cry short, as a battle cry is, and echoes "the sword of Gideon" in the dream of verse 14.
- [ ] [JDG 9:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/9/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "các hào trưởng Si-chem" (L); "các nhà lãnh đạo Si-chem" (B, R) — The Hebrew calls them the "lords" (owners) of Shechem, the leading landholders who governed the city (m1); "hào trưởng" is the Vietnamese word for such local men of standing, and Balanced and Readable say "leaders", as the English does.
- [ ] [JDG 9:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/9/#v7) L/B/R · register · Jotham speaks to the lords as "các ông" and of himself as "tôi" — The youngest son, a fugitive, speaks to the city's leaders with formal respect, which makes the rebuke of verses 16-20 sharper.
- [ ] [JDG 9:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/9/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "một tà linh" — A spirit that brings harm and strife (m1); "tà linh" (evil spirit) follows the English "an evil spirit", and the meaning decision says it may be a spirit of discord.
- [ ] [JDG 10:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/10/#v16) L · word_choice · "linh hồn Ngài không chịu nổi" at Literal — The Hebrew says "his soul became short", meaning he could no longer bear their suffering (m2); Vietnamese has no such idiom, so Literal keeps "soul" and says the sense.
- [ ] [JDG 10:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/10/#v18) R · word_choice · "thủ lĩnh" at Readable for "head" — The leader of the whole region (m3), as the English Readable says "chief"; it is the same word Readable uses for the judges, since Jephthah becomes one in chapter 11.
- [ ] [JDG 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/11/#v2) L/B/R · register · The half-brothers call Jephthah "anh" and themselves "chúng tôi" — Vietnamese must show who is older. The story lets the wife's sons grow up after Jephthah was born, so he is treated as the elder brother; "chúng tôi" (not "chúng ta") shuts him out of "our father's house", as the brothers mean to.
- [ ] [JDG 11:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/11/#v12) L/B/R · register · Jephthah, through his messengers, calls himself "ta" and the king "ông" — A leader speaking to a foreign king in a dispute: "ta" keeps his standing as Israel's spokesman, and "ông" stays civil, as the letter argues its case rather than insulting the king. "ngươi" would sound like a master to a servant.
- [ ] [JDG 11:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/11/#v27) L/B/R · register · Jephthah's message switches to "tôi" and "ông" — Verses 15–27 are Jephthah's own words carried by the messengers; after the opening "ta" of verse 12 the long argument settles into the plainer "tôi", which the English cannot show. A reviewer may prefer "ta" throughout.
- [ ] [JDG 12:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/12/#v3) L · word_choice · "liều mạng sống mình trong lòng bàn tay" at Literal — Literal keeps the picture of holding one's life in one's palm, joined to the Vietnamese "liều mạng" so that the sense (I risked my life) is clear; Balanced and Readable say only "liều mạng".
- [ ] [JDG 12:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/12/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "Si-bô-lết" and "Xi-bô-lết" — Vietnamese spelling keeps the two sounds apart with "s" and "x", which many speakers still pronounce differently, so the reader can see the difference the test turned on.
- [ ] [JDG 13:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/13/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "rượu mạnh" (L), "thức uống có men" (B, R) for *shekhar* — The word covers every drink that makes a person drunk besides wine (meaning decision). Vietnamese "rượu mạnh" today means spirits, which ancient Israel did not distil, so Balanced and Readable describe it; Literal keeps the traditional word.
- [ ] [JDG 15:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/15/#v2) L/B/R · register · The father-in-law calls Samson "con" and himself "ta"; Samson speaks of himself as "tôi" — A father-in-law speaks to his son-in-law as to a son. Samson's "tôi" in verse 1 keeps him at a distance, which suits the quarrel; a reviewer may prefer "con" for Samson too.
- [ ] [JDG 15:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/15/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "lừa" and "đống" without the Hebrew play on words — In Hebrew "donkey" and "heap" sound the same (meaning decision). Vietnamese has no pair of words that does this, so the saying keeps the sense and its repeated lines, and the wordplay is left to the meaning decision.
- [ ] [JDG 17:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jdg/17/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "đền thờ thần" (L), "miếu thờ" (B, R) for "house of God" — The Hebrew can be read "house of God" or "house of gods"; it is a private shrine (meaning decision). "miếu" names a small private place of worship; Literal keeps "đền thờ" with the lowercase "thần", which leaves the reading open.

## Ru-tơ (9)

- [ ] [RUT 1:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/1/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "chị dâu" for "sister-in-law" — Vietnamese must say whether a sister-in-law is older or younger; Orpah is named first in verse 4, so she is taken as the elder. The Hebrew does not say.
- [ ] [RUT 1:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/1/#v21) L/B/R · register · Naomi calls the women of Bethlehem "các chị" and herself "tôi" — Women of her own town whom she has known; "các chị" is warm but not familial, as their surprised question in verse 19 suggests.
- [ ] [RUT 2:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/2/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "lưu ý / lưu tâm đến" and "người ngoại quốc", without the Hebrew wordplay — In Hebrew "take notice of" and "foreigner" sound alike (meaning decision); Vietnamese cannot keep the play, so it keeps the plain sense.
- [ ] [RUT 3:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/3/#v9) L · register · Boaz, startled in the dark, says "ngươi" at Literal; Balanced and Readable "Ai đó?" — He does not yet know who is there; a bare "Who are you?" needs a pronoun in Vietnamese, and "ngươi" is the neutral one for an unknown person at Literal. The other levels avoid the pronoun.
- [ ] [RUT 3:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/3/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "lòng nhân từ" for Ruth's *hesed* — The Joshua–Ruth terms note gives "lòng nhân từ" for *hesed* between people. At 1:8 the same word was given as "tình yêu thương thành tín" because one sentence joins the LORD's love and the daughters-in-law's; here it is Ruth's alone. A reviewer may prefer one word for both places.
