- [ ] [RUT 4:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/4/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ông Mỗ" for "Mr. So-and-so" — The Hebrew uses a stand-in instead of the man's name (meaning decision); Vietnamese "ông Mỗ" is the ordinary placeholder for an unnamed man, as the English "Mr. So-and-so" is.
- [ ] [RUT 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/4/#v3) L/B/R · register · Boaz and the nearer redeemer say "anh" and "tôi" to each other — Two kinsmen of about the same standing, speaking in public before the elders; "anh em chúng ta" at Literal keeps the Hebrew "our brother" for a kinsman.
- [ ] [RUT 4:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/4/#v9) B/R · register · "quý vị" for "you" to the elders and all the people — A formal address to a public gathering of men and women; Literal keeps the plainer "các ông", as he spoke to the elders in verse 2.
- [ ] [RUT 4:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rut/4/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-ram" for Ram — The Hebrew name is Ram; Matthew 1:3–4 in Vietnamese gives "A-ram" from the Greek. The terms note's rule is followed so the two genealogies agree, but a reviewer may prefer "Ram".

## 2 Sa-mu-ên (27)

- [ ] [2SA 1:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/1/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "cơn quặn thắt" (L); "cơn đau đớn" (B); "đau đớn quá" (R) — The Hebrew word is found only here (meaning decision); Vietnamese names a gripping pain at every level, the sense the English follows.
- [ ] [2SA 1:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/1/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "nó", "hắn" for the Amalekite once he is condemned — Vietnamese marks contempt in its pronouns; once David has judged him, the narration and David's order speak of him as "hắn" and "nó", while his slayer stays "người ấy".
- [ ] [2SA 2:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/2/#v27) L · word_choice · "từ sáng nay quân lính đã rút lui" (L) — Literal keeps the Hebrew "from the morning" and its sense as Literal English reads it; Balanced and Readable follow the English Balanced and Readable, which make the pursuit go on until the morning (meaning decision).
- [ ] [2SA 3:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/3/#v7) L/B/R · register · Ish-bosheth says "ta" and "ngươi" to Abner; Abner answers with "tôi" and "ông" — As the Samuel terms note sets for a king; Abner, the older kinsman who made him king, answers in anger without any royal form, which the Vietnamese pronouns show.
- [ ] [2SA 4:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/4/#v6) L/B/R · supplied_words · "[như] để lấy lúa mì" (L); "như thể đến lấy lúa mì" (B, R); Baanah is Rechab's "em" — The wheat was probably a pretext (meaning decision), so Literal brackets "như". Vietnamese must choose older or younger brother; Rechab is named first in verses 5-6, so Baanah is taken as the younger, though verse 2 names him first.
- [ ] [2SA 5:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/5/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "đường dẫn nước" (L, B); "đường hầm dẫn nước" (R) — A channel or shaft bringing water into the city (meaning decision); the word is uncertain, and Vietnamese keeps it general.
- [ ] [2SA 5:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/5/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây bóp-ca" — The tree is not certainly known (meaning decision); Vietnamese transliterates the Hebrew name, as the English "balsam" is only a guess.
- [ ] [2SA 6:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/6/#v21) L/B/R · register · David says "ta" and "bà" to Michal in this quarrel — The Genesis note sets "em" and "anh" between husband and wife at Balanced and Readable; here David answers his wife's contempt as king, and the cold "ta" and "bà" carry the break between them that verse 23 seals.
- [ ] [2SA 8:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/8/#v3) L · word_choice · "khôi phục quyền lực mình" (L) — Literal gives the sense "take back his power" rather than "his hand", since the Hebrew may also mean setting up a monument again (meaning decision) and Vietnamese "tay" would not carry either.
- [ ] [2SA 9:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/9/#v7) L/B/R · register · David says "ta" and "con" to Mephibosheth; Mephibosheth says "đầy tớ của vua" (L), "đầy tớ của bệ hạ" (B), "tôi" (R) — The king speaks to the son of his covenant brother as an older man to a younger, warmly but as king; Mephibosheth answers in the subjects' forms of the Samuel terms note.
- [ ] [2SA 10:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/10/#v11) L/B/R · register · Joab says "anh" for himself and "em" to Abishai — Brothers, as the Genesis note sets: the older (Joab, named first among Zeruiah's sons) says "anh" and calls the younger "em".
- [ ] [2SA 13:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/13/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" for Tamar, Absalom's sister — Vietnamese must say whether a sister is older or younger; nothing in the Hebrew says, and Absalom treats her as the one he protects (verse 20), so she is "em gái" to him. Amnon, the firstborn, calls her "em" and himself "anh"; Tamar calls him "anh".
- [ ] [2SA 13:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/13/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "áo dài [đến] cổ tay [và cổ chân]" (L); "áo dài tay" (B); "áo đặc biệt có tay dài" (R) — The same wording as Joseph's robe in the Vietnamese Genesis 37:3 should be checked by a reviewer so readers see the link; "áo nhiều màu" follows an old guess the meaning decision sets aside.
- [ ] [2SA 14:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/14/#v31) L/B/R · register · Joab and Absalom say "anh" and "tôi" to each other — Joab is Absalom's cousin and an older man; they meet here as equals in anger, so "anh" and "tôi" both ways.
- [ ] [2SA 15:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/15/#v25) L/B/R · register · the king says "ông" to Zadok the priest, and to Hushai (verses 33-36) — The Samuel terms note gives "ta" and "ngươi" for the king to his subjects; to a priest and to an older friend Vietnamese keeps "ông", while the king still says "ta".
- [ ] [2SA 16:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/16/#v17) L/B/R · register · Absalom, now king, says "ông" to the older Hushai; Hushai says "vua" / "bệ hạ" / "đức vua" and "tôi" — Absalom courts David's old friend; he mocks him, but still speaks to an elder politely.
- [ ] [2SA 17:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/17/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-ma-sa", "Gít-ra", "A-bi-ga-in", "Na-hách"; Abigail is "em gái" of Zeruiah — Names as the Samuel terms note and the usual Vietnamese forms; Vietnamese must say older or younger sister, and Zeruiah, mother of David's oldest commanders, is taken as the older.
- [ ] [2SA 18:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/18/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · Abishai is "em Giô-áp" — Vietnamese must say which brother is older; Joab is always named first and leads, so Abishai is taken as the younger.
- [ ] [2SA 18:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/18/#v9) L/B · word_choice · "cây sồi" (L, B); "cái cây lớn" (R) for the terebinth — Vietnamese has no common name for the terebinth; "cây sồi" names a big spreading tree readers know, and Readable says only "a big tree", as the English does.
- [ ] [2SA 19:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/19/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "người được xức dầu của CHÚA" (L, B); "vị vua CHÚA đã xức dầu" (R) — The Samuel terms note sets "vị vua được CHÚA xức dầu" for Balanced; here Balanced follows the English Balanced, which keeps "the LORD's anointed", and Readable takes the note's Readable form.
- [ ] [2SA 20:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/20/#v9) L/B/R · register · "em" for Joab's "my brother" to Amasa — Joab and Amasa were cousins (meaning decision); Joab, long David's commander, is taken as the elder and calls Amasa "em", which makes the greeting warm before the blow.
- [ ] [2SA 20:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/20/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-bên Bết-Ma-a-ca"; "người Bê-rít" — The usual Vietnamese forms of the town and the clan, kept through the chapter.
- [ ] [2SA 21:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/21/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "treo ... lên" (L); "xử tử và treo ... lên" (B); "xử tử ... và treo xác ... lên cột" (R) — The rare verb means to put to death and display the bodies (meaning decision); Literal keeps one verb, Balanced and Readable spell out both steps as the English does.
- [ ] [2SA 23:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/23/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "lời sấm" (L, B); "sứ điệp" (R) for the oracle; the poem set in lines — The word for a solemn word spoken under God's inspiration (meaning decision); Readable uses a plainer word. David's last words are set in lines, as the English.
- [ ] [2SA 23:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/23/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "Giô-sép Ba-sê-bết", "người Tác-kê-môn", "A-đi-nô người Ết-nít" — The list of David's warriors keeps the usual Vietnamese forms of each name; the names in verses 8-39 follow the same pattern and are kept through Samuel and Chronicles.
- [ ] [2SA 23:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/23/#v36) L/B/R · word_choice · "người Ga-đít" for the Gadite — "người Gát" is used in these chapters for the Gittites, men of Gath; the tribe of Gad takes the older form "Ga-đít" so the two are not confused.
- [ ] [2SA 24:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2sa/24/#v14) L/B/R · register · David says "tôi" to Gad the prophet — To God's prophet the king speaks as an equal, not with the royal "ta".

## 1 Các Vua (10)

- [ ] [1KI 12:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/12/#v4) L/B/R · register · the assembly calls Rehoboam "ngài" and themselves "chúng tôi" — The tribes have not yet accepted Rehoboam as king and are negotiating on terms; "ngài" is respectful without the court's "bệ hạ", which his own courtiers use below.
- [ ] [1KI 12:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/12/#v6) L/B/R · register · Rehoboam says "các ông" to his father's advisers and "ta" for himself — The king speaks to the honoured older men of his father's court with "các ông"; to the young men of his own age he says "các ngươi" (verse 9).
- [ ] [1KI 12:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/12/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · numbers: words at Literal, figures with a dot at Balanced and Readable — Large numbers are written in figures (1.700, 20.000), as in 2 Samuel; Literal spells out the Hebrew's "a hundred and eighty thousand" so its wording can be followed.
- [ ] [1KI 13:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/13/#v21) B/R · register · God says "ngươi" at every level in this word of judgment — In a word of judgment God speaks to the disobedient prophet as "ngươi", as he does in Samuel when he judges (Eli); "con" would sound tender here.
- [ ] [1KI 13:30](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/13/#v30) L/B/R · idiom · "anh tôi ơi" for "my brother" in the lament — The cry of mourning needs a kin word in Vietnamese; "anh" honours the dead man and does not claim to know his age.
- [ ] [1KI 14:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/14/#v2) L/B/R · register · Jeroboam and his wife: "anh" and "em" — As the Genesis note sets for husband and wife, and as the terms note sets for Ahab and Jezebel; the king speaks to his wife as a husband, not as a ruler.
- [ ] [1KI 15:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/15/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "mẹ" kept for Maacah — The Hebrew says "his mother"; the meaning decision explains that she was probably his grandmother who stayed queen mother. Vietnamese must name the kin exactly, so the Hebrew's word is kept and the note carries the explanation.
- [ ] [1KI 18:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/18/#v17) L/B/R · register · Ahab calls Elijah "ông", even in anger — The terms note sets "ông" for the king speaking to a prophet; the hostility is carried by "kẻ làm rối loạn", not by a rude pronoun.
- [ ] [1KI 19:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/19/#v20) L/B/R · register · Elisha to Elijah: "ông" and "tôi"; Elijah to the young Elisha: "anh" and "tôi" — At the call the two are not yet master and disciple; Elisha uses the polite "ông", and the older prophet calls the young farmer "anh".
- [ ] [1KI 22:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ki/22/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "viên quan" for *saris* here — The terms note gives "hoạn quan" for *sarisim* at Literal; here the English Literal reads "official" for a single court officer, so all levels say "viên quan".

## 2 Các Vua (8)

- [ ] [2KI 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/11/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "chị của A-cha-xia" — Vietnamese must say whether a sister is older or younger; the Hebrew does not say, and "chị" is chosen because she acts as a grown woman with charge of the child (uncertain).
- [ ] [2KI 15:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/15/#v10) B/R · register · hắn for the usurper (B, R) — Vietnamese narration must choose a pronoun that judges: the murderer who seizes the throne is "hắn" at Balanced and Readable, the murdered king "vua" or "ông". Literal keeps the neutral "ông".
- [ ] [2KI 18:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/18/#v19) L/B/R · register · the king of Assyria to Hezekiah: ngươi; "phán" for the great king — The message speaks with an emperor's contempt, so Hezekiah is "ngươi"; the officer uses the royal "phán" for his master, the same verb as in "CHÚA phán thế này", which sharpens the boast.
- [ ] [2KI 18:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/18/#v22) L/B/R · setting_alternative · {{deity_pronoun:ngài|Ngài}} for the LORD in the Assyrian's mouth — Here the speaker names the LORD as Israel's God whom the people trust, so the pronoun follows the name and takes the span, as the English does; where foreigners mean a local god (17:26) Vietnamese writes "thần".
- [ ] [2KI 20:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/20/#v1) L/B/R · register · Isaiah to Hezekiah: vua — A prophet calls the king "vua", as the terms note sets; the message is from the LORD but Isaiah relays it in his own respectful voice, so the king is not "ngươi" here.
- [ ] [2KI 21:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/21/#v11) L/B/R · register · hắn for Manasseh in the LORD's judgment — In the LORD's sentence the condemned king is "hắn", which carries the judgment without the contempt of "nó".
- [ ] [2KI 23:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/23/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · thẩm phán — The judges keep the word used in the Vietnamese Samuel, "thẩm phán"; the Joshua–Ruth drafts propose "quan xét", which a reviewer may prefer.
- [ ] [2KI 25:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ki/25/#v24) L/B/R · register · Gedaliah to the commanders: các ông — Gedaliah, a governor speaking to army commanders who are not his subjects, uses the respectful "các ông".

## 1 Sử Ký (15)

- [ ] [1CH 2:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/2/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "chị em" (sisters, older or younger) — Vietnamese names a sister as older ("chị") or younger ("em"); the list does not say which, so the general "chị em" is used. 2 Samuel 17:25 calls Abigail Zeruiah's "em gái".
- [ ] [1CH 2:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/2/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "em" for Jada, Shammai's brother — Vietnamese must say older or younger; Shammai is named first in verse 28, so Jada is taken as the younger brother.
- [ ] [1CH 2:55](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/2/#v55) L/B/R · word_choice · "thầy thông giáo" for the scribes — The usual Vietnamese word for scribes, people skilled in writing; the meaning decision notes it may be a clan name.
- [ ] [1CH 3:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/3/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" for Tamar — As in 2 Samuel 13, Tamar is the younger sister ("em gái") of Absalom and Amnon.
- [ ] [1CH 3:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/3/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "Hích-kia", kept apart from King Hezekiah ("Ê-xê-chia") — The Hebrew is the shorter form of Hezekiah's name, borne here by a man of the post-exile line; a distinct form keeps him apart from the king of verse 13.
- [ ] [1CH 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/4/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" for the one daughter named after her brothers — Vietnamese names a sister as older or younger; a daughter named after her brothers is taken as the younger, as with Tamar (3:9).
- [ ] [1CH 5:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/5/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "người cai trị" (L, B); "người lãnh đạo" (R) for *nagid* — The Samuel terms give "người lãnh đạo" for *nagid*; here, where the word is set beside Judah's strength among his brothers, Literal and Balanced say "người cai trị", as the English says "ruler" at those levels, and Readable keeps "người lãnh đạo".
- [ ] [1CH 7:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/7/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" for "his sister" — Vietnamese must name a sister as older or younger; the text does not say, and the younger is taken, as with the other sisters of these lists.
- [ ] [1CH 7:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/7/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "em" for Sheresh, Peresh's brother — Vietnamese must say older or younger; Peresh is the son whose birth is told, so Sheresh is taken as the younger.
- [ ] [1CH 8:39](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/8/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "em" for Eshek, Azel's brother — Vietnamese must say older or younger; Azel's line is given first, so Eshek is taken as the younger.
- [ ] [1CH 10:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/10/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây sồi" for the terebinth — 1 Samuel 31:13 has a tamarisk ("cây thánh liễu" in the Vietnamese Samuel); Chronicles has another tree word, and the Vietnamese keeps the difference, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CH 22:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/22/#v18) L/B/R · register · The king says "ta" and "các ông" to the leaders of Israel — David speaks to the nation's leaders with the respect he gives the Levite heads in 15:12; a reviewer may prefer the king's "các ngươi" of the Samuel note.
- [ ] [1CH 26:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/26/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "Giô-ên em ông" — Joel is named after Zetham here and in 23:8, so he is taken as the younger brother; Vietnamese has to choose.
- [ ] [1CH 27:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/27/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "chú của Đa-vít" — The Hebrew word is a father's brother; Vietnamese must say whether he was younger ("chú") or older ("bác") than David's father, and the younger is assumed.
- [ ] [1CH 28:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ch/28/#v2) L/B/R · register · David says "tôi" and calls the assembly "anh em" — David calls them "my brothers"; Vietnamese "anh em" with "tôi" keeps that closeness, as he speaks to the assembly in 13:2.

## 2 Sử Ký (12)

- [ ] [2CH 2:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/2/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "Hu-ram"; Solomon and Huram say "ông" and "tôi" — Chronicles spells the king's name Huram (m1), and Vietnamese keeps that spelling, as the terms note does. The two kings speak as equals with "ông" and "tôi", as Solomon and Hiram do in 1 Kings 5 (uncertain).
- [ ] [2CH 2:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/2/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "gỗ đàn hương" for algum — The wood cannot be identified with certainty (m1); Vietnamese keeps the choice of 1 Kings 10:11-12 for the same tree (uncertain).
- [ ] [2CH 10:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/10/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · the wording of 1 Kings 12:1-19 through the chapter — 2 Chronicles 10 retells 1 Kings 12:1-19 almost word for word; the Vietnamese of Kings is kept at each level, with its address forms: the people say "ngài" to Rehoboam, the elders "vua" (L), "bệ hạ" (B), "đức vua" (R); Rehoboam says "các ông" to the elders and "các ngươi" to the young men …
- [ ] [2CH 11:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/11/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "dê quỷ" — Beings worshiped in the form of goats (m1); the word is the one for male goats, so Vietnamese joins "dê" with "quỷ" to show they were worshiped as spirits.
- [ ] [2CH 13:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/13/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "Mi-ca-gia" for the queen mother — The Hebrew text names her Micaiah (m1), where 1 Kings 15:2 and 2 Chronicles 11:20 have Maacah. Vietnamese keeps the name the verse gives, in a form kept apart from the prophet Mi-chê of 1 Kings 22 (uncertain).
- [ ] [2CH 15:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/15/#v2) L/B/R · register · the prophet says "tôi" and "các người" to the king and people — The "you" is plural, king and people together (m4). As Elijah to the people in 1 Kings 18, the prophet uses the plain "các người", not the commanding "các ngươi" of God or a king.
- [ ] [2CH 19:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/19/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "thẩm phán" for the judges — The word of the Samuel and Kings drafts; the Joshua-Ruth drafts propose "quan xét", so a reviewer should settle one (uncertain).
- [ ] [2CH 30:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/30/#v19) B/R · setting_alternative · "mình" with no gender span — his"
- [ ] [2CH 31:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/31/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "Si-mê-i em ông" — Vietnamese must say who is older; Shimei is "second" to Conaniah, so he is taken as the younger brother. Uncertain: the Hebrew does not say.
- [ ] [2CH 32:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/32/#v9) B/R · register · the narrator calls Sennacherib "hắn" at Balanced and Readable — As the 2 Kings drafts do for an enemy king who mocks God; Literal and the first mention keep his title.
- [ ] [2CH 32:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/32/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "phán thế này" (L); "phán như vầy" (B); "Đây là lời ..." (R) for the Assyrian king — The 2 Kings drafts give the great king's message with "phán", echoing the prophets' formula as the Hebrew does (uncertain there too); "các ngươi" for the people.
- [ ] [2CH 36:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ch/36/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "nói thế này" (L); "nói như vầy" (B); "Đây là lời ..." (R) for Cyrus — Vietnamese keeps "phán" for God (and, in 32:10, for the Assyrian king's claim to divine authority); Cyrus honors the LORD and is given the plain verb. A reviewer may prefer "phán" for a royal edict.

## Ê-xơ-tê (2)

- [ ] [EST 2:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/est/2/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây" (L); "giá treo cổ" (B); "cây cột" (R) — As the English levels: "giá treo cổ" is the Esther word for Haman's gallows (5:14; 7:9-10), but the Hebrew word is wood, a tree or a pole, and the death may be impaling or the hanging up of a body (meaning decision m1).
- [ ] [EST 5:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/est/5/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · giá treo cổ — The Hebrew says 'tree' or 'wood'; as meaning decision m1 says, the execution may have been impaling or hanging up a body. 'Giá treo cổ' keeps the picture of a tall structure for execution, as the English 'gallows' does.

## Gióp (9)

- [ ] [JOB 7:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/7/#v7) L/B/R · register · Job, now speaking to God, calls him "Chúa" and himself "con" (not "tôi" as with his friends) — Vietnamese must name the relation in every pronoun; the terms note gives Job's prayers the psalmists' "Chúa" / "con". His words here are bitter, and "tôi" would keep more of their edge, so a reviewer should weigh it.
- [ ] [JOB 9:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/9/#v28) L/B/R · register · Job turns to God: "Chúa" for "you" and "con" for himself (9:28-31), then "tôi" again from 9:32 — Job speaks to God directly here for the first time in the speech (m1). He takes the address of the Psalms' laments, "Chúa" and "con"; when he speaks about God again (9:32-35) he says "tôi". "Tôi" before God is possible in so bitter a speech, so the choice is marked uncertain.
- [ ] [JOB 13:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/13/#v20) L/B/R · register · Job to God: "Chúa" for "you" and "con" for himself — Job now speaks to God directly (m1). He addresses God as "Chúa" and calls himself "con", as the laments of the Psalms do; his tone is bitter, and "tôi" would also be possible.
- [ ] [JOB 16:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/16/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "vị" for the one who testifies (verses 19 and 21) — The meaning decision leaves open whether the witness is God himself or a heavenly being. "Đấng" would name him as God; "người" would make him an ordinary man. "vị" is respectful without deciding, and takes no deity_pronoun span.
- [ ] [JOB 17:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/17/#v3) L/B/R · register · Job speaks to God as "Chúa" and "Ngài" and calls himself "con" in verses 3-4, against "tôi" in the rest of the speech — As in the laments of the Psalms, a speaker praying to God calls himself "con". Job's speech is bitter, and "tôi" would keep his voice the same throughout; a reviewer should choose.
- [ ] [JOB 19:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/19/#v25) L/B/R · register · "tôi" for Job, and "Ngài" with the span for the Redeemer — Job is speaking to his friends here, not to God, so he says "tôi"; the Redeemer is "Ngài" because the most likely reading is that he is God (meaning decision m2).
- [ ] [JOB 33:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/33/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng ấy" ... "nói", and no pronoun for the one who found the ransom — The Hebrew does not say whether God or the angel is gracious and speaks (m1). "Đấng ấy" is used of God and of heavenly beings, and Vietnamese can leave the subject of "đã tìm được giá chuộc" unspoken, so the line stays open as the English does.
- [ ] [JOB 40:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/40/#v4) L/B/R · register · Job to God: "Chúa" for "you" and "con" for himself — As the Psalms' laments. Here Job is humbled, and "con" fits; "tôi" stays the alternative for his bitter speeches earlier in the book.
- [ ] [JOB 42:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/job/42/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "rút lại [lời mình] ... ăn năn" — Follows m1 and m2: Job takes back what he said and repents; the alternatives are in the meaning decisions.

## Thi Thiên (110)

- [ ] [PSA 2:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/2/#v7) L/B/R · setting_alternative · the king says "tôi" with no setting span; God calls him "con", with the deity_pronoun setting — The English lets the reader capitalise the king's pronouns, as the meaning decision explains. In Vietnamese the king would say "ta" only to his subjects, and it would blur his voice with God's "Ta" in the same verse, so he says the plain "tôi", which has no respectful capital form. God's word to …
- [ ] [PSA 4:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/4/#v7) L/B/R · register · "chúng con" for "us" when the psalmist prays to God for the people — The psalmist says "tôi" for himself, but when he prays with and for the people he speaks to God as a believer does, "chúng con"; "chúng ta" stays for the people speaking among themselves, which Vietnamese has to tell apart.
- [ ] [PSA 5:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/5/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "ống sáo" for the flutes — The English translates the word as flutes, so Vietnamese does too; the meaning decision notes that it may instead be the name of a tune.
- [ ] [PSA 6:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/6/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-mi-nít" kept as a name (L, B); "cung thứ tám" (R) — The word means "the eighth", but no one knows what in the music it named (see the meaning decision). Literal and Balanced keep it as a name, as the English does; Readable gives the plain meaning of the word in quotation marks, without claiming to know more. "quãng tám" (octave) would claim more …
- [ ] [PSA 7:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/7/#v1) L/B · word_choice · "bài Si-ga-giôn" (L, B); "Bài ca" (R) — No one knows what kind of song a shiggaion was (see the meaning decision), so Literal and Balanced keep the word as a name, in the form the terms note sets; Readable says only that it is a song, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 8:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/8/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ghít-tít" kept as a name, with "theo [điệu]" — The word may name an instrument or a tune, perhaps one from the town of Gath (see the meaning decision); since no one knows, every level keeps it as a name, as the English does. "điệu" (tune) is the most neutral word to hang it on, bracketed at Literal.
- [ ] [PSA 9:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/9/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Mút-láp-ben" kept as a name, "theo [điệu]" — The words seem to mean "death of the son", probably the name of a tune (see the meaning decision); like the English, every level keeps the name rather than translating words whose use here is unclear.
- [ ] [PSA 9:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/9/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "Hi-gai-ôn" kept as a word, beside "Sê-la" — Higgaion is a musical direction whose exact sense is unknown (see the meaning decision); like Selah, it is kept as a word at every level.
- [ ] [PSA 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/10/#v13) L/B · register · "Ngài" for the "you" the wicked man says to God — Even a man who scorns God speaks of him with the ordinary word for God; Vietnamese has no neutral "you" here, and anything ruder would add a meaning the Hebrew does not mark.
- [ ] [PSA 11:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/11/#v1) L/B/R · register · "các người" for the advisers the psalmist answers — The psalmist rejects their advice; "các người" is the plain word for a group spoken to with some distance, as at 4:3. "các bạn" would sound too warm for people whose counsel he refuses.
- [ ] [PSA 16:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/16/#v1) L/B · word_choice · "bài Mích-tam" (L, B); "Bài ca" (R) — No one knows what a miktam was (see the meaning decision); Literal and Balanced keep the word as a name, in the form the terms note sets, and Readable calls it a song, as the English Readable does. "bài thơ vàng" (golden poem) rests on a guess from the word's sound.
- [ ] [PSA 16:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/16/#v2) L/B/R · register · "mình" for "you" when the psalmist speaks to his own soul — The psalmist talks to himself (see the meaning decision). "mình" is the word Vietnamese uses when one speaks to oneself; "ngươi", the older form in such verses, is archaic. "thưa" is the respectful verb for speaking to God.
- [ ] [PSA 18:49](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/18/#v49) B/R · register · "Ngài" for both "he" and "you" in this verse — The English moves from "he" to "you" in mid-verse (see the meaning decision); Vietnamese uses "Ngài" for both, so the turn to speak to God is not marked by the pronoun, as in Psalm 23:4.
- [ ] [PSA 20:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/20/#v2) L/B/R · register · "vua" (the king) for "you" through the psalm, and "chúng tôi" for the people — The people are praying for their king (see the meaning decision). Vietnamese addresses a superior by his title, and "vua" is plain and respectful; "bệ hạ" belongs to court speech, and "ngài" would be confused with the pronoun for God.
- [ ] [PSA 21:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/21/#v9) L/B/R · register · "vua" for "you" when the people turn to speak to the king (verses 9-13), as in Psalm 20 — The voice turns from God to the king (see the meaning decision). Vietnamese speaks to the king by his title, as the people do in Psalm 20; Readable adds "Thưa vua" where the English Readable adds "King,".
- [ ] [PSA 22:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/22/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-giê-lết Ha-sa-ha" kept as a name (L, B); "Con Nai Cái Lúc Rạng Đông" (R) — The words mean "the doe of the dawn", probably the name of a tune (see the meaning decision). As in the English, Literal and Balanced keep the name and Readable translates it as a tune's title.
- [ ] [PSA 22:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/22/#v27) L/B/R · register · "các người" (L, B) and "anh chị em" (R) for the "you" the psalmist blesses — The psalmist turns to bless the poor who share the meal with him; "các người" is the plain plural "you", and Readable, which makes the greeting a quotation, has him call them "anh chị em", as one speaks to a gathering of one's own people.
- [ ] [PSA 23:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/23/#v4) L/B/R · register · "Ngài" for "you" when the singer speaks to God, the same word as for "he" — The style guide has the psalmist speak to God as "Ngài" or "Chúa"; "Ngài" keeps the deity_pronoun setting the English has here. But "Ngài" serves for both "he" and "you", so the turn from speaking about God to speaking to him, which the meaning decision points out, shows less than in Hebrew. A …
- [ ] [PSA 24:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/24/#v4) L · register · "linh hồn Ta" (my soul), the LORD speaking of himself, at Literal — The Hebrew, as handed down, has the LORD say "my soul" here (see the meaning decision), so Literal keeps God's "Ta" with the setting; Balanced and Readable explain it as swearing falsely by God's own life, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 38:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/38/#v1) R · supplied_words · "Để nhắc nhớ trước mặt Đức Chúa Trời" (to call to mind before God) at Readable — The words mean to call something to mind before God (see the meaning decision); Vietnamese "để nhắc nhớ" alone could mean a note to oneself, so Readable names before whom.
- [ ] [PSA 42:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/42/#v4) L/B/R · register · the mockers say "ngươi" (L) and "anh" (B, R) for "your" — The question is a mocking one (see the meaning decision); Literal uses the old contemptuous "ngươi", while Balanced and Readable use "anh", how people speak to a man as an equal, which keeps the scorn in the question itself.
- [ ] [PSA 45:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/45/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sô-sa-nim" kept as a name (L, B), "Theo điệu 'Hoa huệ'" (R); "bài Mát-kin" (L, B), "Bài ca để dạy dỗ và giúp hiểu biết" (R) — Both words are uncertain (see the meaning decisions). Literal and Balanced keep them as names, as the English does; Readable gives the likely sense, as the English Readable does, with the same uncertainty.
- [ ] [PSA 45:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/45/#v2) L · word_choice · "sôi trào" (bubbles over) — The rare verb seems to mean bubbling up like boiling water (see the meaning decision); "sôi trào" keeps that picture, and Balanced and Readable say it more plainly.
- [ ] [PSA 45:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/45/#v3) L/B/R · register · "ngài" for "you" when the poet speaks to the king, with the deity_pronoun setting as in the English — "ngài" is the ordinary respectful word for addressing a king, so the lowercase default reads naturally, and the capital alternative follows the English setting for readers who hear the king as the Messiah (see the English decision and Hebrews 1:8-9).
- [ ] [PSA 45:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/45/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "hỡi Đức Chúa Trời" (L, B), "Lạy Đức Chúa Trời" (R) for the one addressed — The Hebrew reads most simply as addressing the king as God (see the meaning decision, and Hebrews 1:8-9), so the address is kept. "hỡi" is the plain call in a poem; Readable uses "Lạy", the word of prayer, as for God elsewhere.
- [ ] [PSA 46:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/46/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-la-mốt" kept as a name (L, B); "Cho giọng cao" (R) — The word means "young women" and probably names high voices, but this is not certain (see the meaning decision). Literal and Balanced keep it as a name; Readable gives the likely sense, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 46:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/46/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la" kept at the end of the verse at every level — No one now knows what the word means; it is probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision). Vietnamese keeps it as a name, as the English keeps "Selah".
- [ ] [PSA 47:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/47/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la" kept at the end of the verse at every level — The word's meaning is no longer known; it is probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision). Vietnamese keeps it as a name, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 47:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/47/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "bài Mát-kin" (L); "thi thiên khéo léo" (B); "bài ca ngợi khen thật hay" (R) — Here the word stands in the poem itself, not in a title, so Balanced and Readable translate its likely sense, a skillful song, as the English does; the sense is uncertain (see the meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 47:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/47/#v10) L · supplied_words · "[làm] dân của Đức Chúa Trời" — The Hebrew sets the two phrases side by side; the nobles most likely become God's people (see the meaning decision), and the bracketed "làm" (as, to be) says so.
- [ ] [PSA 48:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/48/#v3) L/B · word_choice · "Xa-phôn" kept as a name (L, B); "núi thật của Đức Chúa Trời" (R) — Zaphon was the name of a mountain held to be the home of the gods; the psalm says Zion is the true one (see the meaning decision). Readable gives that sense, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 48:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/48/#v8) L/B/R · register · "Ngài" for "you" as the psalm turns to speak to God — The psalm turns here from speaking about God to speaking to him (see the meaning decision). As in Psalm 23:4, "Ngài" serves for "you", with the deity_pronoun setting.
- [ ] [PSA 48:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/48/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la" kept at the end of the verse — The word's meaning is no longer known; it is probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 49:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/49/#v6) L · supplied_words · "những kẻ bám gót tôi" for "my heels" — "The iniquity of my heels" most likely means the wrong done by those at my heels (see the meaning decision); "gót chân tôi" alone would make no sense in Vietnamese.
- [ ] [PSA 49:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/49/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la" kept at the end of the verse — The word's meaning is no longer known; it is probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 49:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/49/#v19) L/B/R · register · "anh" for the "you" the teacher turns to — The teacher suddenly speaks to the rich man himself (see the meaning decision); "anh" is the plain word for addressing a grown man as an equal, with a touch of challenge that "bạn" lacks.
- [ ] [PSA 50:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/50/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la" kept at the end of the verse — The word's meaning is no longer known; it is probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 50:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/50/#v7) L/B/R · register · "các con" for God's people addressed (7-15) — The Hebrew "you" is Israel as one person. God speaks to his own people as a father would, even while he brings a charge; "các con" keeps that relation, where "ngươi" would sound archaic and cold.
- [ ] [PSA 50:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/50/#v16) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" for the wicked one addressed (16-22) — God turns from his people to the wicked, who claim his covenant but break it. The older "ngươi" marks the distance of a judge from the accused, against "các con" in verses 7-15; the Hebrew "you" is singular here too.
- [ ] [PSA 51:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/51/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây kinh giới" for hyssop — Hyssop was a small bushy herb used to sprinkle water or blood in cleansing rites (see the meaning decision). "kinh giới" names a related herb Vietnamese readers know and is the name used for it in Vietnamese Bibles; the exact plant is not certain.
- [ ] [PSA 52:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/52/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "bài Mát-kin" (L, B); "Bài ca ... để dạy dỗ và giúp hiểu biết" (R) — The word probably means a skillful or teaching song, but this is uncertain (see the meaning decision). Literal and Balanced keep it as a name; Readable explains it, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 52:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/52/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la" kept at the end of the verse's last line — The word's meaning is no longer known; it is probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 53:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/53/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ma-ha-lát" kept as a name at every level — No one knows what the word means (see the meaning decision); every level keeps it as a name, and Readable calls it a tune, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 53:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/53/#v5) L/B/R · register · "dân Ta", with God as the speaker of "my" — "My people" is most likely God speaking inside the psalm (see the meaning decision), so Vietnamese gives him "Ta", with the deity_pronoun setting as the English has.
- [ ] [PSA 53:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/53/#v6) L/B/R · register · "các ngươi" (L, B) for God's people addressed; "dân Ngài" (R) — The "you" is most likely God's people (see the meaning decision). "các ngươi" is the poem's solemn plural address; Readable names them as "dân Ngài" (his people), as the English Readable makes it explicit.
- [ ] [PSA 55:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/55/#v14) L/B/R · register · "anh" for the friend who betrayed him — The psalmist turns to speak to a friend who was his equal (see the meaning decision); "anh" is how one man speaks to a close friend of his own age, and it keeps the closeness that makes the betrayal hurt. "ngươi" is kept for enemies.
- [ ] [PSA 55:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/55/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "nó" for the friend once he is spoken about (21-22) — Speaking about the betrayer rather than to him, the psalmist uses "nó", which in anger lowers someone once close; "hắn" is kept for open enemies.
- [ ] [PSA 56:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/56/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · the tune's name kept in Vietnamese letters (L, B) and translated at Readable; "bài Mích-tam" (L, B), "Bài ca" (R) — Both words are uncertain (see the meaning decisions). Literal and Balanced keep them as names, as the English does; Readable gives the likely sense of the tune and calls the miktam a song.
- [ ] [PSA 58:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/58/#v2) L/B/R · register · "các ngươi" for the "gods" addressed — The psalm challenges the mighty ones who rule and judge (see the meaning decision); "các ngươi" is the solemn, distant plural for those under accusation, as God speaks to the wicked in Psalm 50.
- [ ] [PSA 60:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/60/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Su-san Ê-đút" kept as a name (L, B); "Hoa huệ của giao ước" (R) — The words mean something like "lily of the testimony" and probably name a tune (see the meaning decision); Readable translates as the English Readable does, with the same uncertainty.
- [ ] [PSA 60:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/60/#v14) L/B/R · register · "chúng ta" in the closing verse — After praying to God as "chúng tôi", the singer turns to encourage the people in the last line; "chúng ta" includes the hearers, as the turn from speaking to God to speaking about him suggests.
- [ ] [PSA 62:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/62/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "theo Giê-đu-thun" (L), "Theo cách của Giê-đu-thun" (B), "Theo lối hát của Giê-đu-thun" (R) — Jeduthun was a leader of the temple singers; what "on Jeduthun" means is not certain (see the meaning decision). Balanced and Readable read it as his style, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 67:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/67/#v2) L/B/R · register · "chúng ta" for "us" while God is spoken of (2, 7-8), "Ngài" for "you" when he is addressed (3-6) — The psalm moves between a blessing spoken about God among the people and praise spoken to him; Vietnamese marks this with "chúng ta", which includes the hearers, as in the priestly blessing it echoes (see the meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 68:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/68/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "Nguyện" (May ...) for the opening wish — The words echo the song sung when the ark set out (Numbers 10:35) and are most likely a wish (see the meaning decision), which "Nguyện" expresses.
- [ ] [PSA 68:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/68/#v14) L/B/R · register · "các ngươi" for the men addressed — The verse is hard and its "you" uncertain (see the meaning decision); "các ngươi" keeps the poem's formal plural address without deciding who is meant.
- [ ] [PSA 68:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/68/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "Chúa là CHÚA" (B, R) for the Lord GOD, as 69:7 and 140:8; Literal keeps the Hebrew order — Where the divine name stands beside Adonai, English writes "the Lord GOD" to avoid "the Lord LORD"; Vietnamese has the same problem with "Chúa CHÚA", so the name is written "ĐỨC CHÚA TRỜI" in capitals here, as French writes "le Seigneur DIEU". Literal keeps the Hebrew order. This form is not yet …
- [ ] [PSA 68:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/68/#v29) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" for Israel addressed by the poet — The Hebrew "your" is singular, Israel as one people (see the meaning decision); the poet addresses it with the poem's formal "ngươi", while God's own address to his people in verse 24 is "các con".
- [ ] [PSA 69:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/69/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "Chúa là CHÚA Vạn Quân" for Adonai YHWH of hosts, with "là" bracketed at Literal — Vietnamese says "Chúa" (Lord) for Adonai and "CHÚA" for the divine name, so the two side by side would read "Chúa CHÚA". Joining them with "là" ("the Lord, who is the LORD") keeps both words, and the divine_name setting still switches the second to "Gia-vê". "vạn quân" (of the countless armies) …
- [ ] [PSA 69:33](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/69/#v33) L/B/R · register · "các bạn" and "hỡi những người..." for the singer turning to his fellow worshipers — The singer speaks warmly to people who seek God. "các người" is cold, used for a rebuke; "anh em" leaves out the women. "các bạn" is the plain friendly plural "you" and needs no gender setting.
- [ ] [PSA 70:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/70/#v1) R · supplied_words · "Để nhắc lại trước mặt Đức Chúa Trời" (to call to mind before God) — The title asks God to remember the one who prays (see the meaning decision); "nhắc nhớ" alone would leave a new reader asking who is to remember what, so Readable says it.
- [ ] [PSA 71:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/71/#v4) L/B/R · register · "Đức Chúa Trời của tôi ơi" for "my God" called out — "ơi" after the name is the warm, personal way to call out to someone close; it suits "my God" and is still respectful in prayer. "Lạy" stands where the Hebrew calls God by name or title alone.
- [ ] [PSA 71:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/71/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "đàn hạc" (harp) and "đàn lia" (lyre) — Two stringed instruments (see the meaning decision); "đàn hạc" and "đàn lia" are the plain modern names for a harp and a lyre, where the older "đàn cầm", "đàn sắt" are Sino-Vietnamese literary words few readers can picture.
- [ ] [PSA 74:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/74/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "bài Mát-kin" (L, B); Readable says what it is for — Maskil is a word whose exact sense is uncertain, a song of insight or a skillful song (see the meaning decision). As the terms list sets it, Literal and Balanced keep it as a name; Readable explains it, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 74:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/74/#v1) L/B/R · register · the people praying say "chúng tôi" for "us" — One voice for the singer through the Psalms: "tôi", and "chúng tôi" when the people speak together, as the published Psalm 23 has it. "chúng con" (we your children) is also natural in prayer; a reviewer may prefer it where the whole people speak to God.
- [ ] [PSA 75:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/75/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đừng hủy diệt" (L); "Theo điệu \"Đừng Hủy Diệt\"" (B, R) — The words mean "do not destroy" and probably name a tune (see the meaning decision). As the English does, every level translates them, and Balanced and Readable say it is a tune; "thi thiên" and "bài ca" stand for the two Hebrew words for the song.
- [ ] [PSA 75:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/75/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la", kept as it is at the end of the verse — Selah is a word whose meaning is no longer known, probably a musical direction (see the meaning decision). Like the English, every level keeps it as a word, in the Protestant tradition's spelling, and does not guess at a translation.
- [ ] [PSA 77:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/77/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-la", kept at the end of the verse — As in Psalm 75:4: a word whose meaning is no longer known, probably a musical direction, kept as a word at every level.
- [ ] [PSA 78:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/78/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · God's torah: "sự dạy dỗ" (L), "lời dạy dỗ" (B), "lời dạy" (R), as Psalm 1:2 — The word is God's instruction, given to be taught (see the meaning decision). Literal keeps the familiar "luật pháp" (law); Balanced and Readable say "lời dạy dỗ" (teaching), which is what the word means here, as the English says "instruction" and "teaching".
- [ ] [PSA 81:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/81/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ghi-tít", kept as a name — The word may name an instrument or a tune, perhaps from Gath (see the meaning decision); since that is not certain, every level keeps it as a name, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 81:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/81/#v7) L/B/R · register · God says "Ta" and calls Israel "con" (you) — From here to verse 17 God speaks to Israel (see the meaning decision). He says "Ta", as the style guide sets for God speaking, and calls his people "con", as a father speaks to a child; "ngươi" is archaic and cold, and the Hebrew "you" is singular.
- [ ] [PSA 84:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/84/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "ghít-tít" kept as a name (L, B); "theo điệu Ghít-tít" (R) — The word's sense is lost: it may be an instrument or a tune from Gath (see the meaning decision). Vietnamese keeps it as a name, as the English does, and Readable calls it a tune, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 85:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/85/#v5) L/B/R · register · "chúng tôi" for "us" when the people pray — The singer says "tôi" through these psalms, as in the published Psalms, so the people praying together say "chúng tôi"; "chúng con" would be the language of a church prayer and change the voice from psalm to psalm.
- [ ] [PSA 87:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/87/#v3) L/B/R · register · "thành" (the city) for "you" when the city is spoken to — The singer speaks to Zion. "ngươi" is the old word for "you" in such a speech, but it is archaic and now sounds rude; Vietnamese can call the city by name or as "thành" and keep it as the one addressed. Used so through the psalm.
- [ ] [PSA 88:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/88/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "ma-ha-lát lê-a-nốt" kept as a name (L, B); "theo điệu dành cho lúc khổ đau" (R) — The words are probably a tune or a way of singing, and their sense is uncertain (see the meaning decision). Literal and Balanced keep them as a name; Readable gives the likely sense, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 88:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/88/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "vong linh" (L), "linh hồn người chết" (B), "người chết" (R) for the shades — The word pictures the dead as weak shadows (see the meaning decision). "bóng ma" means a ghost that haunts; "vong linh" is the spirit of one who has died, and the other levels say it more plainly.
- [ ] [PSA 91:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/91/#v3) L/B/R · register · "bạn" for the one spoken to, as Psalm 121:3 — The psalm speaks to anyone who trusts God (see the meaning decision), man or woman, young or old. "bạn" is the neutral modern word that fits anyone, as in the published Psalm 121.
- [ ] [PSA 95:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/95/#v7) L/B/R · register · "mọi người" for "you" when the singer turns to the people — The singer now speaks to the gathered worshipers. "mọi người" (everyone) is a warm, neutral way to address a crowd of men and women; "anh em" names only brothers, and "các người" would be a rebuke.
- [ ] [PSA 95:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/95/#v8) L/B/R · register · "các con" for "you" when God speaks to his people in verses 8-11 — God speaks from here to verse 11 (see the meaning decision). He warns his own people as a father warns his children; "các con" says that, where "các ngươi" is archaic and "các người" would treat them as strangers.
- [ ] [PSA 103:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/103/#v2) L/B · register · "mình" for "you" when the psalmist speaks to his own soul (verses 2-5) — The psalmist talks to himself. "mình" is how Vietnamese speaks to oneself, both "you" and "self"; "ngươi" is archaic. Readable says "tôi" (I), as the English Readable says "me".
- [ ] [PSA 103:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/103/#v5) L · word_choice · "đời mình" (your life) at Literal too — The Hebrew word, usually "ornament", here means the life or its desire (see the meaning decision); "your ornament" would mean nothing in Vietnamese, so Literal gives the sense with the other levels.
- [ ] [PSA 104:30](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/104/#v30) L/B · word_choice · "thần linh của Ngài" in lowercase (L, B); "hơi sống của Ngài" (R) — Here the word is God's life-giving breath (see the meaning decision), so it is written in lowercase, like the English "spirit"; Readable says "breath of life", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 105:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/105/#v11) L/B/R · register · God says "Ta" for himself, with the deity_pronoun setting in mid-sentence, and calls his people "con" (one) and "các con" (many); Readable says "các con" … — "Ta" is God's word for himself in the Psalms. "ngươi" is archaic; "con" is how God speaks to his people elsewhere in these drafts (Psalm 2:7). The Hebrew moves from "you" (one) to "your" (many), as the meaning decision explains; Literal and Balanced keep the shift, Readable smooths it.
- [ ] [PSA 106:33](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/106/#v33) L/B/R · word_choice · "Thần của Ngài" (his Spirit), capitalised — The meaning decision reads this as God's own Spirit, as the English capital does; "Thần" is the usual Vietnamese Old Testament word for God's Spirit, while "Đức Thánh Linh" would bring in the New Testament's title.
- [ ] [PSA 108:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/108/#v14) L/B/R · register · "chúng ta" (we, including the hearers) when the singer speaks about God to the people, after "chúng con" in the prayer of verses 12-13 — Verse 14 speaks of God as "he", no longer to him; Vietnamese marks the turn by moving from the "chúng con" of prayer to the inclusive "chúng ta".
- [ ] [PSA 110:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/110/#v1) L/B/R · setting_alternative · God calls the king "con", and the psalmist speaks to him as "ngài" (verses 2-3, 5), both with the deity_pronoun setting, as the English does for the king's … — The English lets the reader capitalise the king's pronouns, because the New Testament reads the psalm of Jesus (see the meaning decision). God speaks to the king as "con", as in Psalm 2:7, and the poet addresses a king as "ngài", as in Psalm 45:3; each takes the setting so that the choice stays …
- [ ] [PSA 114:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/114/#v5) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" for the sea, the river, the mountains and the earth spoken to in verses 5-7 — The poet teases the sea and the hills as if they were people; "ngươi", the old word of a speaker who stands above the one spoken to, fits that playful address to things, as in Vietnamese poetry.
- [ ] [PSA 115:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/115/#v14) L/B/R · register · "các bạn" for the worshipers blessed in verses 14-15 — The priest's blessing turns to the people as "you"; "các bạn" is the plain modern plural used for fellow worshipers in Psalm 69:33. "các ngươi" is archaic and "anh chị em" belongs to the church.
- [ ] [PSA 118:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/118/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "người đến" (the one who comes) in lowercase, and "được ban phước" for barukh said of a person — In the psalm the one who comes is the king or the pilgrim, and the priests bless all the worshipers (see the meaning decision); the Gospels' "Đấng" belongs to the crowd's use of the verse for Jesus. "chúc tụng" is kept for blessing God. The priests say "chúng tôi" and call the worshipers "các bạn", …
- [ ] [PSA 120:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/120/#v3) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" (L, B), "các ngươi" (R) for the liar spoken to — The psalmist turns on the liar with scorn. "ngươi" is the stern literary word of address to an enemy; "mày" would be coarse and "anh" friendly. Readable speaks to the liars as a group, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 120:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/120/#v4) L/B · word_choice · "cây đậu chổi" for the broom shrub (L, B); Readable describes the coals — The plant is a desert shrub whose wood makes coals that burn hot and long (see the meaning decision). "đậu chổi" names the broom plant plainly; Readable says only what matters, that the coals burn a long time, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 120:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/120/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "Mê-siếc" and "Kê-đa" for Meshech and Kedar — The names take the Protestant tradition's transliterations, which Vietnamese Bible readers know.
- [ ] [PSA 121:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/121/#v3) L/B/R · register · "bạn" for the one person spoken to in verses 3-8 — The voice turns to speak to one person, man or woman, young or old. "bạn" is the neutral modern word that fits anyone; "con" would make the speaker a parent or priest, "anh" fixes age and sex, and "ngươi" is archaic.
- [ ] [PSA 122:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/122/#v2) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" (L, B) for Jerusalem spoken to; Readable calls the city by name, "Giê-ru-sa-lem ơi" — The singer speaks to the city as to a person. "ngươi" is the literary word of address the Psalms use for a city or a people; it can sound stern to a modern ear, so Readable names the city instead and avoids the pronoun.
- [ ] [PSA 127:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/127/#v2) L/B · register · "các người" for "you" spoken to the hearers — The singer speaks to all who work early and late, not to an enemy, so the plain "các người" fits better than the stern "các ngươi"; Readable turns it into a general statement.
- [ ] [PSA 128:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/128/#v2) L/B/R · register · "bạn" for the one person spoken to, as in Psalm 121 — The song turns to speak to the one who fears the LORD (see the meaning decision). As in the published Psalm 121, "bạn" is the neutral modern word that fits anyone.
- [ ] [PSA 130:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/130/#v7) R · register · "các ngươi" (R) for Israel spoken to — Readable adds "you", as the English Readable does; "các ngươi" is the literary plural of address to a people that the Psalms use, as for Jerusalem in Psalm 122.
- [ ] [PSA 132:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/132/#v1) L/B/R · register · "người" for David, spoken of with respect — David is the king the psalm honors; "người", the respectful literary pronoun for a revered figure, fits a prayer better than the everyday "ông".
- [ ] [PSA 132:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/132/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ép-ra-ta" and "Gia-a" for Ephrathah and Jaar; "Hòm Giao Ước" for the ark (R) — The names follow the Protestant transliterations. Readable names the ark, as the English Readable does, with the name Vietnamese Bible readers know.
- [ ] [PSA 132:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/132/#v11) L/B/R · register · God says "Ta" (with the deity_pronoun setting mid-sentence) and calls David "con" — God speaking says "Ta", as the style guide sets. To his servant David he says "con", the warm word of a father to a son, which suits a promise of a lasting family line; "ngươi" would sound distant.
- [ ] [PSA 137:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/137/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ba-by-lôn" for Babylon — The name takes the Protestant tradition's form, which Vietnamese Bible readers know.
- [ ] [PSA 137:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/137/#v5) L/B/R · register · "tôi" for the singer and "ngươi" (L, B) for Jerusalem, as in Psalm 122 — One singer makes an oath to the city (see the meaning decision). As in Psalm 122, the city is spoken to as "ngươi" at Literal and Balanced and by name at Readable.
- [ ] [PSA 138:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/138/#v2) L/B · word_choice · "tình yêu thương thành tín" for loyal love and "sự thành tín" for faithfulness, side by side — The two key terms stand together; the terms note gives "sự thành tín" for faithfulness, and the shared word "thành tín" shows that the two belong together, as loyal love and faithfulness do in Hebrew.
- [ ] [PSA 140:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/140/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "CHÚA, Chúa của tôi" (L); "Chúa là CHÚA" (B, R) for the divine name joined to the title Lord — The Hebrew joins the divine name to the title "my Lord" (see the meaning decision). Two words "Chúa" side by side would read as a slip, so Literal keeps the Hebrew order with "Chúa của tôi" after the name, and Balanced and Readable say "the Lord who is the LORD", as the English says "Lord GOD". The …
- [ ] [PSA 141:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/141/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "CHÚA, Chúa của tôi" (L); "Chúa là CHÚA" (B, R), as Psalm 140:8 — The divine name joined to the title "my Lord" takes the same wording as in Psalm 140:8.
- [ ] [PSA 142:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/142/#v1) L/B · word_choice · "bài Mát-kin" (L, B); "Bài ca để dạy dỗ" (R) — The meaning of maskil is not certain (see the meaning decision); Literal and Balanced keep it as a name, in the form the terms note gives, and Readable gives one likely sense, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 145:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/145/#v17) L/B · word_choice · "nhân từ" (L, B) for the word the English gives as "faithful"; "yêu thương và trung tín" (R) — The Hebrew word here is the one for a person of loyal love, loyal and kind (see the meaning decision). "thành tín" alone would lose the kindness; "nhân từ" keeps it, and Readable says both, as the English Readable says "loving and loyal".
- [ ] [PSA 148:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/148/#v7) L/B · word_choice · "các thủy quái" (L, B) for the sea monsters; "những sinh vật khổng lồ dưới biển" (R) — The word means the great creatures of the sea (see the meaning decision); "thủy quái" keeps their strangeness without naming one animal, and Readable describes them, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 149:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/149/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "những người trung tín" for the faithful at every level, through Psalms 148-149 — The faithful are people of loyal love (see the meaning decision); "trung tín" says that loyalty plainly, where "các thánh" (the saints) would suggest holiness instead.
- [ ] [PSA 150:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/150/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "kèn sừng cừu đực" for the ram's horn; "đàn hạc" and "đàn lia" for harp and lyre — The ram's horn is the shofar (see the meaning decision); "kèn" alone would suggest a metal trumpet, and "tù và" a village horn, so Vietnamese names what it is made of.
- [ ] [PSA 150:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/psa/150/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "chũm chọe" for the cymbals — Both are Vietnamese words for cymbals; "chũm chọe" is the one most readers use today.

## Châm Ngôn (11)

- [ ] [PRO 1:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/1/#v14) L/B/R · register · the gang calls the young man "anh" and themselves "chúng tôi", with "chúng ta" for "let us" — The sinners speak to him as a friend and an equal, to win him over; "anh" is the friendly word between young men. "mày" would make them openly rough, which is not how a lure sounds.
- [ ] [PRO 6:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/6/#v9) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" (L, B) and "anh" (R) to the sluggard — The father turns to rebuke the lazy man directly, as a type, not his own son. "ngươi" keeps the scornful tone of a rebuke in poetry; Readable uses the plain "anh".
- [ ] [PRO 6:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/6/#v25) L/B · word_choice · "thèm muốn" (L), "khao khát" (B) for desire — The verb is the word of the tenth commandment (see the meaning decision); "thèm muốn" is the word the Vietnamese Bible tradition uses in "chớ tham", close enough for a reader to hear the commandment.
- [ ] [PRO 7:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/7/#v4) L/B · register · "Em là em gái của anh" for "You are my sister" — "My sister" is a word of love for a bride (see the meaning decision), as in the Song of Songs. Vietnamese lovers say "anh" and "em", so the son speaks to Wisdom as a young man to the woman he loves.
- [ ] [PRO 8:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/8/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "đã có ta" (L); "đã tạo nên ta" (B), "đã làm nên ta" (R) — The verb can mean "acquire, possess" or "bring into being" (see the meaning decision). Literal keeps the plain "có" (have), which leaves that open, as the English Literal's "possessed" does; Balanced and Readable follow the English reading.
- [ ] [PRO 9:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/9/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "Người đàn bà Ngu Dại" for the woman Folly, and "nàng" for her — Folly is pictured as a woman, set against Wisdom (see the meaning decision). She takes the same pronoun as Wisdom so that the two hostesses match, as they do in the Hebrew; "ả" and "mụ" would add contempt the text leaves to her words.
- [ ] [PRO 15:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/15/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "Âm Phủ" (L, B), "nơi của người chết" (R) for Sheol; "A-ba-đôn" (L), "chốn hủy diệt" (B) — The shared terms list keeps the name at Literal and says what it is at Balanced and Readable. Abaddon is named at Literal and translated at Balanced, as the English does. Psalm 139:8, drafted earlier, has "Âm Phủ"; a reviewer may want the two books brought together.
- [ ] [PRO 25:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/25/#v6) L/B/R · register · "con" for "you" in this collection too — Chapters 25–29 address the reader without naming a son, but the book is framed as a father's teaching (1:8), and the earlier chapters say "con"; keeping "con" keeps one voice through the book.
- [ ] [PRO 25:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/25/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "xút" (soda, natron) — The word is natron, a soda that fizzes when vinegar is poured on it (see the meaning decision). "xút" is the plain Vietnamese word for soda used as a cleaning salt; a reviewer may prefer "muối kiềm".
- [ ] [PRO 30:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/30/#v4) L/B/R · register · "anh" for the hearer Agur challenges (verses 4–33) — Agur speaks to his hearers as an equal, not as a father to a son, so the polite adult "anh" replaces the "con" of the earlier chapters; "ngươi" would sound archaic. A reviewer may prefer "bạn".
- [ ] [PRO 30:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/pro/30/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "đa-man" (rock hyrax) — The animal is the rock hyrax (see the meaning decision), which Vietnamese names "đa-man". It is neither a rabbit nor a rat; few readers will know the name, so a reviewer may want a short description at Readable.

- [ ] [ECC 3:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ecc/3/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "cõi đời đời" (L); "ý thức về cõi đời đời" (B); "niềm khao khát điều còn mãi mãi" (R) — The book's settled wording for 'olam here, following the reading the meaning decision takes (m2).

The draft follows the reading in which the woman speaks for herself and the others to her beloved, so the "you" stays "anh" through the verse; if the young women speak to her, the words would be "chúng tôi" and "chị". "Let us run" is the two …

## Ê-sai (14)

- [ ] [ISA 7:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/7/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "người thiếu nữ" (L, B); "một thiếu nữ" (R) — The terms note's form: "thiếu nữ" is a young woman of age to marry, as the meaning decision reads the Hebrew word, and the published word for it in Proverbs 30:19. Matthew 1:23 has "trinh nữ" because it quotes the Greek, which says "virgin"; the meaning decision sets out both.
- [ ] [ISA 9:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/9/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng Cố Vấn Kỳ Diệu, Đức Chúa Trời Quyền Năng, Cha Đời Đời, Hoàng Tử Bình An"; at R "Vua mang lại bình an" — The terms note's forms, each a pair of words as in the Hebrew (meaning decision m4); they are drafted from the Hebrew and not from a published Vietnamese Bible. A reviewer should weigh them against the English Balanced's "Planner of Wonders" and "God the Mighty Warrior".
- [ ] [ISA 21:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/21/#v4) L · supplied_words · "[Ngài]" — The verb has no stated subject; the English L's bracketed "he" may be the vision or God who gave it. Vietnamese needs a pronoun, and "[Ngài]" is the respectful one; it is left unspanned because the referent is not certain.
- [ ] [ISA 24:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/24/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng công chính" — The meaning decision leaves open whether the righteous one is God or the righteous people; Vietnamese must choose a noun, and "Đấng" follows the reading that it is God, which a reviewer may change to "người công chính".
- [ ] [ISA 26:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/26/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "vong linh" (L); "người chết" (B, R) — The terms note's form for rephaim, as Psalm 88:11; Proverbs 9:18 L has "âm hồn", and a reviewer should settle on one.
- [ ] [ISA 31:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/31/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "chiến đấu trên núi Si-ôn" — The meaning decision leaves open whether the LORD fights against or upon Zion; "trên" keeps both readings open, as the English "on" does.
- [ ] [ISA 33:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/33/#v17) L/R · word_choice · "người", lowercase, for the king — The meaning decision leaves open whether the king is the LORD or a king of David's line; the plain "người" keeps both readings, as the English unspanned "his" does.
- [ ] [ISA 34:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/34/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "con vật của ban đêm" — The meaning decision says the creature is unknown; Vietnamese keeps a neutral description, as the English "night creature" does, without naming a demon.
- [ ] [ISA 41:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/41/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ta [là] Đấng ấy" (L); "Ta vẫn là Đấng ấy" (B); "Chính {{deity_pronoun:ta|Ta}} là Đấng ấy" (R) — Vietnamese "là" cannot end a sentence and has no free "he" for God here, so the noun "Đấng" (the One) carries the English "he" and takes no deity_pronoun span. The fuller note on "I am he" is at 43:10.
- [ ] [ISA 43:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/43/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ta [là] Đấng ấy" (L); "chính Ta là Đấng ấy" (B); "chỉ một mình Ta là Đức Chúa Trời" (R) — The main note on 'ani hu ("I am he"). Vietnamese "là" cannot end a sentence, so Literal brackets "là" and gives "Đấng ấy" (the One) for "he"; being a noun it takes no deity_pronoun span. Readable says what the phrase claims, as the English Readable does. John's Vietnamese "Ta là [Đấng Hằng Hữu]" …
- [ ] [ISA 52:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/52/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · the servant is "người", lowercase, with no span, through 52:13-53:12 — As at 42:1: in Isaiah the servant takes no deity_pronoun span; Acts 8:32-33 spans the pronoun only because the New Testament applies these words to Jesus. The passage was drafted from the Hebrew gloss, keeping clear of familiar Vietnamese wording.
- [ ] [ISA 53:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/53/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · the speakers in 53:1-6 say "chúng tôi" — The meaning decision reads "we" as a group who once misjudged the servant and now confess to their hearers; the exclusive "chúng tôi" marks them as the speakers set apart from those they address, and keeps the draft clear of the familiar "chúng ta" wording. A reviewer may prefer the inclusive …
- [ ] [ISA 53:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/53/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "dân tôi" (my people), with no deity_pronoun span — The speaker of "my people" is not marked: the English leaves "my" lowercase with no span, reading it as the voice that speaks in 53:1-10. Vietnamese must still choose a pronoun, and "tôi" follows that reading; if God is the speaker, as in 52:13-15 and 53:11-12, it would be "dân Ta" with a span.
- [ ] [ISA 62:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/isa/62/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · the speaker says "tôi" — The English gives the "I" of 62:1 and 62:6 no deity_pronoun span, reading it as the prophet's voice (as in 61:1, 10); Vietnamese must choose a pronoun and follows that reading with "tôi". If God is the speaker, "Ta" with a span would be needed.

## Giê-rê-mi (22)
