- [ ] [JER 35:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/35/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · Giô-na-đáp (here and verses 10, 19); Giê-hô-na-đáp (verses 8, 14, 16, 18) — The Vietnamese keeps the two forms of the name as the Hebrew and the English do. 2 Kings 10:15-23 writes the long form as Giô-na-đáp; a reviewer may prefer one form for both.
- [ ] [JER 35:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/35/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · Nê-bu-cát-rết-sa — The verse has the form of the name with r (Nebuchadrezzar); Vietnamese keeps it as the English does, writing Nê-bu-cát-rết-sa beside the published Nê-bu-cát-nết-sa. A reviewer may prefer one spelling for both.
- [ ] [JER 36:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/36/#v6) L/B/R · register · anh (for Baruch); tôi (Jeremiah) — Jeremiah speaks to his scribe and companion as "anh", a friend and co-worker, and says "tôi" for himself; Baruch answers others as "tôi".
- [ ] [JER 36:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/36/#v29) L/B/R · register · ngươi (to King Jehoiakim) — God's word to the king who burned the scroll is a sentence of judgment, so it says "ngươi" at every level, as to the foreign kings; the terms note's "con" (B, R) is for a king God comforts, as Hezekiah and Josiah. A reviewer should confirm.
- [ ] [JER 37:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/37/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · Gi-rê-gia — A new name for these books, written in the usual pattern of the published transliterations.
- [ ] [JER 39:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/39/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · Nẹt-ganh-Sa-rết-xe, Sam-ga-Nê-bô, Sa-sê-kim; Ráp-sa-ri (L), trưởng các quan trong triều (B, R); Ráp-mác kept — New Babylonian names and titles, written in the pattern of the published transliterations; Balanced and Readable translate Rab-saris and keep Rab-mag, as the English does.
- [ ] [JER 40:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/40/#v16) L/B/R · register · anh (Gedaliah to Johanan) — The governor answers one of his commanders with "anh", the word of a superior who trusts him; the commanders say "ông" to the governor.
- [ ] [JER 42:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/42/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · Đức Chúa Trời của chúng tôi — The people speak to Jeremiah of "our God" as their own, excluding the listener in form; Vietnamese "chúng tôi" keeps their words as a pledge made to him.
- [ ] [JER 44:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/44/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · Mích-đôn, Tác-pha-nết, Mem-phít, Pha-trốt — New place names in Egypt, written in the pattern of the published transliterations; *Noph* is given as Memphis at every level, as the English does.
- [ ] [JER 44:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/44/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · Nữ Vương trên trời — The goddess's title is kept as the people say it at every level, capitalised as a title; "trên trời" is plain and modern.
- [ ] [JER 46:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/46/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · Ngài (He made many stumble) — The subject is the LORD of verse 15, so Vietnamese says "Ngài", the pronoun for God, where the English has a plain "He".
- [ ] [JER 46:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/46/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · A-môn của Thê-bê — "No" is given as Thebes at every level, as the English does; the god Amon is written A-môn, as the king's name in 2 Kings 21.
- [ ] [JER 48:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/48/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · Gia-hác, Mê-phát and the other towns of Moab — The towns of verses 21-24 are written as Joshua 13 and 21 and 1 Chronicles 6 have them where they are published; the rest are new, in the same pattern.
- [ ] [JER 48:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/48/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · Ta (the speaker who wails, verses 31-36) — The speaker who wails over Moab continues from "declares the LORD" in verse 30 and is the "I" who stops the wine in verse 33 and the offerings in verse 35, so Vietnamese says "Ta" through these verses. The English "I" does not show the choice.
- [ ] [JER 49:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/49/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · niềm vui Ta — The speaker continues the LORD's oracle, so "my joy" is "niềm vui Ta"; the English "my" carries no span here, since the speaker is not marked. A reviewer may judge it the prophet's voice.
- [ ] [JER 50:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/50/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · Mê-ra-tha-im, Phê-cốt; tận diệt (L, B), tiêu diệt hết thảy, như vật đã dâng hiến cho Ta (R) — New names, with their meanings given at Readable as the English does. The ban (*herem*) is "tận diệt" at Literal and Balanced; Readable says what it means, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [JER 51:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/51/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · Lép-ca-mai — The Hebrew writes a code name for Chaldea here; Vietnamese keeps it as a name at every level, and Readable explains it, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [JER 51:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/51/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · các vua Mê-đi — The Medes are written as the published form of Acts 2:9 ("người Mê-đi").
- [ ] [JER 51:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/51/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · A-ra-rát, Min-ni, Ách-kê-na; tướng chỉ huy — The kingdoms of the north, written in the pattern of the published names (A-ra-rát as Genesis 8:4); the marshal is "tướng chỉ huy", as the English Balanced and Readable say "commander".
- [ ] [JER 51:41](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/51/#v41) L/B/R · word_choice · Sê-sác; Readable adds "tức là Ba-by-lôn" — A code name for Babylon, as in 25:26; Vietnamese keeps it at every level and Readable explains it, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [JER 51:59](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jer/51/#v59) L/B/R · word_choice · Sê-ra-gia con trai Nê-ri-gia, con trai Mách-sê-gia; quan coi việc nghỉ chân (L), quan lo việc doanh trại (B) — Seraiah is Baruch's brother (son of Neriah); Mahseiah is new. His office is given as the English gives it, by level.

## Ca Thương (1)

- [ ] [LAM 2:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/lam/2/#v13) L/B/R · register · The poet addresses Zion as "ngươi" through 2:13–19 — Vietnamese has no neutral "you"; "ngươi" is the address the prophets use to a city or people, and it keeps the poet's voice apart from Zion's own "tôi". It can sound cold for words meant to comfort, so a reviewer may prefer a warmer word.

## Ê-xê-chi-ên (23)

- [ ] [EZK 1:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/1/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · hổ phách (L); kim loại sáng loáng (B); kim loại chiếu sáng (R) — Nobody knows what chashmal was (see the meaning decision). Literal follows the English "amber"; Balanced and Readable describe what Ezekiel saw, a metal gleaming in fire. The same words are used in 1:27 and 8:2.
- [ ] [EZK 4:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/4/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · lúa mì spen-tơ — Spelt has no common Vietnamese name; the borrowed "spen-tơ" with "lúa mì" tells the reader it is a kind of wheat. A reviewer may prefer another word.
- [ ] [EZK 7:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/7/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · thủ lĩnh (L, B); người cai trị (R) — Nasi' as the Pentateuch's word, kept through Ezekiel, 34-48 included. A reviewer may prefer "ông hoàng" for the prince of chapters 44-48.
- [ ] [EZK 8:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/8/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · đồng bằng — The place of 3:22-23, where the same Hebrew word was rendered "thung lũng" after the English "valley"; here the English says "plain", and Vietnamese follows it. A reviewer may want one word for both.
- [ ] [EZK 10:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/10/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · các bánh xe quay — Galgal, the name for the wheels in this chapter, kept apart from the ordinary "bánh xe" of chapter 1 by "quay" (whirling). A reviewer may prefer another word.
- [ ] [EZK 16:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/16/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · qin'ah: "lòng ghen" — The LORD's zeal for a covenant that is his alone (meaning decision); "lòng ghen" keeps the marriage picture without the pettiness of "ghen tuông", and is related to the "Đức Chúa Trời kỵ tà" of Exodus 20:5.
- [ ] [EZK 16:60](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/16/#v60) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" kept through the promise of 16:60-63 — The terms note keeps "ngươi" to the end of the chapter, so the promise is spoken to the same guilty Jerusalem; a reviewer may prefer the warmer "con" of God's promises from here on.
- [ ] [EZK 23:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/23/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "Phê-cốt, Sô-a và Cô-a" — "Phê-cốt" is the form published in Jeremiah 50:21; "Sô-a" and "Cô-a" are new forms in the same syllabified spelling.
- [ ] [EZK 34:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/34/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · nasi' "thủ lĩnh" (L, B) / "người cai trị" (R) — The term sheet's word for Ezekiel's nasi', as the Pentateuch's nasi; it keeps Ezekiel's distinction from "vua" (melek). Marked uncertain in the term sheet: a reviewer may prefer "ông hoàng", especially in chapters 44-48.
- [ ] [EZK 38:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/38/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "Gót, ở xứ Ma-gốc, thủ lĩnh tối cao của Mê-siếc và Tu-banh" (L, B) / "người cai trị tối cao" (R) — The term sheet's wording: nasi' rosh as "thủ lĩnh tối cao", with the other reading (Rosh as a place) in the footnoted meaning decision. The names are those of Genesis 10:2. Revelation 20:8 has "Ma-gót", from the Greek; the two spellings name the same figure.
- [ ] [EZK 39:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/39/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "thủ lĩnh tối cao của Mê-siếc và Tu-banh" (L, B) / "người cai trị tối cao" (R), as 38:2 — The term sheet's wording, recorded as uncertain at 38:2; kept the same here.
- [ ] [EZK 40:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/40/#v5) B/R · setting_alternative · metric by default with the original unit as the alternative, worked out on the long cubit of 53 cm: "{{units:khoảng 3,2 m|một cây sậy}}", "{{units:khoảng 2,7 … — Vietnamese uses metric by default, as in the other books, so the spans differ from the English (feet). The measuring cubit here is "a cubit and a handbreadth" (about 45 cm plus 7,5 cm), so the term sheet sets it at about 53 cm and the reed of six such cubits at about 3,2 m, not the 45 cm cubit …
- [ ] [EZK 40:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/40/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · ta' "phòng gác" (L, B) / "phòng nhỏ" (R); 'ulam "tiền sảnh" at every level — The term sheet's words: "tiền sảnh" as 1 Kings 6:3; "phòng gác" says what the recess probably was, and the footnoted meaning decision gives the uncertainty. Used through chapters 40-48.
- [ ] [EZK 40:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/40/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · 'elim "trụ cửa" at every level — The term sheet's word, as 1 Kings 6:31, kept through chapters 40-48; the footnoted meaning decision says the exact structure is uncertain.
- [ ] [EZK 40:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/40/#v14) B/R · word_choice · "đo" (B, R) where the Hebrew says "made"; "làm" kept at L — The verse is hard (footnoted meaning decision); Balanced and Readable follow the English reading "measured". Readable says "cao" for the sixty cubits, the likeliest sense of a jamb's measure, where the English R leaves it open.
- [ ] [EZK 41:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/41/#v1) B/R · setting_alternative · "khoảng 3,2 m" (default) / "sáu cu-đê" (alternative): metres by default, worked out on the long cubit of about 53 cm — In Ezekiel 40-48 the measuring cubit is "a cubit and a handbreadth" (40:5), the 45 cm cubit of the published Kings plus a handbreadth of about 7.5 cm, so about 53 cm, and the reed of six such cubits about 3.2 m. Every units span in chapters 41-48 is worked out on it, with a decimal comma; the …
- [ ] [EZK 41:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/41/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "trụ cửa" for 'ayil (jamb) at every level — The word used for the door frame in 1 Kings 6:31, kept for the same Hebrew through Ezekiel 40-48. What exactly the 'elim were (jambs, pilasters or projecting walls) is uncertain, as the English decision on this word says.
- [ ] [EZK 41:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/41/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "khu riêng" for gizrah at every level — The word means a place cut off or set apart; "khu riêng" keeps that without guessing at its use, and is used for every occurrence in chapters 41-42.
- [ ] [EZK 41:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/41/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "hành lang" for 'attiq (galleries) — The Hebrew word is rare and its sense uncertain (terraces, balconies or passages); "hành lang" is the most general of these and is used for every occurrence in chapters 41-42.
- [ ] [EZK 44:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/44/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "thủ lĩnh" (L, B) / "người cai trị" (R) for nasi' — As the Pentateuch's nasi' and the earlier chapters of Ezekiel. He is a human ruler, lowercase and with no deity_pronoun span ("người"). A reviewer may prefer "ông hoàng" for the prince of chapters 44-48.
- [ ] [EZK 46:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/46/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "có tường bao" for qeturot — The likeliest sense of a rare word, as the English; the footnoted meaning decision gives the others.
- [ ] [EZK 47:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/47/#v6) L/B/R · register · the guide says "Hỡi con người" and "ông" — The guide is a heavenly messenger, not God: as the angels of Daniel and Zechariah and Gabriel in Luke 1:13, he speaks to Ezekiel with "ông" and calls himself "tôi"; "Hỡi con người" stays as in God's address.
- [ ] [EZK 47:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/ezk/47/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "Bê-rô-tha", "Síp-ra-im", "Hát-se Hát-ti-côn", "Ha-u-ran" (new) — Spelled on the pattern of the published names; "Đa-mách" and "Ha-mát" as published.

## Đa-ni-ên (14)

- [ ] [DAN 5:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/5/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · MÊ-NÊ, MÊ-NÊ, TÊ-KEN và PHÁC-SIN; PHÊ-RÉT (5:28), in capitals at every level — The Aramaic words are written in capitals in the Vietnamese syllable spelling Vietnamese readers know, without the older "U-" (the text reads "and PARSIN"). The puns cannot be kept: MÊ-NÊ "đếm" (numbered), TÊ-KEN "cân" (weighed), PHÊ-RÉT "chia" (divided) and "Ba Tư" (Persia); Daniel explains each …
- [ ] [DAN 6:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/6/#v17) L/B/R · register · Darius says "ông" to Daniel, and "ta" for himself — Darius speaks to Daniel with "ông", out of respect and concern, where Nebuchadnezzar and Belshazzar say "ngươi". The same holds in verse 21.
- [ ] [DAN 7:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/7/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · Đấng Thượng Cổ (L, B); Đấng Cao Niên (R), with "Ngài" — "Ancient of Days" is "Đấng Thượng Cổ", a short fixed phrase readers know, at Literal and Balanced; Readable says "Đấng Cao Niên", plainer, as the English Readable says "the Ancient One". He is God, so he takes the deity_pronoun "Ngài".
- [ ] [DAN 7:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/7/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · [một vị] giống như con người (L); một vị giống như con người (B); một vị trông giống như một con người (R); lowercase, with "vị ấy" and no span — In the vision this is a description, so "con người" is lowercase and the figure takes no deity_pronoun, which keeps him distinct from the Ancient of Days; "vị" gives him honour without making a title. "Giống như con người" is the wording of Revelation 1:13. Jesus takes the phrase as his title "Con …
- [ ] [DAN 7:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/7/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · các thánh của Đấng Chí Cao (L, B); dân thánh của Đức Chúa Trời (R) — "Các thánh" can mean God's holy people or holy beings, and so keeps the openness the meaning decision describes at Literal and Balanced; Readable names God's holy people, as the English Readable does. The same wording holds through verses 21-27.
- [ ] [DAN 8:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/8/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng Thống Lãnh của đạo binh", capitalised, with "Ngài" spans — The meaning decision reads the Prince of the host as God himself. "Đấng" marks a divine person, and "Thống Lãnh" keeps him apart from "thủ lĩnh", the term sheet's word for the angelic princes (sar) of chapters 10-12. Joshua 5:14's "tướng chỉ huy đạo quân" is a different figure. The same title …
- [ ] [DAN 8:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/8/#v19) L/B/R · register · Gabriel says "tôi" and "ông" to Daniel at every level — The term sheet's address for the angels of Daniel: respectful and equal, as Gabriel speaks to Zechariah in Luke 1:13, 19. The angels of chapters 9-12 keep it.
- [ ] [DAN 8:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/8/#v24) L/B/R · register · "hắn" for the fierce king — Vietnamese marks a hostile person with the pronoun. "Hắn" is used for the oppressor king of 8:23-25 and of 11:21-45; the other kings of chapter 11 are "vua ấy" or "ông", and the animals and horns are "nó".
- [ ] [DAN 8:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/8/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng Thống Lãnh của các thủ lĩnh" (L, B); "Đức Chúa Trời, Đấng Thống Lãnh trên mọi người cai trị" (R) — The same title as "Đấng Thống Lãnh của đạo binh" in verse 11; "thủ lĩnh" is the term sheet's word for sar, so the "prince of princes" pattern shows. "Vua của các vua" is the New Testament title (1 Timothy 6:15) and a different word.
- [ ] [DAN 9:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/9/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "bảy mươi bộ bảy" (L, B); "bảy mươi nhóm bảy" (R) — The Hebrew gives the number and no unit. "Tuần lễ" would add "days", and "bảy mươi lần bảy" is the wording of Matthew 18:22, which means something else. The same words hold through verses 25-27.
- [ ] [DAN 9:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/9/#v27) L/B/R · register · "người ấy" for the one who makes the covenant — The meaning decision takes him to be the coming ruler of verse 26, but readers have taken him in other ways; the neutral "người ấy" leaves the reading to the decision rather than to the pronoun.
- [ ] [DAN 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/10/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "thủ lĩnh" (L, B); "thiên sứ cai quản" (R) for sar as an angel — The term sheet's wording. "Thủ lĩnh" is the Kings word for sarim (2 Kings 24:12) and keeps the Hebrew's single word for human and heavenly princes; Readable says what the meaning decision says they are, as the English "ruling angel" does. The same words hold in 10:20-21 and 12:1.
- [ ] [DAN 10:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/10/#v16) L/B/R · register · "thưa chúa tôi" (L, B); "thưa ngài" (R), lowercase, with no deity_pronoun span — Adoni is a polite address to a superior, as Joshua 5:14 "chúa tôi"; lowercase keeps it apart from "Chúa" for God. Daniel speaks of himself as "đầy tớ của chúa tôi" in verse 17. Readable's "ngài" is the respectful human word, not God's "Ngài".
- [ ] [DAN 11:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/dan/11/#v21) L/B/R · register · "hắn" for the contemptible king from here to 11:45 — The hostile pronoun marks the persecutor, as for the fierce king of 8:23-25. It follows the text's own judgment ("a contemptible person") and does not name him; the meaning decision gives the usual identification.

## Ô-sê (3)

- [ ] [HOS 2:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/hos/2/#v18) B/R · register · God to Israel, his bride, in 2:16-25: "ngươi" (L), "con" (B, R) — The terms note gives "con" at Balanced and Readable in Hosea's tender passages (2:16-25; 11:8-9; 14:5-9). In a marriage image "con" from a husband may sound odd to a Vietnamese reader; a reviewer should decide whether the bride passages (2:18-22) keep "con" or take another form.
- [ ] [HOS 3:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/hos/3/#v3) L/B/R · register · Hosea to his wife: "em", himself "tôi" — A husband speaking to his wife says "em"; Hosea keeps "tôi" for himself, as the narrator. Uncertain for a reviewer: a more archaic register might prefer "nàng".
- [ ] [HOS 10:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/hos/10/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "San-man", "Bết-Ạt-bên" — "San-man" is the terms note's form; "Bết-Ạt-bên" is new, built on the usual transliteration of "Beth-" and "Arbel".

## A-mốt (7)

Literal keeps the Hebrew's vague "it" as "điều ấy" (the meaning decision says it is most likely the punishment); Balanced says "hình phạt" without brackets.
- [ ] [AMO 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/amo/4/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "Hạt-môn" — The place is unknown (meaning decision); the name is transliterated after the Protestant pattern for Hebrew names; a reviewer may prefer another spelling.

Readable explains the wordplay in the text, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [AMO 7:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/amo/7/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "dây dọi" at every level — 'Anakh is "plumb line", the published "dây dọi" of 2 Kings 21:13; the meaning decision footnotes "tin" as another reading.
- [ ] [AMO 8:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/amo/8/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "trái cây mùa hạ ... sự cuối cùng" (L, B); "chín ... chín muồi cho ngày kết thúc" (R) — Qayits (summer fruit) and qets (end) are a pun that Vietnamese cannot copy. Literal and Balanced translate the words and leave the pun to the meaning decision's footnote; Readable keeps a pun of its own with "chín" (ripe) and "chín muồi" (ripe for), as the terms sheet proposes, and also states the …

## Giô-na (2)

- [ ] [JON 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jon/2/#v3) L/B/R · register · in the psalm Jonah says "tôi" and speaks of God as "Ngài" — Jonah's psalm is made of lines from the Psalms (120:1 here, then 42:8, 31:23, 69:2, 31:7, 3:9), and the published Psalms have the psalmist say "tôi"; keeping "tôi" lets the quoted lines read as they do there. In his own prayer of 4:2-3 Jonah says "con", as the terms note gives for a prophet …
- [ ] [JON 4:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jon/4/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "thương tiếc" (L), as for the plant in 4:10; "thương xót" (B, R) — The Hebrew uses the same verb for Jonah's feeling for the plant and God's for Nineveh; Literal keeps one word for both. For a city of living people Balanced and Readable say "thương xót", since "tiếc" is said of what is already lost.

## Mi-chê (8)

- [ ] [MIC 1:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/1/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · the town names "Sa-phia", "Xa-a-nan", "Bết-Ê-xên" (and "Ma-rốt", "Bết-Lê-áp-ra", "Mô-rê-sết-Gát" in the next verses) — New transliterations in the Protestant pattern. Zaanan may be the Zenan of Joshua 15:37 ("Xê-nan"), but the Hebrew spelling differs and the play on "go out" needs this form. Published forms are kept for La-ki, Ạc-xíp, Ma-rê-sa and A-đu-lam.
- [ ] [MIC 2:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/2/#v4) L/B · setting_alternative · "Ngài" for the one who changes, takes and divides, with the deity_pronoun span where it is not at the start of a sentence — The meaning footnote: the one who does it is most likely the LORD, so Vietnamese uses the reverent pronoun, as the English uses its deity span. In Readable each "Ngài" starts a sentence and is written plainly.
- [ ] [MIC 2:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/2/#v11) B/R · register · the false preacher says "các người" to the people — "Các ngươi" is God's word to the people in rebuke; a man selling them good news would flatter them with the ordinary "các người". Literal keeps "các ngươi" to stay close to the Hebrew line.
- [ ] [MIC 2:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/2/#v12) L/B/R · register · "những người sót lại" (L, B) / "những người còn sống sót" (R); the remnant are "họ" in this promise at B and R, "chúng" at L where the Hebrew pronoun stays … — The terms note gives "họ" for Israel in promises. Literal "chúng" reads with the sheep of the same line; a reviewer may prefer "họ" there too.
- [ ] [MIC 4:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/4/#v8) L/B/R · register · Zion is addressed as "ngươi" through 4:8-14 at every level, and as "con" at B and R only in the LORD's promise of 4:13 — The terms note keeps "ngươi" for the poet addressing Zion, and the Isaiah note gives God "con" (B, R) to Zion in promises; 4:13 is the one verse here where the LORD speaks ("I will make").
- [ ] [MIC 6:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/6/#v1) L/B/R · register · the LORD's command to the prophet: "ngươi" (L, B) / "con" (R) — The meaning decision: the command is spoken to the prophet, who presents the LORD's case. The terms note gives God "ngươi" (L) / "con" (B, R) to a prophet; here the courtroom setting keeps "ngươi" at Balanced as well, so the summons sounds formal.
- [ ] [MIC 6:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/6/#v6) B · word_choice · "Đức Chúa Trời Chí Cao" (B) — "God on high" is not the title "'elyon" ("Đấng Chí Cao"); Balanced uses "Chí Cao" only as an adjective of height, and Literal keeps "trên nơi cao".
- [ ] [MIC 6:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mic/6/#v8) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "hỡi người phàm" (L, B) / "hỡi loài người" (R), with no gender span, and "ngươi" (L, B) / "anh em" (R) — The English offers "man" as a gender alternative; "người phàm" and "loài người" already cover men and women, so there is no Vietnamese span. Readable's "anh em" is the prophet pleading with his hearers, as the terms note gives.

## Na-hum (2)

- [ ] [NAM 1:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/nam/1/#v2) L/B/R · word_order · the partial alphabet poem of 1:2-8 is not imitated — The meaning footnote: verses 2-8 run from aleph to kaph. Vietnamese cannot keep a Hebrew letter order, so the lines follow the sense and the footnote tells the reader.
- [ ] [NAM 2:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/nam/2/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "nữ tì" (L); "tớ gái" (B, R) — The English moves from "slave women" (L) to "servant women" (B, R); Vietnamese "nữ tì" keeps the bound status at Literal without the harsher "nô lệ", which the terms note keeps for slavery itself.

## Sô-phô-ni (3)

- [ ] [ZEP 1:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zep/1/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "Mác-tết" (L, B), the place name transliterated, / "khu phố gọi là Cối Giã" (R) — The meaning decision: the Mortar is a district of Jerusalem; Literal and Balanced give the name, Readable translates it, as the English Readable explains it.
- [ ] [ZEP 1:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zep/1/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "lửa ghen" (L) / "lửa ghen tức" (B) / "cơn giận ghen tuông" (R) — The meaning decision: God's burning zeal for what is his, not envy; Vietnamese "ghen" of God is the usual Bible word, and the context of fire makes the sense plain.
- [ ] [ZEP 3:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zep/3/#v1) L/B/R · register · Jerusalem is "nàng" in the third person through 3:1-7 — The terms note: Zion personified is "nàng"; the city here is Jerusalem, not yet named (meaning decision), and the Hebrew speaks of her as a woman. God addresses her as "ngươi" in the rebuke of verse 7.

## A-ghê (3)

- [ ] [HAG 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/hag/1/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Giô-suê con trai Giê-hô-xa-đác", with "Xô-rô-ba-bên con trai Sa-la-thi-ên" as in Matthew 1:12 — The terms note: the English "Joshua" is the same Hebrew name as Moses' successor, so his form is used; Ezra's short form is "Giê-sua con trai Giô-xa-đác" and the culture decision links the two men. The 1926 form is "Giê-hô-sua".
- [ ] [HAG 2:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/hag/2/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "luật pháp" (L, R) / "chỉ dạy" (B) for torah, a priestly ruling — Here torah is the priests' ruling from the law (the English "instruction" / "a ruling from the law"), so Vietnamese "luật pháp" is apt; in Habakkuk and Zephaniah 3:4, where it is God's teaching, it is "lời dạy dỗ".
- [ ] [HAG 2:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/hag/2/#v15) L · word_choice · "từ ngày này trở về trước" at Literal — The meaning decision reads "from this day and upward" as looking back on the time before the rebuilding; Vietnamese says "trở về trước" so the direction is clear.

## Xa-cha-ri (11)

- [ ] [ZEC 1:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/1/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "tháng Sê-bát" for Shebat — A new month name, transliterated in the Protestant manner; the culture decision gives the modern date.
- [ ] [ZEC 1:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/1/#v9) L/B/R · register · "thưa chúa tôi" (L, B) / "thưa ngài" (R) from Zechariah; the angel says "tôi" and "ông" — The terms note: 'adoni is "thưa chúa tôi", lowercase, as Joshua 5:14, with "thưa ngài" at Readable; the angels of Zechariah speak as Gabriel does in Luke 1:13. None take a deity span.
- [ ] [ZEC 1:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/1/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "ghen" for God's jealousy, as in Zephaniah 1:18 — The meaning decision: zeal for what belongs to God and fierce protection; Readable adds what it means, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [ZEC 3:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/3/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "kẻ sa-tan" (L, lowercase, a title) / "Kẻ Tố Cáo" (B, R) — The terms note, following the English (Job): the Hebrew has the article, so it is a title, "the accuser"; the name "Sa-tan" (1 Chronicles 21:1) is not used here.
- [ ] [ZEC 3:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/3/#v4) L/B/R · register · the angel of the LORD says "Ta" and speaks to Joshua as "ngươi" (L) / "con" (B, R) — He acts with God's authority, forgiving iniquity, as the angel of the LORD does in Genesis 16:10 and Judges 2:1, whose Vietnamese has him say "Ta"; the sentence is built so that "Ta" opens it and needs no deity span, which the terms note withholds from the angel of the LORD.
- [ ] [ZEC 4:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/4/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "hòn đá thiếc" (L) / "quả dọi" (B, R) — The meaning decision: a builder's plumb weight; Literal keeps "tin stone", Balanced and Readable name the tool, the weight of Amos 7:7's "dây dọi".
- [ ] [ZEC 6:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/6/#v4) L/B/R · register · "Thưa chúa tôi" (L, B) / "Thưa ngài" (R) for "my lord" said to the angel, with no deity span — 'Adoni is a polite address to a superior, as Joshua 5:14 and 1 Kings 18:7; lowercase "chúa tôi" keeps it apart from God. R's "thưa ngài" is plainer.
- [ ] [ZEC 9:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/9/#v1) L · word_order · L reads "mắt của loài người ... [đều hướng] về CHÚA", one of the two readings the meaning decision allows; B and R follow the English, the LORD's eye on all … — Vietnamese cannot keep the Hebrew's ambiguous "to the LORD the eye of humankind" without choosing; L keeps the Hebrew's order and the other reading, which the footnote names.
- [ ] [ZEC 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/11/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây bách xù" for the juniper (berosh) — The tree is uncertain (juniper, cypress or fir); "bách xù" follows the English, a reviewer may prefer the published word for berosh elsewhere.
- [ ] [ZEC 13:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/13/#v3) L/B/R · register · the parents say "mày" (L) / "con" (B, R) to the son — L keeps the harshness of a death sentence; B and R keep the parent-child bond that makes the verse shocking.
- [ ] [ZEC 14:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/zec/14/#v5) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "núi Ta" / "núi của Ta" with no span, since the speaker is not clear; "ở với Ngài" with the deity span — The meaning decision leaves open who says "my mountains"; the English writes "my" with no span, and Vietnamese does the same with a capital "Ta" that reads as the LORD only if the reader takes it so. "Ngài" for "you": the prophet turns to speak to God, and Vietnamese addresses God as "Ngài".

## Ma-la-chi (3)

- [ ] [MAL 1:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mal/1/#v2) L/B/R · register · the disputations: God's words take no quotation marks and say "các ngươi"; the people's retort is "Nhưng các ngươi nói: ..." (L) / "Thế mà các ngươi hỏi: ..." … — The term sheet's pattern for Malachi. The people answer back rather than pray, so they say "chúng tôi", not the "chúng con" of prayer.
- [ ] [MAL 2:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mal/2/#v15) L · word_choice · "Đấng duy nhất" for "one" at L, reading it of God as B does — The Hebrew is very hard; L must choose a subject in Vietnamese, and follows the reading of the English B and R; the meaning decision gives the others.
- [ ] [MAL 2:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mal/2/#v16) L · supplied_words · "[Ngài] ghét" at L — The English L supplies "he"; the founder's open note keeps "I hate divorce" as the reading at B and R.

## Ma-thi-ơ (37)

- [ ] [MAT 1:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/1/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · names in the forms of the Vietnamese Protestant tradition, which follow the Greek: Pha-rê, Ếch-rôm, A-ram, Na-ách-son, Sanh-môn, Bô-ô, Ra-háp, Ô-bết, Ru-tơ, … — Vietnamese Bible readers know the genealogy in these transliterations (style guide, section 4). A reviewer should check them against the forms the Vietnamese Old Testament will use for the same people.
- [ ] [MAT 1:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/1/#v18) L/B/R · register · "cô" for Mary in the narrative of 1:18-25 — Mary is a young woman pledged to be married; "cô" is the respectful plain word for her. "Bà" would make her older, and "nàng" is literary.
- [ ] [MAT 1:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/1/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "cho cô đi" (L); "ly dị" (B); "hủy bỏ hôn ước" (R) — Literal keeps the Greek picture of sending away; Balanced uses the legal word, since a betrothal could only be ended by divorce; Readable "hủy bỏ hôn ước" (break off the betrothal) is how Vietnamese readers would picture ending it before the wedding.
- [ ] [MAT 2:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/2/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "các nhà thông thái" (L, B); "mấy nhà thông thái chuyên nghiên cứu các vì sao" (R) for the magi — The meaning decision describes learned men who studied the stars and interpreted signs. "Nhà thông thái" (learned man) is plain and carries no judgment; "nhà chiêm tinh" sounds like a fortune-teller today, "pháp sư" means a sorcerer, and "đạo sĩ" is the Catholic tradition's word. Readable adds what …
- [ ] [MAT 2:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/2/#v5) B/R · register · "tâu" for speaking to the king (B, R) — Vietnamese has a verb for speaking to a king, and Balanced and Readable use it as a careful native narrator would; Literal keeps the plain "nói" of the Greek.
- [ ] [MAT 2:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/2/#v13) L/B/R · register · the angel says "ta" for himself (lowercase, no span) — An angel speaking with God's authority uses "ta", but he is not God, so no deity_pronoun span applies.
- [ ] [MAT 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/2/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "con" in lower case for "my son" — In Hosea the son is Israel, and Matthew reads the words as fulfilled in Jesus; the lower case keeps both, as the English "my son" does.
- [ ] [MAT 2:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/2/#v18) R · word_choice · "các con bà đã chết hết" (R) — The meaning decision reads "they are not" as "they are dead"; Readable says it plainly, since "không còn" could also mean only that they were taken away.
- [ ] [MAT 2:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/2/#v22) R · word_choice · "lên làm vua" (R) for Archelaus's rule — The Greek verb is "reigns as king"; Readable uses the plain words, while Literal and Balanced say "cai trị" (rule), since Archelaus ruled without the title of king.
- [ ] [MAT 3:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/3/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "vương quốc thiên đàng" for the kingdom of heaven, at every level and through Matthew — Mark's "kingdom of God" is "vương quốc của Đức Chúa Trời" in Vietnamese, so Matthew's phrase keeps "vương quốc" for God's rule as king, with the familiar "thiên đàng" for "the heavens". Literal does not copy the Greek plural, which is a Hebrew way of saying "heaven". "Nước Trời" is the Catholic …
- [ ] [MAT 3:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/3/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "nia", "sân đập lúa", "trấu" for winnowing fork, threshing floor and chaff — The Greek tool is a fork or shovel for tossing grain in the wind; Vietnamese farmers winnow with a "nia" (a flat tray), which does the same work and which every reader can picture. "Trấu" is the husk separated from the grain. Readable says "dụng cụ" (tool), as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [MAT 3:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/3/#v14) L/B/R · setting_alternative · John calls Jesus "Thầy" for "you", with no setting span, and himself "tôi" — John puts himself below Jesus; "Thầy" (Teacher) is the respectful noun of address the style guide gives to those who honour him, and nouns of address take no deity_pronoun span.
- [ ] [MAT 4:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/4/#v5) L/B/R · register · the narrator calls the devil "nó"; the devil keeps "ông" inside the psalm he quotes — As for the unclean spirit in Mark 1:25, "nó" speaks of the devil without honour or abuse. The devil turns Psalm 91 into words to Jesus, so the psalm's "you" takes the devil's word for Jesus.
- [ ] [MAT 4:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/4/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "thế gian" (L); "thế giới" (B, R) for the world — Literal keeps the key word "thế gian"; here the world means the nations and their kingdoms, so Balanced and Readable use "thế giới", the word the style guide keeps for the world as a place.
- [ ] [MAT 5:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "thừa hưởng trái đất" (L, B); "ban trái đất cho họ làm cơ nghiệp" (R) — "Đất" alone could mean soil or a plot of land; "trái đất" is the earth. "Cơ nghiệp" (inheritance) at Readable keeps the sense of a gift passed on, as in Psalm 37:11.
- [ ] [MAT 5:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "tạo hòa bình" (L); "đem lại hòa bình" (B); "giúp người ta sống hòa thuận" (R) — The meaning decision speaks of making peace between people, which Vietnamese calls "hòa bình" or "hòa thuận"; the key term "bình an" is kept for the peace God gives.
- [ ] [MAT 5:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v17) L/B · word_choice · "làm trọn" (L, B) for fulfill — The meaning decision reads the verb as bringing the law and the prophets to the full meaning and completion God intended; "làm trọn" holds both carrying out and completing, where "ứng nghiệm" (1:22) would say only that a prediction came true. Readable follows the English Readable.
- [ ] [MAT 5:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "phá bỏ" (L); "coi nhẹ" (B); "phạm" (R) for "breaks" — The meaning decision reads the verb as setting a commandment aside as not binding; Literal keeps the picture of loosening or undoing, Balanced says "treat lightly", and Readable uses the everyday word for breaking a rule.
- [ ] [MAT 5:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v21) L · word_choice · "có lời nói với người xưa" (L) — Matthew leaves the speaker unnamed, and the meaning decision says the words are the law as it was taught; "phán" would make God the speaker of the whole saying, including the teachers' addition about judgment.
- [ ] [MAT 5:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v26) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "đồng cô-đơ-ran" (L, and the alternative at B, R); "đồng xu cuối cùng" (B), "từng đồng xu một" (R) by default — As in Mark 12:42: Literal keeps the coin's name in transliteration, and the units setting gives the plain sense by default. The Vietnamese spelling of the coin's name is not established, and a reviewer should check it.
- [ ] [MAT 5:43](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v43) L/B/R · word_choice · "người lân cận" (L, B); "người gần gũi mình" (R) for neighbor — As in Mark 12:31 for Literal and Balanced. Mark's Readable says "người khác", but here the neighbor is set against the enemy, so Readable says "người gần gũi mình", those close to you.
- [ ] [MAT 5:48](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/5/#v48) L/B/R · word_choice · "trọn vẹn" for perfect — The meaning decision reads the word as complete, whole and undivided, above all in love; "trọn vẹn" says wholeness, while "hoàn hảo" suggests being without any flaw.
- [ ] [MAT 7:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/7/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "vương quốc thiên đàng" for "the kingdom of the heavens" — Matthew's form of "the kingdom of God". "vương quốc" follows Mark ("vương quốc của Đức Chúa Trời"), and "thiên đàng" (heaven) is the word Protestant readers know from "nước thiên đàng"; the plural of the Greek is left unmarked, as in Vietnamese "trời" has no number.
- [ ] [MAT 8:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/8/#v5) L/B/R · register · "Thưa Chúa" and "tôi" in the officer's words; Jesus calls him "ông" — The officer honours Jesus as Lord (meaning decision) but speaks as a man of rank and a Gentile, so "Thưa Chúa" with "tôi" fits him better than the "con" of a believer; "Chúa" is a noun of address and takes no setting span. Jesus answers him as "ông", for a respected man.
- [ ] [MAT 9:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/9/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "các con trai của phòng cưới" (L) with no gender span; "khách dự tiệc cưới" (B); "bạn bè của chàng rể" (R) — As in Mark 2:19: Literal keeps the Hebrew-style idiom; Balanced and Readable say who is meant. The English Literal puts "sons" under the gender setting, but the idiom names the groom's friends at a wedding, and Vietnamese has no second wording that would mean the same, so no span is used.
- [ ] [MAT 12:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/12/#v18) L/B/R · setting_alternative · {{deity_pronoun:người|Người}} for the servant; God says "Ta" with the setting — In Isaiah 42:1-4 God speaks of his servant, whom Matthew sees in Jesus (meaning decision), and the English puts the setting on "he" and "him". Vietnamese "Ngài" would have God honour his servant above himself; "người", the third-person pronoun of respect, fits God speaking and still carries the …
- [ ] [MAT 12:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/12/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "Thần của Đức Chúa Trời" (L, B); "Thần Đức Chúa Trời" (R) — "Thần" (capitalised) is the usual Protestant word for "the Spirit of God" (as at 3:16); the Greek here does not say "Holy", which comes in 12:31-32.
- [ ] [MAT 12:37](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/12/#v37) L/B/R · register · "anh" for the singular "you" — The Greek turns to the singular, a saying aimed at each hearer (meaning decision). "Anh" is the plain form for one unnamed grown hearer; "con" is the form Jesus uses to disciples, and the plural "các người" would lose the turn to one person.
- [ ] [MAT 15:39](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/15/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ma-ga-đan" — The SBLGNT name (meaning decision, with the variant in its footnote), transliterated in the Protestant way; the place is otherwise unknown, so Vietnamese does not tie it to Magdala ("Ma-đơ-len").
- [ ] [MAT 16:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/16/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "các dấu hiệu của thời đại" (L, B) — Here the Greek word for sign is used of what can be read in events, set beside the weather signs; "dấu hiệu" (indication) suits that reading better than "dấu lạ" (a sign shown by God), which 16:1 and 16:4 keep for the sign they demand. Readable says what the signs show (meaning decision).
- [ ] [MAT 16:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/16/#v22) L/B · word_choice · "can gián" (L); "can ngăn" (B) for Peter's rebuke, against "quở trách" for Jesus in 16:23 — Here Jesus answers with a different verb (16:23 has no "rebuke"), and Vietnamese "quở trách" said by a disciple to his teacher would sound like a superior scolding; "can gián" is to remonstrate. Readable says what he did, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [MAT 17:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/17/#v14) L/B/R · register · "Lạy Chúa, xin thương xót" with "tôi" for the father and "cháu" for his son — He kneels and calls Jesus Lord in a plea for mercy, so he says "Lạy Chúa"; as a grown man and not a disciple he speaks of himself as "tôi", as the officer does in 8:6. A Vietnamese parent speaking to a respected person calls his child "cháu" (Mark 9:17). A reviewer may prefer "con" here as in 15:22.
- [ ] [MAT 17:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/17/#v24) L/B/R · register · "Thầy của các anh" in the collectors' words, with no deity_pronoun span; "các anh" for Peter and the disciples — The collectors speak of Jesus as the disciples' teacher, a noun that takes no setting span; they are not his opponents, so they speak politely. The Greek "your" is singular; Vietnamese uses the plural "các anh" since Jesus is the teacher of all the disciples.
- [ ] [MAT 17:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/17/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "con cái" for "sons", with no gender span — The English keeps "sons" with no setting; Vietnamese "con cái" means the king's children, the family set against outsiders, which is the point (meaning decision), and "con trai" would narrow it needlessly. The picture still points to the Son in 17:26.
- [ ] [MAT 23:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/23/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "nộp một phần mười" (L), "dâng một phần mười" (B, R); "bạc hà, thì là và rau cần" — Tithing is giving a tenth to God (meaning decision); "bạc hà" (mint) and "thì là" (dill) are known garden herbs. Vietnamese has no everyday name for cumin: "rau cần" is the name familiar from older Vietnamese Bibles, while the botanical name is "thì là Ai Cập"; a reviewer should choose. Readable …
- [ ] [MAT 26:50](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/26/#v50) L/B/R · register · "Này bạn" and "bạn" for Jesus' "Friend" to Judas — As the king's and the landowner's "Friend" in the Vietnamese Matthew 20:13 and 22:12: a courteous address that carries a note of reproach (meaning decision). Jesus no longer says "con" to the disciple who has come to hand him over.
- [ ] [MAT 28:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mat/28/#v9) L/B/R · register · "Chào các con": Jesus calls the women "các con" (also 28:10) — The women are his followers, and the risen Jesus speaks to them as he speaks to his disciples; a reviewer may prefer "các bà", the form the angel uses.

## Mác (25)

- [ ] [MRK 1:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/1/#v2) L/B/R · register · God says "Ta" and speaks to the one who comes as "Con", capitalised, with no setting span — Vietnamese must choose a relation for "you". The meaning decision says the one addressed is Jesus, whom the voice calls "Con" (Son) in 1:11; God speaking to Israel in Exodus 23:20 also speaks to his son (Exodus 4:22). "Ngài" is not how God addresses anyone, and "ngươi" is archaic and cold. A …
- [ ] [MRK 1:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/1/#v8) L/B/R · register · John speaks to the crowds as "các người" — A prophet addressing the whole crowd, men and women, speaks as the style guide has Jesus speak to crowds; "các ông" (used to the envoys in John 1:19-27) would address only men.
- [ ] [MRK 2:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/2/#v18) L/B/R · register · the questioners call Jesus "Thầy", with no setting span — They ask him as a teacher answerable for his disciples (meaning decision), and are not shown as opponents, so they use the respectful noun of address the style guide allows to others who respect him.
- [ ] [MRK 2:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/2/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "đồng lúa", "bông lúa" for the grainfields and heads of grain — The crop was wheat or barley; "lúa" is used in the general sense of grain, while "lúa mì" would name only wheat and "ruộng" suggests wet rice paddies. A reviewer may judge whether "lúa" reads as rice.
- [ ] [MRK 3:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/3/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "linh ô uế" (L, B); "tà linh" (R) for the unclean spirits, and "chúng" for them (whole book) — As in 1:23: "ô uế" keeps Mark's "unclean", the ritual impurity that set them against the holy; Readable says "tà linh", the plain modern word for an evil spirit. "Tà ma" is the older wording, "thần ô uế" the Catholic one. "Chúng" is the ordinary pronoun for hostile beings.
- [ ] [MRK 3:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/3/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "Si-môn người Ca-na-an" (L, B); "người được gọi là 'người Nhiệt Thành'" (R) — Literal and Balanced keep the traditional form of Mark's word. A Vietnamese reader may take "người Ca-na-an" for a Canaanite, which it is not (the meaning decision says it comes from the Aramaic for "zeal"), so Readable says "Nhiệt Thành" (zealous) instead, as the English Readable says "the …
- [ ] [MRK 4:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/4/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "ngụ ngôn" (L, B) for every parable in Mark; Readable says "câu chuyện" where a story is told (chapters 4 and 12), "hình ảnh so sánh" for a picture saying … — One word for Mark's parables at Literal and Balanced, the one most of the Vietnamese drafts of Mark already used. "Ví dụ" (the 1926 word) now means "example", and "ẩn dụ" is a grammar term. "Dụ ngôn" is the other word in current use, and "ngụ ngôn" is also the everyday word for a fable, so a …
- [ ] [MRK 4:41](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/4/#v41) L/B/R · register · "người này" for "this one"; "{{deity_pronoun:ngài|Ngài}}" for "him" — The disciples ask who he is, so "người này" keeps the open question of the Greek; the pronoun that follows is the reverent "Ngài" under the setting, since the answer the story points to is that he does what only God does.
- [ ] [MRK 5:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/5/#v6) L/B/R · register · "tôi" and "ngài" (with the deity_pronoun setting) in the spirit's words — The man speaks for the spirit, which knows who Jesus is and is afraid of him (meaning decisions). Vietnamese must choose a pronoun: "ông" would be too familiar for one who falls down before Jesus, and "Thầy" is what disciples say. "Ngài", the pronoun of fearful respect, carries the setting like the …
- [ ] [MRK 5:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/5/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "Quân Đoàn", capitalised as a name — The Latin army word was understood by Mark's readers: a unit of several thousand soldiers (meaning decision). "Quân đoàn" is the Vietnamese word for such a unit, so the reader hears what the name means; a transliteration like "Lê-giôn" would hide it.
- [ ] [MRK 5:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/5/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "heo" — "Heo" (southern) and "lợn" (northern) are both standard. "Heo" is the word most Protestant Bible readers know from this story; a reviewer may choose "lợn" for the whole Bible.
- [ ] [MRK 5:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/5/#v25) L/B/R · register · "bà" for the woman — Vietnamese marks age in the pronoun. She has been ill for twelve years and has spent all she owned, so "bà", a respectful word for a grown woman, suits her better than "cô" (young woman).
- [ ] [MRK 6:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/6/#v17) L/B/R · register · "ông" for John, "ông ta" for Herod — Both men need a pronoun. John, the righteous prophet, is "ông"; Herod is "ông ta", a little more distant, which also keeps it clear who does what in 6:17-28.
- [ ] [MRK 6:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/6/#v18) L/B/R · register · "vua" for "you" in John's words — Vietnamese addresses a ruler by his title. "Vua" is plain and respectful without the flattery of "bệ hạ", which suits a prophet rebuking a king; the girl, asking a favour, says "bệ hạ" (6:25).
- [ ] [MRK 6:54](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/6/#v54) L/B/R · word_choice · "cáng" — The word is a pallet, the poor man's bed (meaning decision). Here the sick are carried on it, and "cáng" (stretcher) says that plainly; "giường" would suggest a heavy bed.
- [ ] [MRK 7:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/7/#v28) L/B/R · register · "Thưa Chúa" for "Lord" — The English keeps "Lord" here, and the meaning decision says the word is respectful and, for Mark's readers, more than that. "Thưa ông", the style guide's address for people who do not yet know Jesus, would drop that second sense, so this woman, who kneels and trusts his word, says "Chúa". A …
- [ ] [MRK 8:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/8/#v29) L/B/R · register · "Thầy" for "you" in Peter's confession — The disciples call Jesus "Thầy" (Teacher), the style guide's form; Peter names who he is with the title itself. "Chúa" would read the later faith of the church into the address.
- [ ] [MRK 8:33](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/8/#v33) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" for "you" in the rebuke — Jesus addresses Peter as Satan, so the warm "con" he uses for his disciples would clash with the words; "ngươi" is the form used to speak down to an adversary. It is old-fashioned, and a reviewer may prefer "con" to keep Peter in view.
- [ ] [MRK 9:49](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/9/#v49) L/B/R · word_choice · "được ướp muối" (salted), with the neutral "được" — "bị" would make the saying only a punishment; "được" fits the meaning decision that reads it as testing and purifying, as salt is put on a sacrifice. The sense is disputed.
- [ ] [MRK 12:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/12/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "ngụ ngôn" (L, B); "câu chuyện" (R) for parables, as in 4:2 — "Ngụ ngôn" is the everyday word for a story told to teach a lesson, which is what a Gospel parable is; "dụ ngôn" is the Catholic term, "lời ví dụ" (1926) now reads as "example", and "ẩn dụ" is a grammar term (metaphor). The Vietnamese John keeps other words for the different Greek word paroimia …
- [ ] [MRK 12:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/12/#v36) L/B/R · register · God says "Ta" of himself and "{{deity_pronoun:con|Con}}" to David's Lord, both with the deity_pronoun span; "phán" for God speaking — Vietnamese must choose a word for "you" that states a relation: "con" is how God speaks to the king he enthrones (as in Psalm 2), and it takes the span because the English "your/Your" does; "ngươi" sounds distant for one seated at God's right hand. A reviewer may prefer another form.
- [ ] [MRK 12:42](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/12/#v42) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "hai đồng lép-ta, tức là một đồng cô-đơ-ran" (L, and the alternative in B, R); their worth as the B, R default — Literal keeps the coin names in transliteration, as the English keeps "lepta" and "quadrans"; the setting gives their worth by default. The Vietnamese spellings of these coin names are not established, and a reviewer should check them.
- [ ] [MRK 14:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/14/#v6) L/B/R · register · "các con" for those who scolded her, through 14:9 — Vietnamese must choose a relationship that the Greek "you" leaves open. Mark says only "some"; John 12:4 names a disciple, and in Mark Jesus is at a meal with his own, so he is drafted as speaking to them as their teacher. If those who scolded are taken to be other guests, "các người" would fit.
- [ ] [MRK 14:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/14/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · "cám dỗ" for peirasmos — The meaning decision describes a test that could lead them to fall; "cám dỗ" names that danger, where "thử thách" would be any hardship.
- [ ] [MRK 14:62](https://openwordbible.org/vi/mrk/14/#v62) L/B/R · supplied_words · "Ta là [Đấng ấy]" (L); "Chính Ta là Đấng ấy" (B); "Đúng, Ta là Đấng ấy" (R) — Vietnamese cannot end a sentence with "là", so "I am" needs a complement; "Đấng ấy" points back to the high priest's "the Christ, the Son of the Blessed", the plain sense the meaning decision gives. The echo of God's name that some hear in the bare words cannot be kept; the footnoted meaning …

## Lu-ca (31)

- [ ] [LUK 1:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v13) L/B/R · register · The angel speaks to Zechariah as "ông" and calls himself "tôi" (1:19-20) — Zechariah is an old priest, so "ông" is the respectful address. The angel says "tôi", not "ta": "Ta" is kept for God and for Jesus speaking, with its deity_pronoun span, and an angel's "ta" would sound like God speaking.
- [ ] [LUK 1:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v15) L/B/R · register · John in the angel's words is "người" (L, B) and "cậu bé", "cậu" (R) — "người" is the respectful third person a prophecy uses for someone of standing; "nó" is too familiar for a promised prophet. Readable uses the plain word for a boy.
- [ ] [LUK 1:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "báo ... tin vui" for the verb "bring good news" — "Tin Lành" is kept for the gospel as a noun (Mark 1:1). Here an angel announces happy news about a birth, and "tin vui" says that plainly; "tin mừng" would echo the Catholic name for the gospel. Used again in 2:10.
- [ ] [LUK 1:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v26) L/B/R · register · Mary is "cô" in the narrative and in the angel's address (1:28-38) — "cô" is the respectful word for a young unmarried woman. "nàng" is literary and old-fashioned; "bà" suits a married or older woman, as Elizabeth is.
- [ ] [LUK 1:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "Chào cô" for the greeting — The meaning decision reads the ordinary greeting, with an undertone of "rejoice". "Chào" is the ordinary greeting; "Mừng" would echo the Catholic "Kính mừng" and the Ave Maria tradition.
- [ ] [LUK 1:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v38) L/B/R · register · Mary calls the angel "ông" and herself "tôi" — The angel appears as a man and speaks to her as "cô"; "ông" answers that respectfully. "ngài" might suggest she takes him for God.
- [ ] [LUK 1:41](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v41) L/B/R · register · Elizabeth speaks to Mary as "em" and of herself as "tôi" (1:42-45) — Elizabeth is an older relative, so "em" is the natural warm address; "tôi" for herself keeps the humility of 1:43 ("Who am I ...?"), where "chị" would sound like a family chat. A reviewer may prefer another pair.
- [ ] [LUK 1:76](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/1/#v76) L/B/R · word_choice · "rạng đông từ trên cao" (L, B); "một ngày mới bừng sáng ... từ trời cao" (R) — The meaning decision reads the rising light from God, the coming king; "rạng đông" (dawn) keeps the picture without naming the sun.
- [ ] [LUK 2:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/2/#v10) L/B/R · register · The angel speaks to the shepherds as "các anh" and of himself as "tôi" — Shepherds are working men; "các anh" is a friendly, respectful address that fits good news. As with Zechariah (1:13), the angel says "tôi", leaving "Ta" for God.
- [ ] [LUK 2:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/2/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "con trẻ Giê-xu" (L); "Hài Nhi Giê-xu" (B, R) — The Greek says "the child Jesus". "Hài Nhi" is the reverent Vietnamese word for the infant Jesus and serves as the honorific "Đức" does for the grown Jesus; Literal keeps the plain noun.
- [ ] [LUK 2:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/2/#v34) L/B/R · register · Simeon speaks to Mary as "cô" — As the angel does (1:28-38): the respectful word for a young woman. An old man might say "con", but "cô" keeps the solemn tone of a prophecy.
- [ ] [LUK 2:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/2/#v36) L/B/R · word_choice · "An-na" for Anna — The 1926 version spells both Anna and Annas "An-ne"; Annas is "An-ne" in John 18 and Luke 3:2, so Anna takes the form closer to her name and the two stay distinct. A reviewer should confirm.
- [ ] [LUK 2:42](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/2/#v42) L/B · word_choice · "cậu bé Giê-xu" (L, B); "Đức Giê-xu" (R) — The Greek calls him "the boy Jesus", and "cậu bé" carries the respect a bare name would lack, as "Hài Nhi" does in 2:27; Readable uses the usual name.
- [ ] [LUK 3:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/3/#v7) L/B/R · register · John speaks to the crowds as "các người" and of himself as "tôi" — As in Mark 1:8: a prophet addressing a crowd of men and women. To the tax collectors and soldiers (3:13-14), who are men asking him one by one, he says "các anh".
- [ ] [LUK 3:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/3/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "chĩa rê lúa", "sân đạp lúa", "trấu" — The winnowing fork tosses threshed grain into the wind so the chaff blows away; "nia" is a flat basket used for the same work in Vietnam, but the Greek names a fork. "sân đạp lúa" is the floor where grain is threshed.
- [ ] [LUK 3:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/3/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "rao giảng Tin Lành" for the verb "proclaim good news" — Here John's preaching of God's coming salvation is meant, the message Mark calls the gospel, so the word for the gospel is used. In 1:19 and 2:10 an angel brings news of a birth, and "tin vui" is used.
- [ ] [LUK 3:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/3/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · The names in the forms of the Protestant tradition where it has one (Xô-rô-ba-bên, Sa-la-thi-ên, Na-than, Đa-vít, Gie-sê, Ô-bết, Bô-ô, Na-ách-son, A-mi-na-đáp, … — Readers meet these names in the Old Testament; they should look the same there. "Át-min" and "A-rơ-ni" follow the SBLGNT reading (the 1926 version follows another text), "Sa-la" (3:32) and "Sê-la" (3:35) keep apart the two men the English calls Sala and Shelah, and "Giô-suê" is Joshua, as the …
- [ ] [LUK 5:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/5/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "bắt sống người ta" (L); "đánh lưới người" (B); "đi tìm người cho Đức Chúa Trời, thay vì đi bắt cá" (R) — Luke's verb is "catch alive", taking people alive to save them (meaning decision). Literal keeps it; Balanced uses the fishing picture of the parallel Mark 1:17; "bắt người" alone would mean "arrest people". Readable says what the picture means, as Mark's Readable does.
- [ ] [LUK 6:42](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/6/#v42) L/B/R · word_choice · "Bạn ơi" for the address "Brother", and "tôi" and "bạn" in the quoted offer, with no gender span — Spoken to one person, every Vietnamese word for a brother or sister says whether the hearer is a man or a woman and older or younger. "bạn" (friend) says neither and keeps the friendly tone of the polite offer (meaning decision), so it needs no gender span. Matthew 7:4, which has no address, uses …
- [ ] [LUK 7:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/7/#v18) L/B/R · register · "Thầy" for "you" and "chúng tôi" for "we" in John's question, with no setting span (also 7:20) — John asks a real question (meaning decision), so he does not yet speak to Jesus as one who has confessed him Lord; "Thầy" is the respectful noun of address the style guide allows to those who respect him, and takes no span.
- [ ] [LUK 7:30](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/7/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "các chuyên gia luật pháp" (L, B); "các chuyên gia về luật pháp của Đức Chúa Trời" (R) for the lawyers — Luke's word here is "those of the law", different from the scribes ("thầy thông giáo") and from the teachers of the law of 5:17 ("thầy dạy luật pháp"). "luật sư" now means an attorney; "chuyên gia" (expert) says what they were (meaning decision), as the English "experts in the law" does.
- [ ] [LUK 7:37](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/7/#v37) L/B/R · register · "bà" for the woman (7:37-50 in the narrative), "chị ấy" in Jesus' words to Simon, and "con" when Jesus speaks to her — Vietnamese must choose a word that says something of age and relation. Luke gives no age; "bà" is the neutral respectful word for a grown woman in narrative; Jesus speaks of her to Simon as "chị ấy", a plain courteous word between them, and to her as "con", as he does to the woman in Mark 5:34.
- [ ] [LUK 8:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/8/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "ác linh" (L, B); "tà linh" (R) for the evil spirits; "Ma-đơ-len", "Gian-nơ", "Chu-xa", "Su-xan-nơ" — Luke's word here is "evil", not "unclean", so Literal and Balanced say "ác linh" and keep "linh ô uế" for the unclean spirits (8:29); Readable uses "tà linh" for both, as Mark's Readable does. The names follow the Protestant transliterations; a reviewer may prefer the newer "Gian-na", "Su-xan-na".
- [ ] [LUK 8:55](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/8/#v55) L/B/R · word_choice · "linh hồn" (L, B); "sự sống" (R) for her spirit — The meaning decision gives her breath of life; "linh hồn" is the ordinary word for the life-giving spirit that leaves at death and returns here, and Readable says "life", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [LUK 11:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/11/#v34) L/B/R · register · "anh" for the singular "you" in 11:34-36 — The Greek turns to the singular, a saying aimed at each hearer in the crowd. As in Matthew 12:37, "anh" is the plain form for one grown hearer who is not a disciple; "con" is kept for the disciples.
- [ ] [LUK 12:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/12/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "anh tôi" for "my brother" — Vietnamese must say whether a brother is older or younger. The one who held the estate and would not share it was most likely the elder, so the man says "anh tôi"; the Greek does not say.
- [ ] [LUK 12:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/12/#v20) L/B/R · register · "phán" for God speaking, and "ngươi" for the man he rebukes — "phán" is kept for God speaking (style guide). God speaks to the fool as judge; "ngươi" is the traditional word for that and carries the severity of the rebuke, though it is otherwise avoided as archaic.
- [ ] [LUK 14:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/14/#v24) L/B/R · register · lowercase "ta", the master's word, with no deity_pronoun span; "các người" for the plural "you" — The meaning decision says the master is still speaking although "you" is plural; keeping the master's "ta" leaves the footnoted question open instead of turning the sentence into Jesus' own words.
- [ ] [LUK 16:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/16/#v7) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "hộc" for the cor (L); {{units:Khoảng 16 tấn|Một trăm hộc}} (B, R) — Vietnamese measures grain by weight, so the metric default gives tonnes: the English's 600 bushels of wheat is about 16 tonnes, cut by a fifth (meaning decision). Literal keeps the Protestant unit name "hộc". The size of a cor is uncertain, so the figures are approximate.
- [ ] [LUK 19:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/19/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "cây sung" at every level — The sycamore-fig is a fig with low, wide branches (meaning decision). "cây sung", the cluster fig Vietnamese readers know, is its near relative and the familiar Protestant word here; "cây vả" is kept for the common fig (Luke 13:6; 21:29).
- [ ] [LUK 24:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/luk/24/#v17) L/B/R · register · Jesus to the two on the road: "các anh" (24:17-26); "các con" from 24:38 — While they do not know him, Jesus walks with them as a fellow traveller, and "các anh" is how a stranger speaks to grown men. Once he stands among the disciples as himself (24:38), he says "các con", as in John 20:19. A reviewer may prefer "các con" throughout.

## Giăng (71)

- [ ] [JHN 1:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/1/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · exclusive "chúng tôi" (we, not including the reader) for the eyewitnesses — Vietnamese must choose between "we" with or without the hearer. The meaning decision reads "we beheld" as an eyewitness claim, so "chúng tôi"; "among us" follows it. In 1:16 "we all received" includes every believer and is "chúng ta".
- [ ] [JHN 1:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/1/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đấng Tiên Tri" for the Prophet, capitalised — The expected prophet like Moses is a title, as "the Prophet" is in English; "Đấng" and the capitals mark it off from an ordinary "tiên tri" (as Isaiah is in 1:23). For a reviewer to confirm for 6:14 and 7:40.
- [ ] [JHN 1:38](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/1/#v38) L/B/R · register · Jesus calls the newcomers "các anh" and "anh" (1:38-51) — The style guide gives "các con" for Jesus speaking to his disciples. In chapter 1 these men are strangers and then new followers, so Jesus speaks to them as adult equals, "anh" (Simon, Nathanael) and "các anh" (1:38, 1:51). A reviewer may prefer to move to "con" as soon as they follow him.
- [ ] [JHN 1:40](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/1/#v40) L/B/R · word_choice · "người anh em" (brother) without saying older or younger — Vietnamese normally says whether a brother is older or younger; the Gospel does not say, so "người anh em" ("anh em ruột" at Readable) leaves it open, as in 1:41.
- [ ] [JHN 1:45](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/1/#v45) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đức Giê-xu" also in Philip's words; "Na-xa-rét" for Nazareth — The honorific stays with the name in speech as in narrative, since Philip already follows him. Nazareth is not in the guide's list; this is the Protestant form.
- [ ] [JHN 1:51](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/1/#v51) L/B/R · word_choice · plural "các anh" needs no brackets; "Con Người" capitalised; "thiên sứ" for angels — Vietnamese marks the plural in the pronoun itself, so nothing is supplied. "Con Người" is Jesus's title, never under the gender setting. "thiên sứ" (heavenly messenger) is the Protestant word; not in the guide's table.
- [ ] [JHN 2:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/2/#v4) B/R · register · Jesus calls himself "con" to his mother in Balanced and Readable (also "Giờ của con" in the next sentence); Literal keeps the book-wide "Ta" — Once she is addressed as "mẹ", Vietnamese requires the son to call himself "con"; "Ta" to one's mother sounds arrogant. Literal keeps "Ta" with "bà". A reviewer should decide whether "Ta" should give way to "con" at every level here and in 19:26.
- [ ] [JHN 2:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/2/#v5) L/B/R · register · "Ngài" for Jesus in his mother's words to the servants — She speaks of her son to the household servants. "Anh ấy" or "cậu ấy" would be natural for an ordinary son, but would set Jesus lower than the narrator does in the same verse; "Ngài" keeps the book-wide pronoun and the deity_pronoun setting.
- [ ] [JHN 2:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/2/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "thùng" for the Greek measure (metretes) — "Thùng" (a barrel or tub) is a plain Vietnamese word for a liquid measure; a transliteration would mean nothing to readers, and the footnote gives the size.
- [ ] [JHN 2:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/2/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "dấu lạ" for sign (whole book) — "Phép lạ" means miracle and loses John's point that the deed points beyond itself. "Dấu chỉ" is closer to "sign" but is mostly Catholic usage; "dấu lạ" is familiar to Protestant readers and keeps the idea of a sign. A reviewer should weigh "dấu chỉ".
- [ ] [JHN 3:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/3/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "sinh ra từ trên cao"; Readable adds "một lần nữa" — No Vietnamese word means both "from above" and "again". "Từ trên cao" gives the sense Jesus means; Nicodemus's "lần thứ hai" in verse 4 shows the other hearing, and the footnote explains. Readable keeps both senses by saying both.
- [ ] [JHN 3:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/3/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "vương quốc của Đức Chúa Trời" for the kingdom of God (also 3:3) — "Vương quốc" is the modern word for a kingdom and makes clear that God's reign is meant; "nước" is traditional but today first means a country or water, which is confusing beside "sinh ra bởi nước" in this very verse.
- [ ] [JHN 3:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/3/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "chúng tôi" (we, not including the hearer) — Vietnamese must say whether "we" includes the person spoken to. Nicodemus and "you people" are set against this "we", so it cannot be "chúng ta"; who else is included is left open, as the meaning decision says.
- [ ] [JHN 3:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/3/#v26) L/B/R · register · John's disciples call John "thầy" and speak of Jesus as "người ấy" / "ông ấy", with no deity_pronoun spans — Vietnamese pronouns carry the speaker's attitude. These disciples avoid naming Jesus and see him as a rival to their teacher, so "Ngài" would put reverence in their mouths that the scene does not have; "ông ấy" is polite but neutral. "Thầy" (lowercase as a pronoun) is how disciples speak to their …
- [ ] [JHN 4:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/4/#v7) L/B/R · register · forms of address in 4:7-26: Jesus says "Ta" for himself and "chị" to the woman; she says "tôi" and calls him "ông"; the narrator calls her "chị ấy" — Vietnamese must name the relation. "chị" is respectful and warm toward a woman of uncertain age; she, meeting a Jewish stranger, uses the polite "ông". Kept through the scene so the relationship does not shift.
- [ ] [JHN 4:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/4/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "nước sống" — "nước sống" can be heard as fresh water and as water that gives life, so the woman's misunderstanding in 4:11 still works; "nước hằng sống" would give away the spiritual sense at once.
- [ ] [JHN 4:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/4/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "các người" (L); "dân chị" (B); "chị và dân tộc chị" (R) for the plural "you"; likewise in 4:22 — Vietnamese "chị" is singular; the plural must be shown. "các chị" would mean a group of women, so Literal uses the general plural "các người" and the other levels name her people.
- [ ] [JHN 4:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/4/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "trong tâm linh và chân lý" — "tâm linh" is the spirit, lowercase, not the Holy Spirit; it shares "linh" with "thần linh" in 4:24, though less plainly than the Greek, which uses one word. "chân lý" is the book's key term for truth.
- [ ] [JHN 4:54](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/4/#v54) L/B/R · word_choice · "dấu lạ" — The style guide's term for sign: never "phép lạ" (miracle), since John's word points beyond the deed; "dấu chỉ" is closer to "sign" and is left for a reviewer.
- [ ] [JHN 6:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/6/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "năm nghìn" — The style guide writes numbers in words in running text. "Nghìn" and "ngàn" are both standard; "nghìn" is used here, and a reviewer may prefer the southern "ngàn" for the whole book.
- [ ] [JHN 6:33](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/6/#v33) L/B/R · word_choice · "[bánh] từ trời xuống" (L); "bánh từ trời xuống" (B, R) — Vietnamese cannot leave the Greek participle open: "Đấng" would name a person, "bánh" the bread. Every level follows meaning decision m1 (the bread), which the crowd's request in 6:34 supports; the footnote there gives the personal reading.
- [ ] [JHN 6:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/6/#v34) L/B/R · word_choice · "Thưa Thầy" — The style guide gives "thưa ông" for kyrie from people who do not yet know Jesus. Here the same crowd has just called him "Ra-bi" and "Thầy" (6:25-30); switching to "ông" would change the relationship within one scene. "Thưa Thầy" is still a polite address, not the confession "Chúa" (meaning …
- [ ] [JHN 6:41](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/6/#v41) L/B/R · register · "Giê-xu" without "Đức", and "ông ta", in the mouths of those who object to him — The style guide gives Jesus the honorific "Đức" at every level. Here the speakers are doubting him and naming him as a neighbour's son, and "Đức" in their mouths would say the opposite of what they mean; the guide's "ông ta" for opponents goes with it. The narrator keeps "Đức Giê-xu". A reviewer …
- [ ] [JHN 6:63](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/6/#v63) L/B · word_choice · "là thần linh và là sự sống" (L, B); "đến từ Thánh Linh" (R) — The style guide uses lowercase "thần linh" where "spirit" is not the Holy Spirit. Here the words are "spirit" in the sense that they belong to the Spirit (meaning decision), and "thần linh" can also mean "gods" or "spirits" to a new reader, so a reviewer should weigh "linh" or "thuộc về Thánh …
- [ ] [JHN 7:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/7/#v3) L/B/R · register · "anh em" for his brothers; they call him "anh" (elder brother) — Vietnamese must say whether a brother is older or younger. "anh em" for the group leaves the relationship open, as the footnote on "brothers" does; in their speech they call Jesus "anh", since he is presented as the firstborn (Luke 2:7) and they speak to him as family, not as disciples. "anh" is a …
- [ ] [JHN 7:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/7/#v6) L/B/R · register · "các em" for his brothers; "Ta" for himself — The guide's "các người" is for crowds and opponents and would sound hostile to his own family; "các em" matches the brothers' "anh" in verse 3. Jesus keeps "Ta" for himself, as everywhere in the book, so his voice does not change from scene to scene.
- [ ] [JHN 7:40](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/7/#v40) L/B/R · word_choice · Đấng Tiên Tri — The Prophet like Moses is a promised figure, so the title is capitalised and takes the respectful "Đấng"; "Vị Tiên Tri" is a little less weighty and may suit a reviewer better, since the speakers do not yet see him as divine.
- [ ] [JHN 8:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/8/#v6) L/B/R · register · "các người" for the accusers; "chị ấy" for the woman — Jesus speaks to opponents as "các người", as the guide sets. For the woman Vietnamese must choose an age word: "chị" fits an adult woman who is not old and is respectful without distance; "người phụ nữ" rather than "người đàn bà" avoids the slight coldness the older word has today.
- [ ] [JHN 8:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/8/#v10) L/B/R · register · "chị" for the woman when Jesus speaks to her; "Ta" for himself — "chị" is respectful and warm without the distance of "bà" or the condescension "con" could carry here; it matches the narrator's "chị". A reviewer may prefer another age word.
- [ ] [JHN 8:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/8/#v24) L/B/R · supplied_words · Ta là Đấng Hằng Hữu (bracketed at Literal; also 8:28) — Vietnamese "là" cannot stand without a complement, so the bare "I am" the English keeps is not possible. "Đấng Hằng Hữu" (the Ever-Existing One) echoes the familiar Vietnamese wording of Exodus 3:14 and so keeps the claim the meaning decision describes; "Ta là Đấng ấy" (I am he) would be …
- [ ] [JHN 8:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/8/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · đưa ... lên cao — John's word means both raised up on the cross and exalted. "treo lên" (hang up) says only the first and "tôn cao" (exalt) only the second; "đưa lên cao" (raise up high) leaves room for both, as the English "lift up" does. The same wording should be used at 3:14 and 12:32-34.
- [ ] [JHN 8:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/8/#v31) L/B/R · register · Jesus calls his hearers "các người" and himself "Ta"; they call him "ông" and themselves "chúng tôi" (8:33-59) — The style guide's forms for Jesus speaking to crowds and opponents. These hearers had just believed, so a warmer "các anh" is possible here, but the same conversation turns hostile within a few verses and Vietnamese cannot change the relationship mid-scene.
- [ ] [JHN 8:58](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/8/#v58) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ta Hằng Hữu" (I ever exist) at every level — The guide keeps "I am" in the present at every level so that the echo of God's name stays. Vietnamese "là" cannot end a sentence, so a bare "Ta là" reads as cut off; "hằng hữu" (ever existing) keeps the present tense and echoes God's name in Exodus 3:14 ("Đấng Hằng Hữu"), the form used for "I am" …
- [ ] [JHN 9:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/9/#v11) L/B/R · register · "Người tên là Giê-xu" and "ông ấy", without the honorific "Đức" — The guide puts "Đức" before the name at every level, but here the name is in the mouth of a man who knows nothing about Jesus except his name; the honorific would have him confess what he only comes to see in 9:38. The narrator keeps "Đức Giê-xu".
- [ ] [JHN 9:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/9/#v18) L/B/R · register · the authorities call the parents "ông bà" — Polite but official; "các người" would sound rude to elderly parents in modern Vietnamese.
- [ ] [JHN 9:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/9/#v24) L/B/R · register · the leaders call the man "anh" and themselves "chúng tôi"; they call Jesus "người này", "ông ta"; the man calls Jesus "ông ấy" — The forms used through 9:13-34. Vietnamese speakers in a quarrel often drop to "mày"; it was not used here so that the scorn is carried by the words ("dám", "người đó") rather than by a coarse pronoun.
- [ ] [JHN 9:35](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/9/#v35) L/B/R · register · Jesus calls the man "anh" — An adult of unknown age; "anh" is the plain respectful form, as the neighbours and the leaders also use. "Con" would make Jesus his elder or teacher before the man has come to faith.
- [ ] [JHN 10:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/10/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "người chăn tốt lành" for the good shepherd, at every level — kalos here means the true, worthy shepherd, the model of a shepherd; "hiền lành" (gentle) is the familiar wording but narrows the sense, and "mục tử nhân lành" is the Catholic form. A reviewer may prefer the familiar title.
