- [ ] [JHN 10:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/10/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "lễ Cung Hiến" for the Dedication; R adds "lễ Ha-nu-ca" — "Cung Hiến" is the usual Vietnamese church word for a dedication; "Ha-nu-ca" transliterates Hanukkah in the hyphenated style of the guide's names. Neither is in the style guide.
- [ ] [JHN 10:41](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/10/#v41) L/B/R · word_choice · "dấu lạ" for sign — The style guide's key term; never "phép lạ" (miracle). "dấu chỉ" is closer to "sign" and is left for a reviewer, as at 2:11.
- [ ] [JHN 11:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/11/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "chị" (elder sister) for Martha, "em gái" for Mary (11:5) — Vietnamese must say whether a sister is older or younger, which the Greek does not. The draft follows the usual view that Martha, the hostess of Luke 10:38-40, was the elder; the neutral "chị em" reads awkwardly in speech.
- [ ] [JHN 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/11/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "anh" (elder brother) for Lazarus, here and in the sisters' speech ("anh con") — The Greek does not say whether Lazarus was older or younger than his sisters; Vietnamese must choose. "anh" follows the form most Vietnamese readers know from the 1926 version; the ages are unknown.
- [ ] [JHN 11:25](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/11/#v25) L/B/R · register · "Con" for Martha in Jesus's question — Style guide section 6 allows "con" for one person where relation requires; Jesus speaks to Martha as a teacher to a believer who calls herself "con".
- [ ] [JHN 11:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/11/#v28) L/B/R · register · "em" for Mary in her elder sister's words — An elder sister calls her younger sister "em"; this follows the choice of Martha as the elder (11:1).
- [ ] [JHN 11:39](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/11/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "em gái" for Martha as Lazarus's sister — Follows the choice of Lazarus as the elder brother (11:2), which the Greek does not settle.
- [ ] [JHN 11:40](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/11/#v40) L/B/R · register · "con" for Martha, as in 11:23-26 — Jesus keeps the form he used with Martha earlier in the chapter.
- [ ] [JHN 12:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/12/#v4) L/B/R · register · "Giu-đa", "ông ta" for Judas in the narrative — Many Vietnamese versions call Judas "hắn", which condemns him in the pronoun itself; the narrator here uses neutral forms and lets the story judge him.
- [ ] [JHN 12:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/12/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "các thầy tế lễ cả" (L, B); "các thầy tế lễ đứng đầu" (R) — The chief priests, named as in 11:47.
- [ ] [JHN 12:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/12/#v15) L/B/R · register · "ngươi" / "các ngươi" for Zion addressed in the prophecy — The prophet speaks for God to the city; "ngươi" is the usual form for that solemn address in Vietnamese Bibles and is not rude in this register.
- [ ] [JHN 12:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/12/#v24) L/B/R · register · "các con" for Jesus's hearers in verses 24-26 — Jesus is answering Andrew and Philip, so he speaks as to his disciples; the Greeks may also be listening, and from verse 30 he addresses the crowd as "các người".
- [ ] [JHN 12:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/12/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "được nâng lên" — Vietnamese has no single verb that means both "hung on a cross" and "exalted"; "được nâng lên" keeps the exaltation and the physical raising, and verse 33 names the cross. "bị treo lên" would lose the exaltation.
- [ ] [JHN 13:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/13/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ta là [Đấng Hằng Hữu]" (L); "Ta là Đấng Hằng Hữu" (B); "Ta chính là Đấng Hằng Hữu" (R) — Vietnamese "là" cannot stand without a complement, so a bare "Ta là" is not a sentence. "Hằng Hữu" ("the ever-existing one") is the Vietnamese Bible's wording for God's name in Exodus 3:14 and keeps the echo the meaning decision describes; "Ta là Đấng ấy" would give only the plain sense. The same …
- [ ] [JHN 15:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/15/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · còn lại (for the fruit) — Meno is "ở lại" or "ở trong" where people remain in Jesus; for fruit Vietnamese needs "còn lại" (last, stay). Balanced and Readable add "lâu dài" (lasting) to make the sense plain.
- [ ] [JHN 15:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/15/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · yêu (for phileo) — Both Greek verbs mean "love". The world's fondness for its own is given the plain "yêu"; "yêu thương" is kept for the love Jesus commands. No difference of meaning is intended.
- [ ] [JHN 18:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/18/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · Ta là [người đó] (L); Chính Ta đây (B); Ta đây (R) — Vietnamese cannot leave "I am" without a complement, so the bare words that echo God's name cannot be kept; the meaning decision's footnote gives that echo. "Ta đây" (here I am) is how a person answers to his name, and "chính" stresses that he is the very one. Also verses 6 and 8.
- [ ] [JHN 18:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/18/#v29) L/B/R · register · Pilate to the accusers: "tôi" for himself, "các ông" for them; Jesus is "người này" or "ông ta" in their talk — Vietnamese must choose pronouns that show the relation. A Roman governor speaking to the Jewish leaders is formal but not insulting: "tôi" / "các ông". "ta" / "các ngươi" is the old mandarin register and sounds archaic. Kept through John 18:28-19:16.
- [ ] [JHN 18:33](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/18/#v33) L/B/R · register · Pilate to Jesus: "anh" for "you", "tôi" for himself; no deity_pronoun span — A governor questioning a prisoner speaks down to him but not crudely: "anh". "ngươi" is archaic; "ông" would grant more respect than Pilate shows. Since "anh" is Pilate's word, not the narrator's deity pronoun, it takes no capitalisation span where the English "You" has one. Kept through …
- [ ] [JHN 18:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/18/#v36) L/B/R · word_choice · "vương quốc" — "nước" is the traditional word but today reads first as "country"; "vương quốc" keeps the idea of a king's rule. The meaning decision notes that kingship, not territory, is meant.
- [ ] [JHN 19:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "người ấy" (L); "người này đây" (B, R) — "con người" would be the closest word for "the man", but it could be confused with the title "Con Người" (Son of Man), which John does not use here.
- [ ] [JHN 19:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v8) L/B/R · register · Pilate to Jesus: "anh", himself "tôi" (19:8-11) — As in 18:33: a governor to a prisoner, neither archaic nor deferential. "anh" is Pilate's word and takes no deity_pronoun span.
- [ ] [JHN 19:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v19) L/B/R · register · "Giê-xu" without "Đức" in the wording of Pilate's notice — The style guide gives "Đức Giê-xu" at every level because a bare name is disrespectful. That is the narrator's voice; the notice is Pilate's charge against a condemned man, and Pilate would not give him the honorific. The narrator keeps "Đức Giê-xu".
- [ ] [JHN 19:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v21) L/B · register · "Tôi là Vua ..." in the priests' report of his claim — The priests put the claim in Jesus's mouth in their own words; they would not give him the "Ta" that the narrative uses when Jesus himself speaks.
- [ ] [JHN 19:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v24) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "tôi" in the psalm, with no deity_pronoun span — The words are the psalmist's lament (Psalm 22), and a Vietnamese lament says "tôi". "Ta" is how Jesus speaks of himself with authority; it would sound wrong in a cry of distress. The English "my" carries a span; "tôi" takes none.
- [ ] [JHN 19:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "kinh giới" for hyssop — "kinh giới" is the name Vietnamese Bibles have given the hyssop of Exodus 12:22, and keeping it keeps the Passover link the meaning decision notes. The Vietnamese herb of that name is not the same plant, so a reviewer should confirm the term.
- [ ] [JHN 19:30](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "trao thần linh", lowercase — "trút linh hồn" is the familiar idiom for dying but speaks of the soul and of letting go. "trao" keeps the Greek "handed over" (he gives up his life himself), and lowercase "thần linh" keeps "spirit" as the style guide does where it is not the Holy Spirit, leaving room for the hint the meaning …
- [ ] [JHN 19:35](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v35) L/B/R · register · "anh chị em" for "you", the readers — Vietnamese needs a word that sets the writer's relation to the readers. "anh chị em" is how a Christian writer addresses fellow believers and the wider readership; "quý vị" is formal and distant, "các bạn" casual. This is not the gender setting (the Greek has no "brothers" here), so it takes no …
- [ ] [JHN 19:39](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/19/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "một dược" and "trầm hương" — "một dược" is the established word for myrrh. For the second spice, "trầm hương" (aloeswood, a fragrant wood) is the likely sense; "lư hội" is the aloe plant used as a medicine.
- [ ] [JHN 20:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/20/#v13) L/B/R · register · "Hỡi bà" (L, B); "Bà ơi" (R); Mary is "bà" throughout — The Greek "Woman" is respectful. "Hỡi bà" is a dignified vocative; "Này bà" would sound curt and "Hỡi người đàn bà" cold. Readable uses the everyday warm "Bà ơi", as the English has "Dear woman". The same in 20:15.
- [ ] [JHN 20:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/20/#v31) L/B/R · register · "anh chị em" for the readers ("you" plural) — The narrator addresses the readers directly; Vietnamese must choose a relation, and "anh chị em" is the warm, inclusive form used in churches. Plain text, not a gender span, since the Greek has no "brothers" here.
- [ ] [JHN 21:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/21/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "yêu" for both agapan and philein in 21:15-17 — Follows the meaning decision that the two verbs mean the same here; using one Vietnamese verb keeps that reading. A reviewer may prefer to mark the change of verb.
- [ ] [JHN 21:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/jhn/21/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "yêu" again, though Jesus now uses philein — Same verb as in verse 15, following the meaning decision; see the decision there.

## Công Vụ Các Sứ Đồ (42)

- [ ] [ACT 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/2/#v14) L/B/R · register · Peter says "tôi" for himself, "chúng tôi" for the apostles, "chúng ta" where his hearers are included, and "anh em" for the crowd he speaks to (2:14-40; also … — "anh em" is the ordinary Vietnamese "you" for a speaker addressing a crowd of his own people, and fits both forms of the address. It is not under the gender setting, which is kept for the formal address.
- [ ] [ACT 3:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/3/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đầy Tớ" capitalised for God's servant Jesus (also 3:26; 4:27, 4:30) — The word is "pais", the servant of Isaiah (meaning decision), not "doulos", so it is "đầy tớ" at every level; the capital marks the title, as "Đấng Thánh" and "Đấng Công Chính" in 3:14.
- [ ] [ACT 5:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/5/#v3) L/B/R · register · Peter speaks to Ananias as "anh" (5:3-4) and to Sapphira as "chị" (5:8-9); the narrator calls them "ông" and "bà" — Peter speaks as the leader of the believers to members of the church; "anh" and "chị" are plain and close, and keep the warning personal. The narrator keeps the respectful third person it uses for grown men and women.
- [ ] [ACT 5:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/5/#v36) L/B/R · register · "Thêu-đa"; Gamaliel speaks of Theudas and Judas the Galilean as "hắn" — Gamaliel names them as leaders of failed revolts (meaning decisions) and speaks of them with the distance a council member would; "hắn" is the Vietnamese third person for a man one does not respect.
- [ ] [ACT 6:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/6/#v5) L · supplied_words · "các sứ đồ đặt tay" (L) — The Greek leaves open who prayed and laid on hands (meaning decision); Vietnamese "họ" would point back to the whole gathering, so Literal names the apostles as the nearer reading, and Balanced keeps "họ".
- [ ] [ACT 7:44](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/7/#v44) L/B/R · word_choice · "Lều Chứng Cớ" for the tent of the testimony — The tabernacle (meaning decision). Stephen sets the tent against the house Solomon built (7:47), so a word for "tent" is kept; "Đền Tạm" would call it a temple.
- [ ] [ACT 8:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/8/#v18) L/B/R · register · Simon says "tôi" and speaks to the apostles as "các ông" (8:19, 8:24); Peter answers him as "ông" (8:20-23) — Simon is a well-known man of the city, not yet one the apostles know as a brother; "ông" keeps a stern distance without contempt, unlike the "anh" Peter used for Ananias, a member of the church (5:3).
- [ ] [ACT 8:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/8/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "Thánh Linh phán" (L, B); "nói" (R) for the Spirit speaking (also 10:19; 11:12; 13:2) — As in the Vietnamese 1 Timothy 4:1: "phán" for God speaking at Literal and Balanced, with Readable's plainer verb.
- [ ] [ACT 9:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/9/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đạo", capitalised, for "the Way" at every level (for the whole book: 19:9, 19:23, 22:4, 24:14, 24:22) — A name for the movement of those who follow Jesus (meaning decision). The Sino-Vietnamese "đạo" means both a way and a faith one follows, so it carries the picture and the sense together; the capital marks it as a name, and Readable explains it as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [ACT 9:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/9/#v3) L/B/R · register · The risen Jesus says "Ta" and speaks to Saul as "anh" (9:4-6); Saul answers "Thưa Chúa" and "Ngài" — Jesus speaks to one man as "anh" in the Vietnamese Luke (5:14, 18:41), and the retellings of this scene in 22:7-10 and 26:14-18 use "anh" too. To Ananias and to Peter, who are his disciples, he says "con" (9:10-16; 10:13-15).
- [ ] [ACT 9:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/9/#v34) L/B/R · register · Peter speaks to Aeneas as "ông" — A man who has lain sick for eight years and whom Peter meets for the first time; the courteous "ông" is used, as Philip speaks to the Ethiopian (8:30).
- [ ] [ACT 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/10/#v13) L/B/R · register · "tiếng phán" (L), "tiếng nói" (B, R); the voice gives commands with no pronoun, and Peter answers "Lạy Chúa" and "con" — The voice is the Lord's, from heaven (meaning decision); Literal marks it with "phán", as the voice from heaven in the Vietnamese Luke 3:22.
- [ ] [ACT 10:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/10/#v27) L/B/R · register · Peter speaks to Cornelius's household as "quý vị" and says "tôi", "chúng tôi" for himself and the apostles (10:28-43); Cornelius speaks to Peter as "ông" and … — Peter speaks to Gentile strangers who have gathered to hear him; "quý vị" is the courteous modern Vietnamese "you" for such an audience of men and women. The "anh em" of his speeches to Israel would claim a kinship Peter is only now learning (10:28).
- [ ] [ACT 10:45](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/10/#v45) L · word_choice · "ân tứ" (L); "món quà" (B, R) for the gift of the Spirit — The gift that is the Spirit himself (meaning decision), as in 2:38 and 8:20, where Balanced and Readable say "món quà".
- [ ] [ACT 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/11/#v2) L/B/R · register · The Jewish believers speak to Peter as "ông"; Peter answers with "tôi" and "chúng ta" (11:4-17) — They are fellow believers finding fault with a leader of the church; "ông" keeps the respect due to him while they argue.
- [ ] [ACT 12:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/12/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "Gia-cơ, anh của Giăng" — James son of Zebedee (meaning decision) is named before John in the Gospels' lists and is commonly held to be the elder; Vietnamese must choose.
- [ ] [ACT 13:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/13/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "giáo sư" for teachers in the church — The word the Vietnamese church uses for those who teach in a congregation; "thầy" is kept for Jesus as Teacher.
- [ ] [ACT 13:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/13/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sê-giút Phao-lút", kept apart from "Phao-lô" — The proconsul's name and the apostle's are the same Latin name in Greek; Vietnamese gives the proconsul the Latin form so the reader does not confuse the two men in 13:7-9.
- [ ] [ACT 13:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/13/#v32) L/B · word_choice · "cho chúng ta là con cháu họ" (L); "cho con cháu chúng ta" (B, R) — The text reads "to our children" (meaning decision), meaning Paul's generation as heirs of the fathers; Literal makes that plain, the other levels keep the Greek's wording.
- [ ] [ACT 13:46](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/13/#v46) L/B/R · register · Paul and Barnabas speak to the Jews who oppose them as "các ông" — The "anh em" of the synagogue speech gives way to the distant "các ông" when the hearers reject the message, as the apostles speak to the council.
- [ ] [ACT 14:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/14/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "thần Dớt", "thần Héc-mê" — The chief Greek god and his messenger (meaning decision), in the forms modern Vietnamese uses for the Greek gods; the 1926 version gave their Latin names.
- [ ] [ACT 16:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/16/#v18) L/B/R · register · Paul says "ta" and "ngươi" to the spirit — A command with authority to an evil spirit, not speech between people; "ta ... ngươi" is the form of a master to a subordinate, as Jesus' commands to spirits read in the Vietnamese Gospels.
- [ ] [ACT 17:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/17/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "phái Ê-pi-cu-rơ" and "phái Khắc Kỷ" — "Khắc Kỷ" (self-mastery) is the usual Vietnamese name for the Stoic school, and "Ê-pi-cu-rơ" transliterates Epicurus. A reviewer should check both forms.
- [ ] [ACT 18:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/18/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sốt-then" for Sosthenes — The Protestant transliteration; a reviewer should check it against the form chosen for 1 Corinthians 1:1.
- [ ] [ACT 19:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/19/#v20) L · word_order · "nhờ quyền năng của Chúa, lời [Chúa]" (L), following the Greek order — The meaning decision joins "of the Lord" to "the word"; Literal keeps the Greek order, in which the words stand next to "might", and supplies "[Chúa]" so that the word is still the Lord's.
- [ ] [ACT 19:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/19/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ác-tê-mi", "thần Ác-tê-mi" (L), "nữ thần Ác-tê-mi" (B, R) for Artemis — The goddess's Greek name, transliterated; "Đi-anh" is the Latin Diana of older Vietnamese versions. Balanced and Readable say she was a goddess, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [ACT 20:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/20/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sô-ba-tê", "Bi-ru", "Sê-cun-đu"; "Ti-chi-cơ" and "Trô-phim" as in Colossians 4:7 and 2 Timothy 4:20 — Protestant transliterations; a reviewer should check the three names not found in the published letters.
- [ ] [ACT 20:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/20/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-sốt", "Mi-ty-len", "Chi-ô", "Sa-mốt", "Mi-lê" — Protestant transliterations; "Mi-lê" as in 2 Timothy 4:20. "A-sốt" for Assos is spelled like the Philistine Ashdod in some versions, which the context keeps apart; a reviewer may prefer "A-sơ".
- [ ] [ACT 21:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/21/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "Bê-tô-lê-mai" for Ptolemais — The Protestant transliteration; a reviewer should check it.
- [ ] [ACT 22:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/22/#v3) L/B/R · register · "quý vị" for "you" in Paul's defense to the crowd (22:3-21) — The speech is a formal defense before a hostile crowd; after the address of 22:1, "quý vị" keeps the respect Paul shows them, while the address itself calls them brothers.
- [ ] [ACT 22:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/22/#v6) L/B/R · register · "Sau-lơ"; the verb for the Lord speaking is "nói" (22:7-21); the Lord says "anh" to Saul the persecutor (22:7-8) and "con" once Saul offers to obey (22:10-21); … — Jesus speaks to one person as "anh" or "con" as the relation requires (style guide); Saul is first an opponent, then a disciple who asks what he must do, so the address changes with him. The same choice is made in 26:14-18.
- [ ] [ACT 23:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/23/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "con trai của chị Phao-lô"; "cậu" for him — Vietnamese must choose an older or a younger sister; "chị" is chosen with no ground in the text, and a reviewer may prefer "chị em gái" (sister) to leave it open. "cậu" for the young man, as in 20:9.
- [ ] [ACT 23:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/23/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "An-ti-ba-tri" for Antipatris — The Protestant transliteration; a reviewer should check it.
- [ ] [ACT 23:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/23/#v34) L/B/R · register · the governor says "tôi" for himself and "ông" to Paul (Acts 23-26) — As Pilate speaks of himself as "tôi" in Luke 23 and John 19. Paul is a Roman citizen of standing, sent with a letter from the tribune, so Felix speaks to him as "ông", not as Pilate to a common prisoner.
- [ ] [ACT 26:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/26/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · exclusive "chúng tôi" for "our religion" before the king — Agrippa was of Jewish faith, but Paul speaks before a Roman court of Gentiles as well, and Balanced follows the English "our"; a reviewer may prefer the inclusive "chúng ta" in Paul's address to Agrippa.
- [ ] [ACT 26:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/26/#v9) L/B/R · register · "Giê-xu người Na-xa-rét" with no "Đức" as Paul tells how he once thought — Paul speaks of Jesus as he then saw him; he names him "Chúa" when he tells of meeting him (26:15).
- [ ] [ACT 26:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/26/#v14) L/B/R · register · the Lord's words to Saul as in 22:7-8: "anh" to Saul, "Ta" for himself, through 26:18 — The same choice as in the first telling: the Lord speaks to Saul, still his persecutor, as "anh"; here Paul does not tell his own surrender, so "anh" is kept through the commission of 26:16-18.
- [ ] [ACT 27:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/27/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "Giu-lơ"; "đội quân Hoàng Gia" for the Augustan cohort — A unit with the emperor's title of honor (meaning decision); "Hoàng Gia" (imperial) says that in Vietnamese. "Giu-lơ" is the Protestant form of Julius.
- [ ] [ACT 27:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/27/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "A-tra-mít" for Adramyttium; the place names of the voyage in Protestant transliteration ("Si-đôn", "My-ra", "Ly-si", "Cơ-nít", "Cơ-rết", "Sa-môn", "La-sê", … — As the style guide settles names; "Cơ-rết" as in Titus 1:5. A reviewer should check the rarer forms.
- [ ] [ACT 27:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/27/#v23) L/B/R · register · the angel says "con" to Paul — God's messenger speaks for God to his servant, as the Lord speaks to Paul in 18:9 and 23:11.
- [ ] [ACT 28:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/28/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đi-ốt-cua" (L); "Hai Anh Em Song Sinh" (B, R) for the Dioscuri — Castor and Pollux, gods thought to protect sailors (meaning decision); Literal keeps the Greek name, the other levels say what it means, as the English does.
- [ ] [ACT 28:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/act/28/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "Sy-ra-cu-sơ", "Rê-ghi-um"; also "Bu-tê-ô-li", "Chợ Áp-bi-u", "Ba Quán" in 28:13-15 — Transliterations, with the names that have a plain meaning translated as the English does. A reviewer should check them.

## Rô-ma (6)

- [ ] [ROM 15:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rom/15/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "Giê-ru-sa-lem", "I-ly-ri" for Illyricum — The Protestant transliterations. "I-ly-ri" is the traditional Vietnamese form of the Roman province on the Adriatic; a reviewer should check it.
- [ ] [ROM 15:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rom/15/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "Tây Ban Nha" for Spain (also 15:28) — "Y-pha-nho" is the older transliteration in the traditional version and is no longer understood by most readers; "Tây Ban Nha" is the name every Vietnamese reader knows for the same country.
- [ ] [ROM 16:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rom/16/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · Names in this chapter in Protestant transliterations: Phê-bê, Sen-cơ-rê, Bê-rít-ca, A-qui-la, Ê-bai-nết, A-si, Ma-ri, An-trô-ni-cơ, Giu-ni-a, Am-li-a-tu, … — Names follow the Protestant tradition the style guide sets for the whole translation; "Ti-mô-thê" and "Ru-phu" are the forms already used in the Vietnamese Colossians and Mark. Most of these names occur only here, and some forms were drafted from memory of the traditional version or transliterated …
- [ ] [ROM 16:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rom/16/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "Bê-rít-ca" for Prisca — The Greek has the short form Prisca, which the English keeps. "Bê-rít-sin" is the familiar Protestant form of the longer Priscilla; "Bê-rít-ca" transliterates the name Paul wrote. A reviewer may prefer the familiar form.
- [ ] [ROM 16:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rom/16/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "trái đầu mùa" (L), as in 8:23; "A-si" (L, B), "tỉnh A-si" (R) — Firstfruits is the first convert, the first of a harvest still to come (m1). Asia is the Roman province (m2): "A-si" is the traditional form; "châu Á" would be heard as the continent, so Readable adds "tỉnh" (province).
- [ ] [ROM 16:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rom/16/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "Am-li-a-tu" — The traditional version has "Am-li-a", from the shorter form Amplias in the manuscripts it followed; the text here has Ampliatus, so the name is given in full. A reviewer should check it.

## 1 Cô-rinh-tô (29)

- [ ] [1CO 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "tôi" for Paul, "chúng tôi" for Paul with Sosthenes or his fellow workers, "chúng ta" where the readers are included, and "anh chị em" for "you" (plural) … — As in the Vietnamese Romans, Galatians and Colossians: Vietnamese has no neutral "you", and "anh chị em" is the warm, inclusive word Vietnamese churches use. It is plain text here, not a gender span, since the Greek pronoun has no word for brothers; the span is kept for adelphoi. Paul calls them …
- [ ] [1CO 2:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/2/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "các nhà cầm quyền của đời này" for the rulers of this age (also 2:8) — "Nhà cầm quyền" names human authorities, which they at least are (m4); "bậc cai trị" is kept in Colossians for the unseen powers, and would settle the question the other way.
- [ ] [1CO 3:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/3/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "bạn đồng công của Đức Chúa Trời" (L); "những người cùng làm việc phục vụ Đức Chúa Trời" (B); "những người cùng làm việc với nhau cho Đức Chúa Trời" (R) — Literal keeps the Greek, as in 1 Thessalonians 3:2. Balanced and Readable take the reading of m3, that Paul and Apollos work together in God's service; the question is open, as m3 says.
- [ ] [1CO 4:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/4/#v14) L/B/R · register · "những đứa con yêu dấu của tôi" for "my beloved children", with "anh chị em" kept for "you" (4:14-15) — Paul calls them his children, and the Vietnamese says so plainly; but he goes on writing to them as brothers and sisters (4:6; 7:24), so "anh chị em" stays the pronoun, and the relation is not changed for one passage.
- [ ] [1CO 5:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/5/#v3) L/B · word_choice · "để xác thịt bị hủy diệt" (L, B); "để những ham muốn tội lỗi đang cai trị người ấy bị tiêu diệt" (R) — Literal and Balanced keep "xác thịt", the word for sarx, which here means the man's sinful self (m6), not his body; Readable says so, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 6:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/6/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "những người [nam] mềm yếu, những người nam ăn nằm với người nam" (L); "những người nam đóng vai bị động trong quan hệ tình dục với người nam, những người nam … — As the book-wide choice requires, the two Greek words stay two items at every level and describe acts, not an orientation; the meaning decisions give the evidence and the debated alternatives (m3, m4). "Ăn nằm với người nam" is the Literal of the Vietnamese 1 Timothy 1:10 for the second word. …
- [ ] [1CO 7:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/7/#v1) L/B/R · punctuation · the Corinthians' saying in quotation marks (L, B), with "Có người trong anh chị em nói" (R) — As in 6:12-13: Paul quotes their letter and then answers it in verses 2-5 (m1); the extent of the quotation is debated, as m1 says.
- [ ] [1CO 7:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/7/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "những người không lập gia đình" (L), "những người chưa lập gia đình" (B, R) for agamoi; "những người góa bụa" (L), "những người góa chồng" (B, R) for the … — Agamos can include those who were married once (m1), so Literal says "not married" with no word for "not yet"; the widows here are women, which Balanced and Readable make plain.
- [ ] [1CO 7:36](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/7/#v36) L/B/R · word_choice · "trinh nữ của mình" (L); "người con gái mình đã hứa hôn" (B); "một cô gái" he is engaged to (R) — The reading of m1, a man and the young woman he is engaged to; m1 records the other readings. "Anh" for the man in Balanced and Readable, since he is a young man about to marry.
- [ ] [1CO 9:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/9/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "các em của Chúa" for "the brothers of the Lord" — Vietnamese has to say whether a brother is older or younger; the Gospels name Jesus as Mary's firstborn, so the Vietnamese Galatians 1:19 says "em trai" at Readable, and here every level says "em".
- [ ] [1CO 11:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/11/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "đầu" for kephale at every level (11:3-10) — The book-wide choice keeps "head" at every level, read as authority, with "source" footnoted (m1); the Vietnamese "đầu" can carry both senses as the Greek does, so no level settles the question the meaning decision leaves open.
- [ ] [1CO 11:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/11/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "người nam", "người nữ" (L); "người đàn ông", "người phụ nữ" (B, R), with no gender span, for aner and gyne in 11:3-16 — The passage speaks of men and women in general (m2); the Vietnamese 1 Timothy 2:9-15 uses "người nữ" in the same way.
- [ ] [1CO 11:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/11/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "thẩm quyền trên đầu" for exousian epi tes kephales — The Greek names authority, not a sign of authority; the precise sense is uncertain (m2), so no level adds words that would settle it.
- [ ] [1CO 11:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/11/#v29) L/B · word_choice · "phân biệt" for diakrino (11:29, 11:31) and "phán xét", "xét đoán" for krino (11:29-32) — Paul plays on the verbs of judging (m4); Vietnamese cannot keep one root for them all, but each verb is kept the same through the verses, so the reader can follow them. What "the body" means is open (m1), and Literal and Balanced do not settle it.
- [ ] [1CO 12:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/12/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "tiếng lạ" for glossai at every level, with "ngôn ngữ" (languages) footnoted — As the book-wide choice says, the word is "tongues", with "languages" footnoted; Vietnamese "tiếng lạ" says that the speech is in a tongue the speaker has not learned (m6), without deciding whether it is a human language.
- [ ] [1CO 13:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/13/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "để bị thiêu đốt" (L), "cho lửa thiêu" (B), "để người ta thiêu đốt" (R), following the SBLGNT, with "để tôi được khoe mình" (so that I may boast) footnoted — The SBLGNT reads "so that I will be burned"; manuscripts are divided (m1), and the footnote gives the other reading in the word of 1:29-31.
- [ ] [1CO 13:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/13/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "không ghi nhớ điều ác" (L); "không ghi sổ những điều sai trái" (B); "không cứ nghĩ mãi về những điều sai trái người khác đã làm cho mình" (R) for ou logizetai … — The verb is the bookkeeper's "reckon" (m6); Balanced keeps the picture of an account book, as the English "tally" does.
- [ ] [1CO 14:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/14/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "trong Thánh Linh", capitalised; "những điều mầu nhiệm" (L, B), "một điều bí ẩn" (R) — The reading of m3, that the Holy Spirit gives the speech, as the English capitalises it; the other reading, the speaker's own spirit, is given in m3. The mysteries here are only things the hearers do not understand (m4).
- [ ] [1CO 14:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/14/#v9) L/B · word_choice · "lưỡi" for glossa here, the organ of speech — In this verse the word most likely means the tongue that speaks (m1), so Vietnamese gives the body part, not the gift; the English "through the tongue" can keep both senses, but Vietnamese has two words.
- [ ] [1CO 14:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/14/#v12) L · word_choice · "các thần linh", lowercase, for pneumaton (L) — Literal keeps the Greek plural, which most likely means the ways the one Spirit works (m1); a capital "Thánh Linh" in the plural would suggest many Holy Spirits, so Literal uses the plain word, and Balanced and Readable say what is meant, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 14:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/14/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "tâm linh tôi", lowercase, and "tâm trí tôi" for pneuma and nous (14:14-16) — As the English writes "spirit" lowercase: the speaker's own spirit, moved by God's Spirit (m1). "Tâm linh" is the word of 2:11 and 5:3-5, "tâm trí" the word of 2:16 and the Vietnamese Romans 8:6.
- [ ] [1CO 14:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/14/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "người ngoài" (L, B); "Một người ... không hiểu" (R) for idiotes (also 14:23-24); "bạn" for the singular "you" (14:16-17); "A-men" — The book-wide choice: "outsider" (L, B), "someone who does not understand" (R). Paul turns to one speaker in the singular, as in 4:7, so the pronoun is "bạn". "A-men" is the form of the Vietnamese Galatians 1:5.
- [ ] [1CO 14:34](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/14/#v34) L/B/R · word_choice · "các người nữ" (L), "phụ nữ" (B, R); "im lặng" at every level, as for the speakers of 14:28 and 14:30; "thuận phục" (L, B), "vâng phục" (R) — "Im lặng" is the word of verses 28 and 30, so the reader can see that Paul uses the same verb for the speaker in tongues, the prophet and the women; m3 and m5 give the debated sense. "Thuận phục" is the word of the Vietnamese 1 Timothy 2:11.
- [ ] [1CO 15:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/15/#v7) R · supplied_words · "Gia-cơ, em của Chúa" (R) — As in the Vietnamese Galatians 1:19 and 9:5 here: Vietnamese must say younger or older, and Readable says "em".
- [ ] [1CO 15:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/15/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "chịu báp-têm vì người chết" — "Vì" can mean on behalf of or for the sake of (m2), and leaves open what the practice was (m1).
- [ ] [1CO 15:45](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/15/#v45) L/B/R · word_choice · "một sinh linh" for psyche zosa; "một thần linh ban sự sống", lowercase, for pneuma zoopoioun — "Sinh linh" is a living creature with natural life (m2), and keeps the link with "tự nhiên" (psychikos); "linh hồn sống" would suggest an immortal soul. As the English keeps "spirit" lowercase, so does the Vietnamese, with the word the style guide uses for "God is spirit" (John 4:24); m4 says what …
- [ ] [1CO 15:49](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/15/#v49) L/B · word_choice · "sẽ mang" (a statement, as the SBLGNT reads); "hình ảnh" for eikon — The SBLGNT reads the future, "we will wear" (m1); manuscripts that read "let us wear" are noted in m1. "Hình ảnh" is the word of the Vietnamese Colossians 1:15 for eikon.
- [ ] [1CO 16:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/16/#v12) L · word_choice · "không phải là ý muốn" (L), with no possessor — The Greek says only "it was not [the] will" (m3); Literal leaves it unnamed, while Balanced and Readable follow the reading that Apollos himself did not want to come, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 16:22](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1co/16/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ma-ra-na-tha" (L); "Lạy Chúa chúng con, xin hãy đến" (B); "Lạy Chúa Giê-xu của chúng con, xin hãy đến" (R) — Literal keeps the Aramaic in the transliteration readers know; Balanced and Readable give it as a prayer (m2), with "Lạy Chúa" and "chúng con", the style guide's forms for believers praying to the Lord.

## 2 Cô-rinh-tô (36)

- [ ] [2CO 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "tôi" for Paul, "chúng tôi" for Paul writing with or for his companions, "chúng ta" where the readers are included, and "anh chị em" for "you" (plural) through … — As in the Vietnamese 1 Corinthians. Paul's "we" in this letter is mostly Paul himself, perhaps with his companions (1:8, m3); the exclusive "chúng tôi" keeps that open without counting the readers in.
- [ ] [2CO 2:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/2/#v1) R · supplied_words · "như lần trước" (R) — As the English Readable does, Readable says that an earlier visit had already been painful (m2).
- [ ] [2CO 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/2/#v3) B/R · supplied_words · "trước đây tôi đã viết thư" (B); "lá thư ấy" (R) — As the book-wide choice reads it, "I wrote" refers to an earlier, tearful letter sent in place of a visit, not 1 Corinthians (m1); the words supplied do not name which letter.
- [ ] [2CO 3:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/3/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "chức vụ" (L, B), "công việc" (R) for diakonia in 3:7-9 and 4:1; "đang bị bãi bỏ" (L), "đang bị loại bỏ" (B), "sẽ không tồn tại mãi" (R) for katargoumene (3:7, … — The levels follow the book-wide English: "set aside", "done away with", "not going to last", with "fading" footnoted (m3); the verbs are those of 1 Corinthians 13:8-11. "Chức vụ" is the word of 1 Corinthians 12:5.
- [ ] [2CO 4:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/4/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "Tôi đã tin, nên tôi đã nói" for the quotation of Psalm 116:10, with "tôi" for the psalmist; "Thánh Linh của đức tin", capitalised — The psalmist speaks of himself as "tôi", as the style guide says. The Spirit of faith is read as the Holy Spirit (m2), as the English capitalises it.
- [ ] [2CO 5:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/5/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "một cuộc tạo dựng mới" at every level for kaine ktisis, with "người ấy thuộc về" (B, R) — "Một cuộc tạo dựng mới" is the phrase of the Vietnamese Galatians 6:15 for the same Greek words. The Greek has no verb (m1); Literal brackets "[thì có]", leaving open whether the person is a new creation or belongs to the one God has begun, and Balanced and Readable take the reading of m1, as the …
- [ ] [2CO 7:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/7/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "lá thư" for the earlier, tearful letter (7:8, 7:12) — As in 2:3: the letter is read as the tearful letter of 2:3-4, not 1 Corinthians (m1), and the Vietnamese does not name it.
- [ ] [2CO 9:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/9/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "người ấy" (L, B), "họ" (R) for the generous person of Psalm 112:9, with no deity_pronoun span — The subject is the generous person the psalm praises (m2), not God, so the pronoun takes no span and no capital.
- [ ] [2CO 10:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/10/#v1) L/B/R · register · "khoe mình" at every level for kauchaomai and kauchesis in chapters 10-12 — In chapters 10-13 Paul is forced into the rivals' kind of boasting and calls it folly (m1); "khoe mình" keeps the edge of the word, where "tự hào" would make it sound like proper pride. The boasting "in the Lord" of 10:17 uses the same word, so the contrast is visible.
- [ ] [2CO 10:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/10/#v3) L · word_choice · "có quyền năng đối với Đức Chúa Trời" (L), with "từ Đức Chúa Trời" (B) and "của Đức Chúa Trời" (R) — The dative is unclear: made powerful by God, or mighty in his service (m4, uncertain). Literal keeps the bare dative; Balanced and Readable take the more likely sense.
- [ ] [2CO 10:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/10/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · a command: "Hãy nhìn" — The verb can be a statement or a command; the meaning decision takes it as a command (m1, uncertain), and so does the Vietnamese.
- [ ] [2CO 10:10](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/10/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "người ta nói" (L); "có người nói" (B); "Có người ở đó nói" (R) for phesin — A particular critic, or "someone says" (m2, uncertain); Vietnamese needs no pronoun, and "người ta" and "có người" leave the speaker unnamed, as Paul does.
- [ ] [2CO 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/10/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "phạm vi" (L); "lĩnh vực" (B); "khu vực" (R) for kanon — An assigned field of work, measured out by God (m1, uncertain); the three words follow the English sphere, field and area.
- [ ] [2CO 10:17](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/10/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "Chúa" for kyrios — In Jeremiah, God's own name; here most likely Christ (m2, uncertain). As elsewhere in these letters, kyrios in a quotation is "Chúa".
- [ ] [2CO 11:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "xin hãy chịu đựng tôi" (L, B); "Xin hãy kiên nhẫn với tôi" (R), an appeal — The verb can be a statement or a command; the meaning decision takes it as an appeal (m2, uncertain).
- [ ] [2CO 11:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "các siêu sứ đồ" (L); "các siêu sứ đồ ấy" (B); "những người được gọi là siêu sứ đồ ấy" (R) for hoi hyperlian apostoloi (also 12:11) — Paul's mocking name for the rivals (m1, uncertain). "Siêu" is the Vietnamese prefix for "super", and it carries the same mockery; "ấy" (B) and "được gọi là" (R) point at the rivals, as the English "so-called" does.
- [ ] [2CO 11:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "họ được thấy [là] cũng như chúng tôi" (L); "họ ngang hàng với chúng tôi" (B); "họ cũng giống như chúng tôi" (R) — The rivals want to be seen as Paul's equals (m1, uncertain).
- [ ] [2CO 11:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "đầy tớ" (L), "tay sai" (B, R) for Satan's diakonoi; "người phục vụ của sự công chính" (L, B), "những đầy tớ đem lại sự công chính" (R) — The same Greek word for both; Literal keeps a servant word for each, and Balanced and Readable call Satan's servants "tay sai", the word for an evil master's agents. "Người phục vụ" stays for God's servants, as in 3:6 and 6:4.
- [ ] [2CO 11:20](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "vả vào mặt" (L); "tát vào mặt" (B, R) — A deep insult (m2, uncertain); a slap in the face is an insult in Vietnamese too.
- [ ] [2CO 11:26](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "nhịn ăn" (L), "không có gì ăn" (B, R); "trần truồng" (L), "không đủ áo mặc" (B, R) — Going without food, perhaps sometimes by choice (m4, uncertain); too little clothing (m5).
- [ ] [2CO 11:28](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ngoài những điều khác [nữa]" (L); "nỗi lo lắng cho" (B) for merimna — Besides everything not mentioned (m1, uncertain); an anxious care (m3).
- [ ] [2CO 11:29](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "bị làm cho vấp ngã" (L, B); "bị làm cho sa vào tội lỗi" (R); "nóng cháy" (L, B), "nóng như lửa đốt" (R) — Led into sin (m1), as "làm cho vấp ngã" in 1 Corinthians 8:13; burning with distress and indignation (m2, uncertain).
- [ ] [2CO 11:31](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "biết rằng tôi không nói dối" — An oath: God is witness (m1, uncertain); as in Galatians 1:20 and Romans 9:1.
- [ ] [2CO 11:32](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/11/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "quan tổng đốc của vua A-rê-ta" (L); "viên quan cai trị do vua A-rê-ta bổ nhiệm" (B); "viên tổng đốc phục vụ vua A-rê-ta" (R) for ho ethnarches — The Nabatean king and his official (m1, uncertain). A-rê-ta is given in the hyphenated form of the Vietnamese Bible names.
- [ ] [2CO 12:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "các khải tượng và sự mặc khải của Chúa" (L); "... Chúa ban cho" (B); "những điều Chúa đã tỏ cho tôi" (R) — Given by the Lord (m3, uncertain). "Sự mặc khải" is the word of the Vietnamese Galatians 1:12 and Ephesians 1:17 for apokalypsis.
- [ ] [2CO 12:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v6) L/B/R · punctuation · the phrase about the revelations ends verse 6's sentence — As in the SBLGNT and the English (m3, uncertain).
- [ ] [2CO 12:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "một cái gai trong xác thịt" (L, B); "một cái gai nhức nhối trong thân thể" (R) — Some painful, lasting trouble whose nature is not known (m1, uncertain); Literal and Balanced keep "xác thịt" so the reader can trace the Greek, Readable says "thân thể" because the trouble is bodily or close to it.
- [ ] [2CO 12:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ân điển của {{deity_pronoun:ta|Ta}} đủ cho con", with "con" for Paul — The Lord's favor and help are all Paul needs (m2). The Lord speaks to one servant, so "con", as when God speaks to one person in the Old Testament quotations of these letters.
- [ ] [2CO 12:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "dựng lều trên tôi" (L); "giăng lều che phủ tôi" (B); "ở trên tôi như một cái lều che chở" (R) for episkenoo — May come to dwell on me and shelter me (m2); the picture of the tent is kept at every level, since it may recall God dwelling with his people (m3, uncertain).
- [ ] [2CO 12:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "các siêu sứ đồ" (L); "các siêu sứ đồ ấy" (B); "Những người được gọi là siêu sứ đồ ấy" (R) — As in 11:5: Paul's mocking name for his rivals (m1, uncertain).
- [ ] [2CO 12:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "cùng một tinh thần" (L, B), with "bước đi" (L) and "cư xử" (B) — The same attitude and way of acting (m1, uncertain), with a small letter in the English; "tinh thần" is the human attitude.
- [ ] [2CO 12:21](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/12/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "lại" with the humbling (L, B, R) — "Again" goes with being humbled: a second humbling visit (m2, uncertain). Vietnamese "lại" sits next to the verb it belongs to.
- [ ] [2CO 13:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/13/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "theo lời của hai hoặc ba nhân chứng" (L); "lời cáo buộc" (B, R) for rhema here — At least two witnesses (m3), in a proper hearing of each charge (m4, uncertain). Literal says "theo lời", the plain sense of "on the mouth of".
- [ ] [2CO 13:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/13/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "sẽ nhận biết" (L); "sẽ thấy" (B, R) — Either through their own testing or when Paul comes (m2, uncertain); the verb leaves both open.
- [ ] [2CO 13:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/13/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "hãy được an ủi" (L); "hãy được khích lệ" (B); "Hãy vững lòng" (R) for parakaleisthe — Be comforted and encouraged (m3, uncertain); "an ủi" is the book's Literal word for the paraklesis family from 1:3-7.
- [ ] [2CO 13:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2co/13/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "sự thông công của Thánh Linh" for he koinonia tou hagiou pneumatos — The fellowship the Holy Spirit gives, a shared part in the Spirit (m4, uncertain); "sự thông công của Thánh Linh" is the phrase of the Vietnamese Philippians 2:1.

## Ga-la-ti (8)

- [ ] [GAL 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "tôi" for Paul, "chúng tôi" for Paul and those with him, "chúng ta" where the readers are included, and "anh chị em" for "you" (plural) through the whole letter — As in the Vietnamese Colossians and Romans, Paul is "tôi" and the churches are "anh chị em", the warm, inclusive form Vietnamese churches use. Paul is the founder of these churches and in 4:19 calls them his children, but through the rest of the letter he argues with them as brothers and sisters …
- [ ] [GAL 1:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/1/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "anh em của Chúa" (L, B); "em trai của Chúa Giê-xu" (R) — Vietnamese must say whether a brother is older or younger, which the Greek does not. Literal and Balanced keep the open "anh em", as the Vietnamese Mark does for Jesus' brothers (6:3). Jesus was Mary's firstborn (Luke 2:7), so Readable says "em trai", younger brother, as plain Vietnamese needs; a …
- [ ] [GAL 3:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/3/#v13) L/B/R · register · "chúng ta" for "us" and "we" — The meaning decision leaves open whether "us" is the Jewish believers first (m4); Vietnamese must choose between inclusive and exclusive "we", and the inclusive "chúng ta" takes in the readers, who in verse 14 receive the Spirit with Paul.
- [ ] [GAL 3:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/3/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "giao ước" (L); "di chúc" (B, R) — The Greek word is both a will and God's covenant (m2). Literal keeps "giao ước", the word that verse 17 needs, so the reader sees one word in both verses; Balanced and Readable say "di chúc", the everyday sense Paul's illustration uses, as the English does.
- [ ] [GAL 3:23](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/3/#v23) L/B/R · register · "chúng ta" for "we" — The meaning decision says that "we" is Paul and his fellow Jews (m3), which would point to the exclusive "chúng tôi". Paul's argument, though, runs straight on to "you are all sons" in verse 26, and the guardian picture speaks for all God's people before Christ, so the inclusive "chúng ta" is kept, …
- [ ] [GAL 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/4/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "các nguyên tố của thế gian" (L); "các nguyên lực của thế gian" (B); "những quyền lực và luật lệ đang chi phối thế gian này" (R); the same in 4:9 — The words of the Vietnamese Colossians (2:8) for the same Greek phrase. Balanced needs a word that keeps both senses of m2, basic forces and basic rules, and "nguyên lực" (elemental forces) does so without settling the question; Readable names both, as the English does.
- [ ] [GAL 5:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/5/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "niềm hy vọng về sự công chính" (L); "sự công chính mà chúng ta hy vọng" (B); "được nghe Đức Chúa Trời tuyên bố chúng ta là công chính" (R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as the righteousness hoped for, God's verdict at the last day (m2). "Niềm hy vọng" is the word kept for hope, as in the Vietnamese Colossians and Romans; Readable says what the hope is, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [GAL 6:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/gal/6/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "một cuộc tạo dựng mới" — The meaning decision leaves open whether Paul means the new world God has begun or each person made new in it (m2). "Cuộc tạo dựng mới" names God's new act of creating and can include both, as the Vietnamese Colossians says "cuộc tạo dựng mới" at Readable (1:18); "tạo vật mới" would choose the …

## Ê-phê-sô (4)

- [ ] [EPH 1:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/eph/1/#v3) L/B/R · register · "chúng ta" in verses 3-10; "chúng tôi" in verses 11-12; "anh chị em" from verse 13 — Vietnamese must say whether "we" includes the readers. The blessing of verses 3-10 belongs to all believers, so "chúng ta"; the meaning decision reads "we who first hoped in the Christ" as Paul and his fellow Jewish believers, set against "you also" in verse 13 (m21), so verses 11-12 say "chúng …
- [ ] [EPH 1:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/eph/1/#v15) L/B · word_choice · "Thánh Linh của sự khôn ngoan và mặc khải" (L); "Thánh Linh, Đấng ban sự khôn ngoan và mặc khải" (B) — The meaning decision reads the spirit as the Holy Spirit, who gives wisdom and reveals God (m3), so it is capitalised; the lowercase "tinh thần" would choose the other reading.
- [ ] [EPH 4:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/eph/4/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "những miền thấp của đất" — The meaning decision leaves open whether this is the earth below heaven or the realm of the dead (m1). "Những miền thấp của đất" keeps the Greek picture without choosing; "âm phủ" would choose the second reading and "trần gian" the first.
- [ ] [EPH 6:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/eph/6/#v24) L · word_choice · "trong sự không hề hư nát" (L) — The word is what cannot decay or die (m3), read with the love (m2). Literal keeps the noun; Balanced and Readable say it of the love, as the English does.

## Phi-líp (8)

- [ ] [PHP 1:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/1/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "đội cận vệ" (L), "đội cận vệ của hoàng đế" (B, R) — The word is either the imperial guard or the governor's headquarters (m3). The meaning decision reads the soldiers who guarded Paul, as the English does, so Vietnamese names the guard.
- [ ] [PHP 1:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/1/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "sự cứu rỗi" at every level — The meaning decision reads the word as Paul's final vindication before God, whether he lives or dies (m4), which the words of Job 13:16 also suggest (m3). "Sự cứu rỗi" keeps that sense; "được thả ra" would narrow it to release from prison.
- [ ] [PHP 1:27](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/1/#v27) L/B · word_choice · "trong một tâm thần" (L, B), lowercase; "một lòng một ý" (R) — The meaning decision reads "one spirit" as a shared purpose and resolve (m3), so it is lowercase "tâm thần", as in Ephesians 4:23; "Thánh Linh" would choose the other reading.
- [ ] [PHP 2:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/2/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "hình thể Đức Chúa Trời"; "điều để tận dụng cho mình" (L), "điều để dùng cho lợi ích riêng" (B, R) — "Hình thể" is God's own way of being, seen in his glory (m4), and the same word is used for the slave's form in verse 7. The meaning decision reads the second phrase as something to exploit for his own advantage (m6), which Vietnamese says plainly; "chiếm đoạt" would suggest that he did not already …
- [ ] [PHP 3:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/3/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "phân rác" at every level — The word is filth fit only to be thrown out, deliberately coarse (m4). "Rác rưởi" is only rubbish; "phân rác" keeps the coarse edge, as the English keeps "dung".
- [ ] [PHP 3:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/3/#v13) L/B · word_choice · "sự kêu gọi lên cao" (L, B); "gọi tôi lên trời" (R) — The meaning decision reads the prize as what God's upward call holds out (m6). "Lên cao" keeps the direction of the Greek word; Readable says where, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PHP 3:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/3/#v18) L/B · word_choice · "cái bụng" (L, B); "những ham muốn của chính mình" (R) — The belly stands for their appetites and desires (m4). Vietnamese also speaks of serving one's belly, so Literal and Balanced keep the picture; Readable says what it means.
- [ ] [PHP 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/php/4/#v3) L/B/R · register · "anh" for the unnamed partner; "hai bà ấy" for the two women — Paul turns to one man he does not name (m1), a trusted co-worker, so "anh", as for Archippus in the Vietnamese Colossians (4:17). The two women led in the church (m3 of 4:2), so they are spoken of with respect, "bà", as Nympha is in Colossians 4:15; a reviewer may prefer "chị".

## Cô-lô-se (6)

- [ ] [COL 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/col/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "tôi" for Paul, "chúng tôi" for Paul and those with him, "chúng ta" where the readers are included, and "anh chị em" for "you" (plural) through the whole letter — Vietnamese has no neutral word for "you": the pronoun names the relation. Paul writes as an apostle to a church he has not visited (2:1) but greets as brothers and sisters, so he is "tôi" and they are "anh chị em", the warm, inclusive form Vietnamese churches use and the one the Vietnamese John …
- [ ] [COL 1:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/col/1/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "ngôi vua, quyền chủ tể, bậc cai trị, thế lực" for thrones, lordships, rulers, authorities; "bậc cai trị" and "thế lực" for the rulers and authorities through … — The four words name ranks of powers, mostly unseen (m5). "Thế lực" (a power, a force with influence) suits spiritual authorities better than "thẩm quyền", which is a legal competence. The same pair is kept wherever Paul names these powers, so the reader can follow them through the letter.
- [ ] [COL 2:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/col/2/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "các nguyên tố của thế gian" (L); "các nguyên lực của thế gian" (B); "những quyền lực và luật lệ đang chi phối thế gian này" (R); the same in 2:20 — Literal keeps "elements" with the plain word "nguyên tố". Balanced needs a word that keeps both senses of m5, basic forces and basic rules: "nguyên lực" (elemental forces) does so without settling the question. Readable names both, as the English does. "Thần linh" would decide for spirits, which m5 …
- [ ] [COL 3:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/col/3/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "người man di, người Sy-the" (L, B); "người nước ngoài hay người ở chốn hoang dã" (R) — "Man di" was the old word for peoples outside one's own civilisation, which is what a barbarian was to Greeks (m5); "dã man" and "mọi rợ" now mean cruel or savage. "Sy-the" is the traditional Protestant transliteration, to be checked by a reviewer. Readable says what the two names meant.
- [ ] [COL 4:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/col/4/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "Ti-chi-cơ", "Ô-nê-sim", and the other names of the letter in the Protestant transliterations (Cô-lô-se, Lao-đi-xê, Hi-ê-ra-pô-li, Ê-pháp-ra, A-ri-tạc, Mác, … — Names follow the tradition the style guide sets for the whole translation. Spellings not in the style guide's list should be checked by a reviewer, Hi-ê-ra-pô-li in particular. "Người anh em" leaves open whether a brother is older or younger, as in the Vietnamese John (1:40).
- [ ] [COL 4:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/col/4/#v15) L/B/R · register · "bà" for Nympha ("nhà bà") — Vietnamese must choose a word for her by age and standing, which the Greek does not give. A woman who hosts a church in her house is spoken of with respect, so "bà"; a reviewer may prefer "chị". The text follows the SBLGNT reading that Nympha is a woman (m1).

## 1 Tê-sa-lô-ni-ca (8)

- [ ] [1TH 2:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/2/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "dịu dàng" at every level — The SBLGNT reads "gentle"; another reading, "infants", is noted in the meaning decision (m7). Vietnamese follows the SBLGNT.
- [ ] [1TH 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/2/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "người Do Thái" (L); "những người Do Thái ở Giu-đê" (B); "những người Do Thái ở Giu-đê chống đối các tín hữu" (R) — The meaning decision reads "the Jews" here as those in Judea who persecuted the churches there, not the Jewish people as a whole (m3). As in the Vietnamese John, where the authorities are meant, Balanced and Readable say which Jews, and Literal keeps the bare word.
- [ ] [1TH 2:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/2/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "đến cùng" (L); "cuối cùng" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads this as God's judgment, sure and final, with the other senses footnoted (m1). "Cuối cùng" (at last) leaves room for both "finally" and "to the end".
- [ ] [1TH 3:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/3/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "các đấng thánh của Ngài" at every level — The meaning decision leaves open whether the holy ones are angels, God's people, or both, as in Zechariah 14:5 (m4). "Thánh đồ" would choose believers and "thiên sứ" angels; "các đấng thánh" (holy beings) leaves it open.
- [ ] [1TH 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/4/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "cái bình của chính mình" (L); "thân thể mình" (B, R) — The Greek word is "vessel"; the meaning decision reads it as one's own body, with "wife" as the other reading footnoted (m4). Literal keeps the picture of the vessel so the reader sees the word; Balanced and Readable follow the meaning decision.
- [ ] [1TH 4:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/4/#v16) L/B · word_choice · "nghênh đón Chúa" (L, B); "gặp Chúa" (R) — The word was used of a city going out to welcome an arriving ruler (m6). "Nghênh đón" is the Vietnamese word for going out to receive an honored guest, and keeps that picture.
- [ ] [1TH 5:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/5/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "Bình an và an toàn" — The slogan is a false confidence (m1). "Hòa bình và an ninh" would sound like a political slogan of our own day; "bình an" is the key word for peace, which lets the reader set this false peace beside God's peace (5:23).
- [ ] [1TH 5:14](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1th/5/#v14) L/B · word_choice · "những người vô trật tự" (L); "những người sống không theo khuôn phép" (B) — The meaning decision reads the word as those who are out of line, with idleness as a possible part of it footnoted (m1). "Vô trật tự" keeps the picture of breaking ranks; "biếng nhác" would choose the narrower sense.

## 2 Tê-sa-lô-ni-ca (6)

- [ ] [2TH 1:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2th/1/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đức Chúa Trời chúng ta và của Chúa Giê-xu" with "của" repeated — The meaning decision reads two persons, as in verse 2, with the other reading noted (m5). Repeating "của" makes the two plain in Vietnamese.
- [ ] [2TH 2:6](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2th/2/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "điều đang ngăn trở" (L), "điều gì đang kìm giữ" (B), "điều gì đang ngăn" (R) — The meaning decision says this power was known to the Thessalonians and is not known to us (m1). Vietnamese keeps the neuter "điều" here and "người" in verse 7, as the Greek changes gender, without naming what it is.
- [ ] [2TH 2:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2th/2/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "để cho ... đến trên họ" for "God sends" — The meaning decision reads God's sending as his judgment giving them over to what they chose (m1). Vietnamese "để cho" says that God lets it come on them as judgment; a bare "sai" would sound as if he tempts the innocent. The verb is still God's act, as the sentence shows.
- [ ] [2TH 2:13](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2th/2/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "trái đầu mùa" (L, B); "những người đầu tiên" (R) — The SBLGNT reads "as firstfruits", with "from the beginning" as the other reading (m1). "Trái đầu mùa" is the church word for the first of the harvest given to God.
- [ ] [2TH 2:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2th/2/#v16) B · word_choice · "các Ngài" (B) for the Lord Jesus and God together — The Lord Jesus Christ and God the Father act as one (m1), and the English Balanced says "they". Vietnamese "họ" would be disrespectful, so Balanced says "các Ngài", the respectful plural, written with a capital and no setting span, as a noun of address is; Readable names both.
- [ ] [2TH 3:5](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2th/3/#v5) L/B · word_choice · "tình yêu thương của Đức Chúa Trời", "sự kiên trì của {{christos:Đấng Christ|Đấng Mê-si-a}}" (L, B) — The meaning decision reads these as God's love for them and the endurance Christ showed and gives, with other readings noted (m1). Literal and Balanced keep "của", which leaves the phrase as open as the Greek; Readable says what the meaning decision reads.

## 1 Ti-mô-thê (10)

- [ ] [1TI 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "ta" for Paul and "con" for Timothy through the letter; "chúng ta" where Paul includes Timothy or all believers; "anh chị em" for the plural "you" in 6:21 — Paul writes to Timothy as his own child in the faith (m4), and Vietnamese must name that relation in every pronoun. "Ta" and "con" are the words of an older man to a son or disciple in written Vietnamese; "tôi" with "con" would not match, "tôi" with "anh" would lose the father-son bond Paul names, …
- [ ] [1TI 1:8](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/1/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "những người nam ăn nằm với người nam" (L); "những người nam quan hệ tình dục với người nam" (B, R) — The word describes men who have sexual relations with males (m3), an act, not an orientation, as the Corinthians terms set for the English. Vietnamese says the act in plain words at every level and does not use "đồng tính", which names an orientation.
- [ ] [1TI 2:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/2/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "yên lặng" at every level (also 2:12) — The meaning decision reads the word as a calm, settled manner, not necessarily total silence (m2). "Yên lặng" is quiet and calm; "im lặng" would say only that no word is spoken.
- [ ] [1TI 2:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/2/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "cầm quyền trên" (L); "sử dụng quyền trên" (B); "có quyền trên" (R) — The meaning decision reads the rare verb as to exercise authority, with the sense of seizing authority noted as another reading (m1). Vietnamese uses the neutral words for having and using authority and does not choose the other sense.
- [ ] [1TI 2:15](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/2/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "được cứu qua việc sinh con" at every level — The meaning decision leaves the sense open, with the main readings noted (m1). Vietnamese keeps the plain words of the Greek and does not choose; "người nữ" stands for "she", which can be heard as Eve and the women after her (m2).
- [ ] [1TI 3:2](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/3/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "chồng của chỉ một vợ" (L); "chung thủy với một vợ" (B); "chỉ được có một vợ" (R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as faithful to one wife, with other readings footnoted (m2). Literal keeps the Greek words; Balanced and Readable follow the English, which reads it the same way.
- [ ] [1TI 3:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/3/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "các người nữ" at every level — The meaning decision leaves open whether these are women deacons or the wives of deacons (m1). Vietnamese "các người nữ" keeps the Greek word and does not choose.
- [ ] [1TI 3:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/3/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "được xưng công chính trong Thánh Linh" (L); "được Thánh Linh xác nhận là công chính" (B); "Thánh Linh đã cho thấy Ngài là công chính" (R) — The verb is the one kept as "xưng công chính"; here Christ is shown to be in the right, in his resurrection (m4), so Balanced and Readable say "shown", as the Vietnamese Romans does for God (3:4). The meaning decision reads "spirit" as the Spirit, with another reading noted.
- [ ] [1TI 5:9](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/5/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "vợ của chỉ một chồng" (L); "chung thủy với chồng mình" (B, R) — As in 3:2 for the overseers: Literal keeps the Greek words, and Balanced and Readable follow the meaning decision, faithful to her husband, with the other readings footnoted (m2).
- [ ] [1TI 5:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/1ti/5/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "lời hứa nguyện ban đầu" (L, B), "lời hứa ... với Ngài" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word as their promise to serve Christ as enrolled widows, with "first faith" as the other reading (m2). "Hứa nguyện" is a solemn promise made to God.

## 2 Ti-mô-thê (7)

- [ ] [2TI 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "ta" for Paul and "con" for Timothy, as in the Vietnamese 1 Timothy; "anh chị em" for the plural "you" in 4:22; Christ, Savior and the other key terms in the … — Paul writes to Timothy as his beloved child (m2), so the relation set in 1 Timothy 1:1-2 holds. "Ta" can sound lofty, so a reviewer may prefer "thầy" and "con" in both letters.
- [ ] [2TI 1:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/1/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "bà ngoại con là Lô-ít và mẹ con là Ơ-nít" — Lois was Timothy's grandmother through his mother Eunice (m4), so Vietnamese says "bà ngoại", the mother's mother; Vietnamese must choose the side. The names are in the Protestant transliteration; a reviewer should check them.
- [ ] [2TI 1:7](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/1/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "tâm thần" (L, B), lowercase; "Thánh Linh" in the second half (R) — The meaning decision reads the spirit as the Spirit God gives, who brings power, love and self-control, with the human disposition as another reading (m1). Literal and Balanced keep the open Greek wording with a lowercase "tâm thần", which can be heard either way; Readable names the Spirit, as the …
- [ ] [2TI 1:12](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/1/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "điều ta đã giao phó" (L), "những gì ta đã giao phó cho Ngài" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads "my deposit" as what Paul has entrusted to Christ, with what Christ entrusted to Paul as the other reading (m1). Literal keeps the open phrase; Balanced and Readable follow the meaning decision.
- [ ] [2TI 2:24](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/2/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "bắt giữ họ để làm theo ý muốn của nó" with "nó" for the devil — The meaning decision reads both pronouns as the devil, with another reading noted (m2). Vietnamese "nó", the pronoun for a hostile being, makes that reading plain.
- [ ] [2TI 3:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/3/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "được Đức Chúa Trời hà hơi vào" (L, B); "đến từ hơi thở của Đức Chúa Trời" (R) — The word is "breathed out by God" (m2). "Soi dẫn" (inspired) is the familiar church word but names a later idea; "hà hơi" keeps the picture of God's breath, as in Genesis 2:7. A reviewer may prefer the familiar word.
- [ ] [2TI 4:16](https://openwordbible.org/vi/2ti/4/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "miệng sư tử" at every level, explained at Readable — The meaning decision reads the lion's mouth as deadly danger (m2); Literal and Balanced keep the picture, as the English does, and Readable says what it means.

## Tích (4)

- [ ] [TIT 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/tit/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · "ta" for Paul and "con" for Titus, as in the Vietnamese 1 and 2 Timothy; "anh chị em" for the plural "you" in 3:15 — Paul calls Titus his true child in their common faith (m5), so the relation of the letters to Timothy holds. A reviewer may prefer "thầy" and "con" in all three letters.
- [ ] [TIT 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/vi/tit/2/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "thuận phục chồng mình" at every level — The verb of the Vietnamese Colossians (3:18) and Ephesians (5:22-24) for wives, placing oneself under another willingly; the meaning decision notes the debate about its application (m4).
- [ ] [TIT 2:11](https://openwordbible.org/vi/tit/2/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "Đức Chúa Trời vĩ đại và Cứu Chúa chúng ta là Đức Giê-xu" (L); "cũng là Cứu Chúa chúng ta" (B); "Ngài là Đức Chúa Trời vĩ đại và là Cứu Chúa" (R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as one person, Jesus Christ, with two persons as the other reading footnoted (m1). Literal keeps the Greek order; Balanced and Readable add "cũng là", "là", which make the one person plain in Vietnamese.
- [ ] [TIT 3:4](https://openwordbible.org/vi/tit/3/#v4) L/B · word_choice · "sự rửa của sự tái sinh" (L), "sự rửa của sự sinh lại" (B); "rửa sạch ... sinh lại" (R) — The meaning decision reads the washing as the cleansing that brings new birth, with baptism in view, and notes other readings (m3). Vietnamese keeps the word "rửa" and does not say "báp-têm", so the open reading stays; "tái sinh" is the Sino-Vietnamese word for new birth, "sinh lại" its plain form.

- [ ] [REV 4:1](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rev/4/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "bích ngọc", "hồng mã não", "ngọc lục bảo" for jasper, carnelian and emerald — The stones' exact colors are uncertain (m5); Vietnamese uses its usual names for jasper, a red stone like carnelian, and emerald, as the English does. A reviewer may check the gem names here and in 21:19-20.
- [ ] [REV 21:19](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rev/21/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · the twelve stones: "bích ngọc" (jasper), "lam ngọc" (sapphire), "thạch tủy" (chalcedony), "lục bảo ngọc" (emerald), "mã não sọc" (sardonyx), "hồng mã não" … — The meaning decision says the ancient names do not always match modern stones (m2), and the English gives the traditional names. Vietnamese has no settled set for all twelve, so it uses descriptive gem names, keeping jasper and carnelian as in 4:3. A reviewer who knows Vietnamese gem names should …
- [ ] [REV 22:18](https://openwordbible.org/vi/rev/22/#v18) L/B/R · register · "Ta" for the one who testifies — The meaning decision leaves open whether Jesus or John speaks (m2), but Vietnamese must choose a pronoun. The next verses name "the one who testifies to these things" as Jesus (22:20), so Vietnamese gives him "Ta"; "tôi" would choose John. A reviewer should weigh this choice.
